# Changelog

## Unreleased

- tests (ENG-SMOKE-TEST-BUILD-ABORT-UNDER-LOAD): the unit cases of `scripts/smoke-fresh-install.sh` (`src/smoke-fresh-install.test.ts`) drove the script's real `npm ci` and `npm run build` of a fixture clone, so any task's gate could fail on a loaded host: a gate saw `npm run build` exit 134 (abort) in that clone, in the Ctrl-C case, and a run of the whole suite under load once also missed the step's `sleep` in a 40 s `ps` poll. The script now has a test hook: `SMOKE_INSTALL_CMD` and `SMOKE_BUILD_CMD` replace the command of the install and the build step (a shell string run by `bash -c` in the clone under the same `env -i`; a stubbed build need not leave `build/index.js`; the PASS / FAIL line names the variable). The unit cases set both, so they pin the script's own contract (exits, the step's process group, cleanup, isolation) without a registry or a real build; the real end-to-end run stays `npm run smoke:fresh` and its CI job, which never set them. The Ctrl-C cases learn that the step runs from a file the step creates, not from a `ps` poll. New `src/smoke-fresh-install-no-real-npm.test.ts`: with an `npm` on PATH that exits 134 (the status of an abort; no real signal, so no OS crash report) the stubbed run never calls it and Ctrl-C still gives exit 130 and an empty temp dir, and without the hook the install step is npm's (`FAIL install: npm ci exited 134`).
- batch (ENG-DISPATCHER-FOLLOWUPS): twelve items of the dispatcher agent, each from its list of 14. (1) The `resume` reply of a dispatcher root listed every task driver to wake, while only the SessionStart pickup knew the dispatcher: both now go through one function (`dispatcherSplit` in `takeover.ts`), so `resume` names the dispatcher's `SendMessage` and only the sub-sessions its `tasks` key does not name. (2) The refusal of a second `start` of a running task always named `status({ session_id, key: "driver_call" })`, also for a task workflow without `driver:` whose session has no such key; it names the key only when the session has it and otherwise says the main conversation drives that task. (3) `dispatch-driver.md` had no rule for a task that ended `stopped` (the `collect` prompt routes it to `stop` for an agent that drives the tasks itself): the dispatcher hands the driver's `NEEDS-PARENT` line to main, keeps the slot and takes `stop` only on main's word. (5) A task session the dispatcher started and died before writing `tasks` was listed to main at a restart; a direct child of the root that carries a `task` and a `driver_call` is now the dispatcher's, whose repeated `dispatch` finds it (a task the main conversation drives itself stays listed to it). (6) `lessons_every` was read by the agent from the file and validated by nothing; `queue_next` (the pick, dry-run too) now refuses a value that is not a whole number of 1 or more (`lessonsEvery` in `queue.ts`). (8) The duplicate-task guard reads every session file at each `start` under a dispatcher: measured on synthetic 4 KB session files in a temp dir, `readAll` took about 1.4 ms for 50 and 15 ms for 500 files (the live state directory held 481 files), a cost of one `start`, so no cache was added. (9) The agent counted lands for `rotate=due` from its memory; it now counts them in the context key `lands_done`, updated in each `slot_free` `set`, and `rotate=due` is set when the count has reached or passed a multiple of 4×N since the previous LESSONS message (`lessons_lands`), so a message off the exact multiple still rotates. (11) already done by ENG-DISPATCHER-HANDBACK-BY-MESSAGE. (12) `status` answers a state with its full prompt, but the reply of the `transition` that re-enters a visited state is the one-line `Revisit #N`: a dispatcher agent acting on it at `dispatch` had no recipe and guessed the task workflow of an admitted line. Bundled `dispatch` now has `full_on_revisit: true` (a heir overriding the state keeps it). (13, 14) docs/queue.md and the bundled wf-yaml-authoring SKILL say that drivers the main conversation spawned before the switch keep reporting to it (switch on an empty window) and that a second root session on a scratch queue shows that queue in the plan pane. Item 4 was already done (ENG-RELEASE-DOCS-NITS). Still open, handed on as lesson records: (7) the dashboard map draws no chip for the dispatcher agent (a dashboard drawing change of its own), (10) a heir's detached exec `plan_queue` that outlives the call: no later reply repeats the DRIVER block (needs a live trial of a detached exec to reproduce). Pins: `dispatcher-followups-twin-refusal.test.ts`, the `resume` and unwritten-`tasks` cases of `dispatcher-driver.test.ts`, the `lessons_every` cases of `queue.test.ts`.
- engine-task and batch-task (ENG-WAIT-MARKER-ABSOLUTE-PATH): the wait recipe the worker and reproducer briefs teach wrote its done-marker, log and pid by RELATIVE name (`L.done`, `L.log`, `L.pid`). Claude Code resets an agent's shell cwd to the project root after a call that `cd`s outside the working directories, so a reproduce agent that started its job from the reset cwd left three untracked files in the root of the user's project. The briefs (`templates/batch-task.yaml`, `.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml`), docs/queue.md and the bundled task-delegation skill (`references/agent-liveness.md`) now name the three files and the `until` wait line by ABSOLUTE path under the task's logs directory (`{{context.wt}}.log/` in batch-task, `{{context.wt}}-logs/` in engine-task, `<logs dir>` in the docs) and say why (the shell's cwd does not persist across calls; chain `cd` with `&&`). `src/wait-marker-absolute-path.test.ts` runs each shipped recipe from a cwd that is not the logs directory and fails if a file lands there, and scans the prompts, docs and bundled skill for a relative `L.*`; `src/worker-waits-in-foreground.test.ts` pins the new wording.
- trust (ENG-TRUST-AGENT-BYPASS): exec consent's threat model is stated and two of its three bypasses are closed. Consent protects against a workflow that ARRIVES with a project the user has not trusted; what the user's own agent runs is Claude Code's permissions (an `exec` state is run by the engine's server, so Claude Code does not prompt for its command), and `docs/getting-started.md` § "What consent does and does not protect against" says so. New PreToolUse hook `hooks/trust-write-gate.sh` (`src/trust-write-gate.ts`, entry `trust-write-gate-hook.ts`; matcher `Bash|Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit`): an agent's write to `$STATE_DIR/trusted-workflows.json` (an agent forging an approval) or into the global workflows directory (a workflow there runs its exec states with no consent step) is answered `ask`, with a one-line reason; the paths come from `stateDir()` / `workflowDirs()`, so a moved `STATE_DIR` / `WORKFLOW_DIR` is followed. It only decides, never rewrites the call. Caught: Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit, and in Bash a redirect (spaced or glued to a word), `cp`/`install`/`ln`/`rsync` destination, `mv`/`rm`/`tee`/`touch`/`truncate`/`dd of=`/`sed -i` operand (also behind `then`/`do`/`!`/`{`), an interpreter's inline code naming the path (asked even when it only reads), the body of `sh -c`, a relative path after a `cd`/`pushd` to a literal directory, a heredoc body only when a shell or interpreter reads it; a path built at run time (a `cd` to one included) is not (a speed bump, not a sandbox). A default install (`STATE_DIR` and `WORKFLOW_DIR` unset) is pinned through the shell fast path too. Reads, the engine's own writes and `wf-trust` run by the user are untouched. The third bypass (an agent's Bash satisfying `wf-trust`'s terminal check through a pty) is documented as a known limit, not closed. Pinned by `src/trust-agent-bypass.test.ts` (the declared hooks run on Claude Code's payloads) and `src/trust-write-gate.test.ts`.
- queue (ENG-QUEUE-CONVERT-SWEEP-KEY): `wf-queue --convert` of an old Markdown queue dropped the task key `sweep:` into the line's brief text instead of the task's `sweep` key (the converter's key list had not been taught the key ENG-SWEEP-KEY added); it is read as a key now, and docs/queue.md § Converting names the keys the converter reads.
- docs (ENG-RELEASE-DOCS-NITS): six small text defects a reader of the published docs met. A mid-session `context_set` of `project_root` is not refused and `{{project_root}}` / `WF_PROJECT_ROOT` follow the key from then on (said in docs/action-states.md, pinned by a test in `engine.test.ts`); the `link_agent` section of docs/queue.md named an agent's session by a bare "its"; the bundled wf-yaml-authoring SKILL no longer says bundled templates load once at boot while the next bullet says a full reload re-reads them (they load at boot and on every full reload; an edit alone triggers none); the read-only Waits paragraph of the batch reviewer brief (and the engine-task one) no longer offers an `until` chain that the read-only gate refuses (`worker-waits-in-foreground.test.ts` pins the split); the context key `dispatcher_after` is named in docs/queue.md and the SKILL; on the use-case page the curator scene's three step cards align their body text from the top at 1280 px (`align-content: start` on `.ucc-task`; `site-use-case-curator-cards-align.test.ts` measures it in a real browser in both themes).
- engine (ENG-COST-TAB-BLEND-BY-MODEL-ID): two defects of the Cost tab's blended estimate (a stage with `tokens*` and no real `spend`), left by ENG-COST-TAB-BLENDED-RATES-STALE. (1) `priceOf` looked the model up in the family table, so a stage that ran on an older id (`claude-opus-4-1`, $15/$75) was priced at the newest family blend (opus 8) instead of its own (30); it now blends 75% input + 25% output of the rates `turnUsd` would use for the same id, by the same longest-key match (one `ratesOf` owns it), the bare family key being the fallback for an unknown id. (2) The tab's price note printed the blend unrounded, so a rate pair whose blend is inexact in binary would show float noise (4.300000000000001); it is rounded to four decimals at the dashboard (`dashboard/index.html`). A row's `spend`, the per-turn $ and the API's `prices.usd_per_mtok` are unchanged. `docs/dashboard.md` says so. README and the bundled skill do not list the price table. Pins: `src/calibration-blend-by-model-id.test.ts` (red with `priceOf` back on the family table), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (red with the note unrounded).
- skills (ENG-RELEASE-SKILLS-CLI-FIXES): three defects of the skills tooling, each reproduced first in a scratch plugin root and HOME. `wf-skills add x x` installed `x` once but printed it twice (a repeated name is now taken once). `wf-skills remove` and `remove --dry-run` ignored the `.wf-new.*` staging directories of an interrupted update, so an uninstall left them in `~/.claude/skills` (`remove` now sweeps the engine's own `.wf-*` prefix, never another dot directory, and the dry run lists them). `Storage.list()` took `trusted-workflows.json` for a session (the manifest had been moved out of the state directory, the consent file had not): the non-session files of the state directory are one list, `NON_SESSION_FILES`, which every session reader uses, with a pin over the whole list. The learned-skill split's duplicate check was a substring test, so a short block inside a longer one already in `learned-<name>` was taken for written and silently dropped; it now compares whole sections (the "moved from" line and the block), so only a section a run that died already wrote is skipped. A pin fails with each fix removed.
- tests (ENG-RELEASE-FLAKY-TESTS): a first `npm test` on a slow or busy host failed by chance in cases that waited on the wall clock instead of the event they assert. `dashboard.test.ts` asserted that `/api/pool?counts=1` answered in under 400 ms (red on any host whose HTTP round trip took longer, reproduced by delaying every `fetch` by 450 ms); it now asserts that the counts read never enters `Pool.measuredStatus`. The `pool.test.ts` starve case read its first wait line at whatever time the first pool pass ended, so a pass slower than `starve_after` made it say STARVED at once (the test now stalls that pass on purpose, reads the first line at the waiter's arrival, and ends the wait when a STARVED line was seen and logged, the timeout only a ceiling); the `fanout.test.ts` `on_timeout` case gives the two flows that must not time out a ceiling instead of the 200 ms under test; the `exec-timeout-pool-wait.test.ts` fan-out case compares the two shards' waits with each other instead of with 200 ms; the chain test of `test-child-vitest-chain.test.ts` goes through `spawnChild` with a timeout and passes the node path as a shell argument (a path with a space works); a fixture commit of `driver-block-agent-type.test.ts` sets `commit.gpgsign=false` itself. A new static test, `src/test-no-repo-history.test.ts`, fails when a test file pins a commit or runs a history read (`log`, `show`, `rev-parse`, `diff`...) at this repo's root, a git call with no `cwd` or `-C` included, which a depth-1 CI checkout answers differently from a local clone. No change to the engine.
- release (ENG-RELEASE-FRESH-INSTALL-SMOKE): everything so far was exercised on one machine with months of state; nothing showed what a NEW user gets. `npm run smoke:fresh` (`scripts/smoke-fresh-install.sh`, with its node half `scripts/smoke-fresh-mcp.mjs`) makes a `git clone` of HEAD in a temp dir and runs, under `env -i` with HOME, state, pool, npm cache and a free dashboard port inside it, `npm ci`, the build, the manifests, `npm pack --dry-run`, the SessionStart hook as Claude Code runs it, `wf-skills`, the MCP server over stdio (`list`, `start` of `master` and `batch`, the README's and getting-started's workflows, an exec workflow before and after the folder-trust answer), one GET on the dashboard, `wf-run`, getting-started's demo queue through `wf-queue`, `wf-skills remove --state` and, when `claude` is on PATH, `claude plugin marketplace add` / `install` under a temp config dir. One PASS / FAIL / WARN line per step; exit 0 / 1 (a FAIL) / 2 (usage, missing prerequisite) / 130 (interrupt); cleanup kills the running step's process group and removes the temp dir on every exit. The real `~/.claude` is listed before and after: a change in a path the engine owns, a file named after a session the run started or a pool entry with its lease label FAILs. A new CI job runs it. Walking the docs with it: a git-sourced install (the community catalog, this repository as a marketplace) has no `build/` and Claude Code installs dependencies with install scripts off, so its MCP server cannot start (WARN `git-channel`; the install pages now say so); the `wf-*` commands the pages tell the reader to type are not on PATH after a plugin install (getting-started now says how to run or link them); a local checkout must be built before `claude plugin marketplace add`; `--marketplace` is a `claude plugin install` flag.
- batch (ENG-DISPATCHER-HANDBACK-BY-MESSAGE): a live probe of the dispatcher agent showed that Claude Code accepts ONE `SubagentHandback` per result and refuses the second, while the instructions and the DRIVER block called each of the three messages (NEEDS-PARENT, LESSONS, HANDOVER) one hand-back. They now go by `SendMessage` to `main`; the turn-end `DISPATCHING …` line is plain text, never handed back or sent, and a `[handback-send-enforce]` prompt is answered with that line. The DRIVER block, `docs/queue.md` and the bundled `wf-yaml-authoring` skill say that main does nothing with the notification every turn end raises (no way to end a background agent's turn silently was found: a measured cost of the design), that a blocked bundled task's only decision is `close` (the line stays failed; the engine's only retry is `queue_mark` to `running` with `retry: true`, which starts no session, so no in-run route restarts it), and that main ends an orphan child session with `abort` when `close` is refused.
- batch (ENG-LESSONS-TAKE-AND-REPO-FIXES): two defects of `wf-queue --lessons` / `--lessons-take`. `--lessons-take` emptied the triage file before its rows were written to stdout, so a closed pipe or a killed call between the two lost the rows; it now writes first (waiting for the stream's own acknowledgement) and empties the file only afterwards, under the same lock (`takeTriage(file, emit)`). And `--lessons <ID> --repo DIR` for a task whose queue line has an origin checked the `touches` against the origin's repo but filed the line with `DIR`; the check and the filed `repo:` are now the same repo, the origin line's, and `--repo` applies only when the origin line is gone. Pins in `src/lessons-take-and-repo.test.ts`.
- docs (ENG-CLAUDE-MD-ROWS-CATCHUP): CLAUDE.md had fallen behind the code. Source Files names every module (trust, claude-trust, skills, spend, calibration, pool-usage, session-scope, lessons-file, read-only-gate, the site and use-case modules, the test-support modules) and the dispatcher, `transition` `set`, queue and ledger rows are current; Plugin Structure gains the `agents/*.md`, `templates/` (every edit of the bundled skill sets needs `npm run skills:history`) and `docs/` rows; Build & Run names `check:leased`, `check:ci` and `skills:history`; Environment Variables gains `WF_TRUST_STRICT`, `WF_DISPATCHER` and `CLAUDE_SKILLS_DIR`. No code change.
- docs (ENG-DOCS-BLOCK-PIN-ONE-OWNER): the docs' prose-block scanner existed in four copies (`docs-site`, the dashboard, the entry pages and the development page each had their own rules for list markers, svg and front matter), and `action-states.md` and `use-case-anyparse.md` had no pin, so `queue.md` and the use-case page drifted past the 80-word target unseen. The one scanner is now `scanDoc` in `src/test-helpers.ts`, and one page-parametrised test, `src/docs-prose-blocks.test.ts`, runs over every `docs/*.md`: no block of 120 words or more, no table cell over 40 words, no block of 80 words or more except the listed exemption (the two-ways paragraph of `concepts.md`, which README mirrors). The three copies' block and cell pins are gone (the dashboard test keeps its heading checks as `doc-dashboard-headings.test.ts`, the development test its sentence and parenthesis pins), and five blocks over 80 words in `queue.md` and `use-case-anyparse.md` are split with the facts unchanged. Docs and tests only; nothing shipped changes.
- calibration (ENG-CALIBRATION-REVIEW-ROUNDS-OVERCOUNT): the per-task `rounds` of the cost report (`buildCost`, `GET /api/cost`, the dashboard Cost tab) summed the visits of every `review*` state, so a workflow whose review is several states (`review`, `review_spawn`, `review_warm`) counted one round two or three times (a ledger line with visits review 2, review_spawn 2, review_warm 1 reported 5). The `wf-calibration` table's `review_rounds` already used `visits.review` and was right; its earlier readings are not inflated. One definition, `reviewRounds`, now serves both sites: the visits of the state `review` when the entry has one, the sum of the `review*` states (routing aside) only for a workflow with no such state. Cost-report rounds read earlier for such workflows were inflated about 2x. Pinned in `src/calibration-rounds.test.ts`. `docs/queue.md` and `docs/dashboard.md` say what a round is.
- loader (ENG-LIVE-TEMPLATE-RELOAD-BREAKS-RUNNING-SERVER): a running MCP server lost `batch` and every workflow that `extends: batch` (`Workflow "slice-dispatch" not found`, `list` without `batch`, in every project on that checkout) when a land added a top-level key to a bundled template that the server's older schema did not know. The cause: every full load re-read the bundled directory from the live checkout (the directory watcher, a trust change, and a fresh project loader built by every `start` and by every call on a session this process holds no snapshot of), the schema refused the new key, the loader wrote the reason to stderr only, dropped the file, and the heir then failed with `extends "batch": no such workflow`, which the engine reported as `not found`. Now the bundled templates are read as one set: when any of them fails to reload (its schema, its `extends` chain or validation), the whole previous set keeps serving, so no heir is paired with a parent of the other version; a project loader takes the server's own bundled tier instead of reading the checkout again, `list` shows every file that did not load under `BROKEN (did not load)` with the loader's reason (for an unknown key in a bundled template: this server is older than the template, restart Claude Code), and `start` and a call on a session answer `Workflow "<name>" failed to load: <file>: <reason>` instead of `not found`, an heir naming its parent's failure. Snapshots: a session keeps the definitions it started with in the server that started it (the heir, its `extends:` parent and every sub-workflow are in the snapshot, merged at load); another server process, or a session resumed after a restart, has none. Pinned in `src/live-template-reload.test.ts`: an heir's new start, a session moved by a server without its snapshot, the catalogue after a refused reload, the stale set listed with the restart reason, a file that passes the schema but fails validation, a refused parent with a bundled heir changed in the same reload, a call on a session whose workflow failed to load, one line per failure, the failure message of a template that never loaded, and `list`.

- skills (ENG-LEARNED-SKILL-LOADING): when reflection enriched a bundled skill in the user's copy, the manifest counted that copy as `edited` and it stopped receiving the engine's updates for good (a forced update would wipe the enrichment); only the maintainer's hand-sync ever carried a lesson into the bundle. Reflection's ENRICH of a bundled skill now writes `~/.claude/skills/learned-<name>/SKILL.md` (one file per bundled skill, sections appended, frontmatter given in `skill-manager`) and leaves the shipped copy unedited. A scratch probe on Claude Code 2.1.327 (a project skill `learned-<x>` with a description naming `<x>`, `claude -p` on haiku) loaded the sibling by its description alone and again beside an explicit `Skill(<x>)`, 3 of 3, so no workflow's `skills:` group got an optional `?learned-<name>`. The manifest, `sync`, `update` and `remove` ignore `learned-*` (a release never ships one; `list` shows them under their own heading), and a new `wf-skills split <name>` moves the text added to an edited bundled copy into `learned-<name>` and restores the shipped text, only when the diff is pure additions (a changed or removed line, a deleted file or an addition to the frontmatter refuses, writing nothing); the move and the restore run under one hold of the skills lock, a run that died between them is finished by the next without a block twice, and an added file moves byte for byte; the first-edited note names it. `wf-skills diff` on a stack skill compared against the default set's path and now reads the pack's. Promotion into the bundle stays the maintainer's act (`docs/concepts.md`). Pinned in `src/learned-skills.test.ts`: the sibling written and the manifest still updating the shipped copy, `remove` keeping `learned-*` even when a manifest records one, `split` on additions and each refusal, the CLI heading, and the reflection and skill-manager text.
- reflection (ENG-REFLECTION-HOME-CHECK): two of twelve recent lessons were user-corrected process habits ("read the current API spec before blaming the backend", "probe the backend with curl before code hypotheses") that the `classify` table itself calls skills, and both went to project memory; and `suggest_improvements` closed on `done` seconds after the suggestion, before the user could answer, so `evolve` could not fire. `templates/reflection.yaml`: a lesson bound for memory first passes a one-question state `home_check` ("name the bundled skill this lesson would have prevented in a DIFFERENT project, or `none`"; `skill` takes the ENRICH route, `none` goes on to `action`), the table gains the row "The user corrected my approach (not a project fact) -> Skill (ENRICH)", and `suggest_improvements` now ends the turn on the suggestion and calls its transition on the user's next message (a headless run does not wait: `done`), the same wait the manual-verify state of `testing` uses. `skill-manager` section 5 (ENRICH) is triggered by a verifiable mistake or a user-corrected process pattern (the rule and the tell, no code verification), the verified path for a factual gotcha unchanged. `docs/concepts.md` says the batch task workflows have no reflect step by design and that what their review rounds teach is not classed. Pinned in `src/reflection-home-check.test.ts`.
- batch (ENG-LESSONS-RECORDS): a landed engine task's lessons were read and turned into queue lines by the dispatcher on every land (one agent turn per land, a judgment per lesson, a prose phrase the driver copied from a section), though most of them need none. The worker and the reviewer of `batch-task` and `engine-task` now hand their lessons over as records: the answer carries `lessons=<one-line JSON>` (`engine-task`: a `LESSONS: <JSON>` line), the driver copies it verbatim into the `set` of its next transition (`lessons` on the worker's `check`, `review_lessons` on the verdict), and a new exec step `lessons_file` between `mark_done` and `cleanup` writes the two keys to `<queue dir>/../backups/slice-logs/<ID>/` and runs the new `wf-queue <q> --lessons <ID> --records FILE… [--triage FILE] [--repo DIR] [--workflow NAME]` (`src/lessons-file.ts`). `unverified` becomes a backlog line (priority low), `arm-rot-on-base` a pending one (priority high, difficulty 4), each with the `repo:` and `workflow:` of the task it came from and its `touches` checked against that repo; every other kind and every refused record (bad kind or id, empty text or repro, `touches` empty, over 8, a glob, a `..` path, a directory, absent and not `(new)`, a taken or archived id, a duplicate of an open line, a queue refusal) becomes a row of a triage file (`--triage`, else the header key `lessons_triage:`, else `../backups/slice-logs/lessons-triage.txt` beside the queue's directory) that the dispatcher reads in a batch, never per land, with the new `wf-queue <q> --lessons-take [--triage FILE]` (prints and empties the file under the lock a land's append takes; emptying it by hand could drop a row appended meanwhile). The command prints `lessons=filed N, triage M[, already K][ triage_file=<path>]` (the file is named when rows were added, so the `LANDED` line that reaches the main conversation says where they wait) and exits 0 for any records (2 for usage); a missing or empty file is no records, an unparseable one is one triage row with its text, and a rerun files nothing twice. The step never fails a land: any failure prints `lessons=not run` and `cleanup` runs; the driver's `LANDED` line carries the line, `batch` `collect` reads nothing for a filed lesson, and a `CLOSED` line keeps its copied prose. Pins: `src/lessons-file.test.ts` (every rule, the rerun, the CLI) and `src/lessons-flow.test.ts` (the keys, the routes, the exec's never-fails, the driver and dispatcher words), each failing with its code removed. README, `docs/queue.md`, `docs/concepts.md`, `docs/development.md` and the bundled `wf-yaml-authoring` skill say it.
- engine (ENG-PROJECT-ROOT-VAR): a workflow had no way to name the project's root: `{{context.cwd}}` is a batch task's worktree, so a path under the main checkout (the queue file, a `lib/` script) could be spelled portably in an exec (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`) but not in a prompt, and a tracked workflow carried absolute `/Users/...` paths. New template variable `{{project_root}}` (prompts, `command`, `env`, `cwd`, revisit prompts) and exec env `WF_PROJECT_ROOT` beside `WF_PLUGIN_ROOT`: the main checkout of the session's cwd (`projectRoot`: a linked worktree maps to its main worktree, a cwd outside a repo gives the cwd). It is resolved once at `start` and kept in the session context (`context.project_root`, as `context.project` already is), so a worktree removed mid-run still renders it; a session stored before the key existed maps its cwd at each render. `start` refuses an initial `project_root` in its context, as it does `cwd`, `claude_code_pid` and `project`. A state's own `env:` may override `WF_PROJECT_ROOT`; an inherited one is dropped. A workflow without the variable is unchanged. Documented in `docs/action-states.md` (Template variables, the exec env) and the bundled `wf-yaml-authoring` skill. A new variable renders only in sessions of a restarted server.
- batch (ENG-DISPATCHER-DRIVER): an opt-in dispatcher agent over the task drivers. The main conversation did every launch itself in its long context: `start`, the driver spawn, `link_agent`, the `tasks` key, `slot_free`, plus a turn per notification and stray line (measured on one campaign: most of its spend was work a driver's report triggers, not decisions). New workflow key `dispatcher: { model, effort, prompt_ref, agent_type?, env?, after? }`, declared by bundled `batch` with `env: WF_DISPATCHER`, `after: plan_queue`. The opt-in is that environment variable, not a queue header key: `start` answers before a queue file is known, and the nesting depth the agent needs is set in the same place. Unset (or empty / `0` / `false` / `off` / `no`), nothing changes: `start` answers byte for byte as before and the main conversation dispatches as today. Set, `start` of the dispatcher session ends with a DRIVER block for the new agent type `workflow:dispatcher` (`agents/dispatcher.md`, haiku/medium; workflow tools `start` / `status` / `transition` / `context_set` / `link_agent` only, so no `abort`, `delete`, `queue_mark`, `queue_edit` or `queue_add`), kept in the context as `dispatcher_call`, and the `transition` reply that leaves `plan_queue` repeats it. Its instructions are `templates/drivers/dispatch-driver.md`: it drives `dispatch` and `collect` (the keys ride the transition's `set`), absorbs WAITING lines and notifications, relays a stray worker or reviewer line to that task's driver, re-spawns a dead driver from `driver_call`, and hands the main conversation only a driver's `NEEDS-PARENT` line verbatim, a `LESSONS` block of the drivers' last lines once per `lessons_every` lands (a queue header key, default 5; copied, never judged) and a `HANDOVER` at a state it does not drive. Two engine guards apply only under such a session: a second `start` of a task that already runs there is refused, and the restart pickup, once the session has left `plan_queue` or has an agent linked, names the dispatcher's SendMessage in place of the task sessions of its `tasks` key (any other sub-session is still listed for the main conversation). The block carries one line on depth: main → dispatcher → task driver → worker → the worker's helper needs `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH=4`, one more than the default. Not yet run live: the engine cannot drive itself, so where a driver's report goes after the dispatcher handed back, a fourth-level spawn and the rotation relay are for the first trial to show.
- queue (ENG-SWEEP-KEY): an optional task key `sweep:` (one non-empty line: the command a line runs over a whole tree, e.g. a lint rule's `--fix`). A line that sweeps a tree could only say `exclusive: true`, which holds every other line of its repo for hours; a project that computes a footprint lock instead needs the line to declare its command, and the engine refuses an unknown key, so the command was carried as a marker in the text. The key is data for the project's workflow and has no meaning for the engine: it implies no `exclusive`, no pick rule reads it, and `queue_next` only prints it (`| sweep: <command>` on the admitted line; the tab-separated exec form keeps its columns), `GET /api/queue` carries it as `sweep`. `queue_add` / `queue_edit` / `wf-queue --add|--edit` take it (`""` or null removes it) and refuse a value that is empty, spans lines or is not a string with the key named; it sits after `owner` in `TASK_KEYS`, survives a mark, the archive and a restore, and an older server reads it as an unknown key and ignores it. It is part of the brief a recon reads, so an edit that changes it rewrites `base` and `text_hash` like one of `touches` (the hash of a line without `sweep:` is the one it had before). `docs/queue.md`, the tool descriptions and the bundled `wf-yaml-authoring` skill (digest added to `templates/skills-history.txt`) say so. Pin: `src/queue-sweep-key.test.ts` (known key and order, the admitted row and the view, no pick meaning, set / change / remove, the base and hash rewrite, archive and restore, refusals), failing with the key, the hash term, the row or the check removed. A task key is read only by a restarted server.
- skills (ENG-SKILLS-GENERIC-DEFAULT): a new machine got all 29 bundled skills, 15 of them for stacks it may never touch (Haxe, Pixi, CMake, a Zed integration...), against the repo's own rule that bundled skills hold nothing project-specific. The default set is now the 12 universal skills (architecture, browser-verify, ci-github-actions, claude-code-config, cleanup, debug-bridge-scaffold, debugging, mcp-setup, skill-manager, task-delegation, web-reading, wf-yaml-authoring) and the two templates (coding-skill-selector, preferences): a fresh HOME gets 14 directories, not 29, and 822 bytes of descriptions are listed per session, not 1685. The other 15 (aws-lambda, build-cmake, domain-gamedev, domain-pixi, domain-pony, domain-reid, domain-ui, domain-yolo, hxq, hxq-write, ide-zed, lang-as3, lang-haxe, lang-python, target-openfl-native) moved to `templates/skills-stack/`, in the same plugin and a directory the start hook never copies from. `wf-skills add <name>…` and `wf-skills add --all-stack` install them through the manifest, so update and removal work for them as for the default set; `wf-skills list` shows an uninstalled one as `available` (`pack` in `--json`). `coding-skill-selector` now tells the agent to offer a shipped, uninstalled stack skill once, with the exact `wf-skills add <name>` command (never installing silently; a skill you deleted is `deleted`, not offered again), and every start leaves a link to the command at `$STATE_DIR/skills/wf-skills` for the agent to run; in Zed the hook offers `ide-zed` the same way. No workflow `skills:` group names a stack skill without `?`, so a missing one never blocks a gate (pinned). Nothing is removed: an installed stack skill (every one on an existing machine) stays installed and keeps being updated through the manifest; the lang-* rule ("copied only while the selector is installed") is gone with the move. `skills-history.txt` and the drift scripts (`check-skill-sync.sh`, `sync-live-skills.sh`) cover both directories. Docs: `docs/concepts.md` lists the two sets, a line per skill, the install command and the offer; the README bullet and a landing card say what ships by default.

- engine-task (ENG-TASK-WORKTREE-SURVIVES-REBOOT): a Mac reboot wiped `/tmp/wf-<TASK>` (the worktree, its `-logs`) while branch `eng/<id>` and its commit survived, and the session misread it: `project_land` exit 5 -> `project_rebase` exit 1 -> `rebase_conflict` -> `rebase_fix` -> `blocked`, which offers no way back, so the parent re-attached the worktree and rebased by hand. The worktree now lives at `<repo>/../.wt/<repo-name>/<TASK>` (the placement `batch-task` already had), `project_prepare` re-attaches a surviving branch whose directory is gone instead of failing on `-b`, and `project_rebase` reports a missing worktree as its own exit 6 and routes it to a new `rebase_reattach` state (a branch gone too is its own exit 7, never made anew) (the prepare command again, bounded to 2 visits, then back to the rebase) instead of `rebase_conflict`. `cleanup` also admits a legacy `/tmp/wf-<TASK>` path for a session already in flight. `src/worktree-survives-reboot.test.ts` pins the placement, the re-attach, the exit-6 route and the bound; `batch-task`'s only `/tmp` path is its reviewer's transient `review.md`, unchanged.
- engine (ENG-TRANSITION-CARRIES-VALUES): `transition` takes an optional `set: {key: value, ...}`: the keys are written to the context first, in the given order, each as `context_set` writes it (a `difficulty` outside 1..10 under a `model_table` is refused, and the table derives its keys), and the transition is taken in the same call and the same write, so the entered exec, prompt and templates see them. All or nothing: a refused key or a refused move writes none of it (a session waiting for its children refuses `set` outright); the reply starts with `Context set: <keys>.`. History and the event log keep one `context_set` line per key, before the `transition` line, so no reader changes; `context_set` is unchanged and a call without `set` behaves as before. Measured on 251 landed slices, 24.6 requests per slice held nothing but `context_set` calls, 22.7 of them directly followed by the `transition` that reads the key, because a transition must see the key written and the workflows forbade one parallel batch. Bundled `batch-task` (`claim` -> `prepare`, `work` -> `check`, `review` -> land / fix / reject) and the driver instructions (`templates/drivers/task-driver.md`, `agents/driver.md`) now say to pass those keys in `set`. The MCP tool schema changes only after a restart.
- engine-task and batch-task (ENG-WORKER-WAITS-IN-FOREGROUND): a worker, recon or reviewer that started a long check in the background and ended its turn made the harness notify the MAIN conversation ("is waiting on its own background work", 74 such wakes 2026-11-08..18, about a dozen on 2026-11-18 alone), each worker wake and stop again, and the final DONE of five engine-task workers reached the main conversation; one worker delivered the same report 7 times to the `[handback-send-enforce]` prompt. Four scratch agents under a driver-typed agent measured it on Claude Code 2.1.327: (a) a child's foreground command of 200 s gave its parent no notification and the child's line resumed the driver that had ended its turn with plain text; (b) a child that ended its turn with a live background task stopped its driver with the notice "waiting on its own background work", the ping made it stop again with the same notice, and the driver got the `[handback-send-enforce]` prompt four times; (c) a child that finished after its driver had handed back reached the driver (a hand-back message and a completion notice, then the enforce prompt twice), not the main conversation; (d) a child that ended its turn with plain text got "[handback-send-enforce] Your report has not been delivered. Call SubagentHandback(...) now". The briefs (`templates/batch-task.yaml`, `.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml`: worker, reproducer, reviewer) now say: a command the agent waits for runs in a foreground Bash call with timeout 600000; one that may outlast a call (the check behind a pool lease) is started detached with a done-marker and awaited in a chain of foreground `until [ -s L.done ] || ! kill -0 "$(cat L.pid)" … || [ $i -ge 540 ]` calls (run on this harness: the sleep guard does not refuse it); no Monitor for a command it waits for, and a foreground call the harness moved to the background is TaskStopped or awaited in foreground calls before any end of turn; the agent never ends a turn while something it started is alive, mid-run as well as at its last line. A read-only type (the reviewer: the gate refuses a redirect, `$( )` and `sleep`) is given only foreground calls and names a command it cannot finish as unverified. After its last line an agent answers ONLY a `[handback-send-enforce]` prompt with the plain-text line `DELIVERED <task>`, never by SendMessage to main, and a Driver ping that asks for its line gets the line. A sub-agent's Agent tool has no `run_in_background` (every spawn is asynchronous): the dead words are gone from the spawn texts of the briefs and of `templates/drivers/task-driver.md` and `agents/driver.md` (kept identical). The drivers answer a "waits on its own background work" notice with the WAITING line only (the ping rule is removed: it only made the agent stop again), read `DELIVERED <task>` from an agent whose line they hold as no report, and ask ONE "repeat your one line" of an agent whose line they have not received. The final line still goes by SubagentHandback to the direct parent: (a), (c) and (d) show it reaches the driver, and the driver cannot learn its own agent id for a SendMessage. `src/worker-waits-in-foreground.test.ts` pins the rules and runs the reviewer brief's commands through the read-only gate; `src/worker-no-live-bg-at-turn-end.test.ts` follows the new wording.
- site (ENG-SITE-USE-CASE-ANYPARSE): a use-case page, `docs/use-case-anyparse.md` (linked from the landing's new "In real use" section, the sidebar's "Use cases" group and the Docs menu): how the open-source anyparse project is run on this engine, a queue line's way from the queue to main, in seven interactive scenes drawn in the dashboard's tokens, in both themes and at 390 px. User 2026-11-18: «Может даже наприсовать упрощёнными блок схемами, то как у нас работа над anyparse для примера ведётся? … как от сложности выбиратеся путь. и вот онин из путей с разведчиком который может сложность поменять», and after the storyboard: «в целом хорошо выглядит, нужно делать». The scenes (`docs/.vitepress/theme/UseCase*.vue`, sharing `use-case.css`): (1) a queue line whose every field says who reads it, over the project's difficulty ruler and the ledger's lands by difficulty; (2) the pick over a small queue: the reader lands a running line, changes the window or promotes the backlog line, and every waiting line gives the pick's own reason, with the engine's exclusive hold on or off as the project's queue header says (`exclusive_hold`, read by the export; anyparse has it off); (3) difficulty picks the path: what the line says against what the code turns out to be, a flowchart of the way (no recon below the threshold, a recon whose score replaces the line's, a recon score that is the plan, a hand-off to an executor, a split, a line closed on a refuted premise) with every agent's model and effort, the model table and the ledger's count for that way; (4) the review rounds as a lane that grows a row per FIXES, with the ledger's cost of a landed line by rounds and by stage; (5) where the project's rule sends each lesson a landed line leaves; (6) the curator: the ledger's lands cut into windows of today's cadence (a cut of the ledger, not the runs that happened), each against the one before, and the calibration table by difficulty band opening into its stages and models; (7) the numbers, each figure saying how it is counted, with the line that on a subscription the API-equivalent $ is a yardstick of limit use, not a bill. Nothing on the page is typed: the paths, the pick and the lane are pure code the page runs in the browser (`slicePath` in `src/use-case-path.ts`; `toyPick`, `roundsLane` and the scenes' data in `src/use-case-scenes.ts`) over a snapshot, `docs/use-case-anyparse.json`, written by `npm run build && npm run docs:use-case -- --project-dir <the anyparse checkout>` (`scripts/export-use-case.mjs`): the thresholds, the driver and the model table from the project's workflow files (`workflowFacts` of `src/use-case.ts` refuses a file that no longer says one of them; `--model-table <file>` takes the table from a dated copy, the one in force while the ledger's runs were made, which is what the committed snapshot shows: User «Да и опус можно показывать, почему нет?»; `--to` ends the window there), and the ledger's finished runs of that one project's pipeline (`foldUseCase`: lands, a landed line's cost by review rounds, by difficulty, by band and by stage, what the recon did, the curator's windows, hours by pipelines open at once). The snapshot is dated by its own window, names no task, session, agent or path (`assertUseCaseClean` refuses the write otherwise), and the same inputs give the same bytes. The scenes are rendered at build time and have their colours before any script: `config.mts` writes the dashboard's tokens into every page's head on `.dash-tokens` (`theme/dash-tokens.ts`, the rewrite `dashboard-css.ts` now shares). Pins: `src/use-case.test.ts` (the path, `stageRow` against the engine's `chooseStages`, the facts and their refusals, the fold, the committed snapshot), `src/use-case-scenes.test.ts` (the line's fields are the queue's task keys; `toyPick` answers as `planPick` in every state the scene can reach; the lane's bounds are the facts'), `src/site-use-case.test.ts` (real browser, the built site: every scene shows the snapshot's facts and counts and answers a choice, both themes, 1280 and 390 px, the HTML with no script). `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` and `src/site-map-replay.test.ts` follow the shared rewrite, and the keyboard walk of `src/site-install-copy.test.ts` is bounded by the page's focusable elements instead of 40 presses (the Docs menu has one more link ahead of the Install block). The bundled skill does not describe the site's pages.
- skills (ENG-SKILLS-MANIFEST): the SessionStart hook copied a bundled skill into `~/.claude/skills` only when its directory was missing and recorded nothing, so a release's changes never reached an installed copy and an uninstall left every copy behind with no way to tell them from the user's own. Now `$STATE_DIR/skills/manifest.json` (in a subdirectory: a `*.json` directly in the state dir is a session to `Storage.list()`) records, for every file copied, the skill, the relative path, the sha256 as shipped and the engine version. On every start an UNEDITED copy (its files equal the manifest, the version now shipped, or any version the repo ever shipped) is replaced by the shipped version; an edited copy is kept and named once per shipped version in the hook's output (a note after the usual line, with `wf-skills diff <name>`); a skill the user deleted stays deleted (recorded; `wf-skills update <name>` brings it back); a skill directory that is a link is never touched; `lang-*` skills still follow `coding-skill-selector`. A machine with copies and no manifest is adopted by `templates/skills-history.txt`, the digest of every version of every skill the repo ever shipped (bootstrapped from git by `node scripts/skills-history.mjs --from-git`, extended by `npm run skills:history`; `src/skills.test.ts` fails while a shipped version is missing, so a release cannot skip it; nothing reads git at run time). A plugin root that is not newer than the one that wrote a copy never takes it back to older text. New `wf-skills` (`scripts/wf-skills`, `build/skills-cli.js`, a `bin` of the package): `list [--json]`, `diff <name>`, `update <name>…` (an edited copy is saved to `$STATE_DIR/skill-backups` first), `remove [--state] [--dry-run]` (every unedited engine copy and the manifest; never an edited copy or a skill the engine did not ship; `--state` also the state and pool directories after naming them, and the workflow directory while empty). The uninstall path is in `docs/concepts.md` and the README: run `wf-skills remove` before `/plugin uninstall`. The hook and the CLI resolve the skills dir the same way (`CLAUDE_SKILLS_DIR`, else `~/.claude/skills`); the lock never loops on an unwritable state dir (the hook's sync gives up, `update`/`remove` fail); a copy left between the two renames of an interrupted swap is restored, not taken for a deletion; `wf-skills sync` exits 2 on an error. Without node or the build the hook falls back to the old copy-if-missing rule. Read on the author's machine (read-only `wf-skills list`, no manifest yet): of 29 bundled names, 28 are unedited and current, 1 (`coding-skill-selector`) is edited and would be kept, 0 outdated.
