- security (ENG-TRUST-FROM-CLAUDE-FOLDER-TRUST): a project's trust now comes from the answer the user already gave Claude Code, so the common case needs nothing from the user (ENG-EXEC-CONSENT asked for `wf-trust` in a terminal per file and again after every edit). A project's workflow files load their exec/fetch states when Claude Code's user config (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json`, default `~/.claude.json`) holds `projects[<project path>].hasTrustDialogAccepted === true` (`src/claude-trust.ts`; read only, never written). The lookup follows Claude Code's own keying: a trusted folder covers the folders below it up to the git root of their repository, a linked worktree is its main checkout's entry alone, the home directory is never trusted (its answer is session-only), and `CLAUDE_CODE_IGNORE_PERSISTED_TRUST` makes no record count; a missing file, entry or flag, an unreadable or malformed file, or a renamed flag all mean not trusted (fail closed; the flag is an internal record of Claude Code, not a documented interface). An edited file needs no new approval; a running server notices an answer given in Claude Code on its next read (`Loader._syncTrust`; Claude Code's constant rewrites of its config reload nothing unless the config now trusts what was left out). `wf-trust <project>` stays as the fallback for a folder Claude Code has no record for (headless `claude -p`): it approves the PROJECT DIRECTORY once (`projects` in `$STATE_DIR/trusted-workflows.json`) instead of each file's hash, `wf-trust <file.yaml>` still approves one file's sha256, per-file approvals of ENG-EXEC-CONSENT keep working, and `--list` / `--revoke <project dir | hash prefix>` cover both. New option `WF_TRUST_STRICT=1` (off by default) restores the per-file sha256 regardless of Claude Code's answer or a project approval (and `wf-trust <project>` then records each file's hash). An untrusted project's file is left out as before; `list` / `start` now say in one sentence which project is untrusted, that no Claude Code folder trust is recorded for it, and the two fixes (accept Claude Code's trust question for the folder, or `wf-trust <project>`). `modify` and `create` still refuse exec/fetch states. A linked worktree counts as its main checkout only when its pointer is mutual, as Claude Code checks it (`<gitdir>/commondir` resolves to the main `.git`, the gitdir is a direct child of `<common>/worktrees`, `<gitdir>/gitdir` points back at the worktree's `.git`); a forged pointer (a cloned repo naming a trusted project's `.git`) or any read error makes the directory its own root, so it is never trusted through another project, for Claude's answer and for `wf-trust` approvals alike. `wf-trust <project>` in a worktree names the main checkout it records and says it covers its worktrees. `src/trust-claude-folder.test.ts` pins every rule on a scratch config dir and home (trusted / false / absent / renamed flag / malformed / unreadable config, parent and git-root bounds, worktree, home, strict, the `wf-trust` fallback and revoke, a later answer picked up); `src/exec-consent.test.ts` now runs on a strict store.
- site (ENG-SITE-INSTALL-COPY): the landing's Install block had no copy button, unlike the code blocks of the docs pages. Its two `/plugin …` lines are now in the markup VitePress renders for a markdown code block (`div.language-text`, `button.copy`, `span.lang`, `pre`) inside a `.vp-doc` wrapper, so the button is the docs pages' own: same look and position, shown on hover and on keyboard focus, "Copied" feedback, VitePress's click handler copying both lines. `Install.vue` adds no style or script for it, only margin and radius so the block keeps the hero's edges at 390 px. `docs/development.md` names it; README and the bundled skills do not list the landing's blocks. Pin: `src/site-install-copy.test.ts` (real browser, both themes at 1280 and 390 px: Tab reaches the button, Enter copies both commands, `Copied` shows; red with the old `<pre>` restored).
- engine (ENG-COST-TAB-BLENDED-RATES-STALE): the Cost tab's blended price table `PRICES_USD_PER_MTOK` (`src/calibration.ts`) was a hand-kept copy that still carried the Opus $15/$75 era blends (opus 30, sonnet 6, haiku 2), so the tab's per-stage and per-task estimate (a stage with `tokens*` and no real `spend`) overstated Opus about 4x against `TURN_RATES_USD_PER_MTOK`, which had the current rates. The blended table is now derived from the bare family keys of the per-turn table (75% input, 25% output): opus 8 (Opus 5.5 at $4/$20), sonnet 4 ($2/$10), haiku 0.2 ($0.10/$0.50), fable 16 ($8/$40), one source, so a rate edit moves both. A row's `spend` and the per-turn $ are unchanged. `docs/dashboard.md` says the table is derived. README and the bundled skill do not list the price table. Pins: `src/calibration-blended-rates.test.ts` (red with the table hand-kept at the old values), `src/calibration.test.ts`, `src/dashboard.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-COST-TAB-FABLE-PRICE): the Cost tab's blended per-stage price table `PRICES_USD_PER_MTOK` (`src/calibration.ts`) had opus, sonnet and haiku but no fable, so a Fable stage showed no blended $ (per-turn spend through `turnUsd` was unaffected). The table has `fable: 16`, the same blend as the others (75% input, 25% output: Fable 5.2 at $8/$40), so `priceOf` of any `claude-fable-*` id answers and the tab's stage rows and per-task estimate price a Fable stage; `PRICES_UPDATED` is 2026-11-18, and the rate-source date in `calibration.ts`'s comment and `docs/action-states.md` (was 2026-10-06) is the date the Fable 5.2 / Sonnet 5.5 rates were taken. `docs/dashboard.md` names the four families. README and the bundled skill do not list the price table. Pin: `src/cost-fable-price.test.ts` (red with the `fable` entry removed).
- engine (ENG-PRICING-FABLE-5-2): `TURN_RATES_USD_PER_MTOK` (`src/calibration.ts`) had no `claude-fable-5-2`, so a Fable 5.2 turn matched the `claude-fable-5` key and was priced $10/$50 with cache reads at $1 instead of $8/$40, cache read $.20, 5-minute cache write $10. The table has `claude-fable-5-2` with those rates, the bare `fable` family key prices an unknown Fable id as 5.2, and `claude-sonnet-5-5` (and the bare `sonnet` key) reads the cache at $.10, not $.20. `spend.ts` and the dashboard price through `turnUsd` over this one table, no copy. The ledger stores a task's `spend` at land time, so rows already written keep the $ they were landed with. Also, the export-replay tests of `src/replay.test.ts` read the host's real pool log (`~/.claude/workflow-pool`) and failed wherever it did not cover their fixture window; they now point `WF_POOL_DIR` at an empty directory of their own. Pins: `src/pricing-fable-5-2.test.ts`, `src/turn-rates.test.ts`.
- skills (ENG-SKILL-RENAME-WORKFLOW-AUTHORING): the bundled YAML authoring skill is renamed `workflow-authoring` -> `wf-yaml-authoring`. Claude Code bundles its own `/workflow-authoring` (the reference for its native JS workflows), and a personal skill of the same name in `~/.claude/skills` replaces it, so every install of the engine shadowed the native one (User 2026-11-18: «переименовать наш … — да, делаем»). The SessionStart hook now copies `templates/skills/wf-yaml-authoring`, and migrates an earlier install: `~/.claude/skills/workflow-authoring` is MOVED (never deleted, so an enriched live body survives) to `<state dir>/skill-backups/workflow-authoring.<epoch>.<pid>` when its SKILL.md carries the description an engine release shipped (`Reference for authoring workflow YAML` or `Reference for creating workflows with exec/fetch action states`) and the directory holds nothing else but a Finder `.DS_Store`; a user's own skill of that name, or one with extra files, is left alone (move it away by hand if it is still the engine's). Every reference moves with it (README, `docs/concepts.md`, `docs/getting-started.md`, the `planning`, `reflection`, `docs`, `coding` and `bug-fix` templates, `coding-skill-selector`). A live skill dir named `workflow-authoring` is no longer synced from the repo; the live `wf-yaml-authoring` is created by the hook on the next start. Pinned by `src/skill-rename-workflow-authoring.test.ts`.
- security (ENG-EXEC-CONSENT): two ways to get a shell command run through the engine with no one's approval are closed. (1) `modify` accepted a state of `type: exec` (or `fetch`) with any command, and the docs recommended auto-allowing `modify` / `create` / `delete`, so an agent under prompt injection could run arbitrary shell outside Claude Code's Bash permissions, sandbox and hooks: `modify` and `create` now refuse an exec/fetch state (the error says to write the file and have the user run `wf-trust`), a session overlay that already holds one fails at state resolution instead of running (`Engine._noActionFromOverlay`, which also refuses a changed `fanout`, `cwd`, `env` or fetch field of an existing action state), and `getting-started.md`'s recommended allowlist no longer lists `modify`, `create` or `delete`. (2) A project's own YAML (`.claude/workflows/`, a cloned repository's, say a `master.yaml` over the bundled one) ran its exec states on the first `start()` with no consent: a project file that declares an exec/fetch state is now left out of the load (`Loader`, project tier only; the same-name file of the tier below answers, so the bundled `master` runs) until the USER approves its exact content. The approval is the file's sha256 in `$STATE_DIR/trusted-workflows.json`, written by the new `wf-trust <project>` CLI (`src/trust.ts`, `src/trust-run.ts`, `src/trust-cli.ts`, `scripts/wf-trust`, a `wf-trust` bin): it prints every command and request (also each `fanout` `items_command`, `cwd`, `env`, and a fetch's `headers` and `body`) and asks for a typed `yes`, and refuses to run without a terminal, so an agent's Bash cannot approve and no MCP tool can; `--list` and `--revoke <hash prefix>` manage it. A running server picks an approval up on its next read (no restart), a file edited afterwards needs approval again, an identical copy (a worktree) is approved with it, and a session already running keeps the approved definition it started with (a restarted server meets the edited file unapproved, and the transition errors instead of running it). `list` and `start` name the left-out file and how to approve it (`start` of a missing name says so in its error; a started name shadowing an unapproved project file gets a NOTE). Bundled templates, `~/.claude/workflows/` and project files with prompt states only load as before. One-time step for existing projects whose `.claude/workflows/` files have exec states (this repository's own `.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml`, the author's other projects): run `wf-trust <project>` in a terminal once per file version (`<plugin>/scripts/wf-trust <project>`, or `node build/trust-cli.js <project>`). `src/exec-consent.test.ts` and `src/exec-consent-repro.test.ts` pin each refusal and the round trip; docs: `getting-started.md` ("Trusting project workflows"), `workflow-yaml.md`, `action-states.md`, `concepts.md`, README and the bundled `wf-yaml-authoring` skill. Also: `export-replay.mjs` loads with `TrustStore.describeOnly()` (it only describes workflows). Not in scope: template escaping, the dashboard's Origin check.
- site, replay (ENG-REPLAY-DRIVER-MODEL-REEXPORT): the committed replay still carried the workflows from before anyparse's `slice-pipeline` had a `driver:` block, so a lane drew `driver · opus` or `driver · sonnet`. `docs/public/replay/anyparse.json` is re-exported with the same window as before (`--from 2026-11-16T09:30:00Z --hours 4`, `--project-dir` the anyparse checkout, `--workflows` the engine's `.claude/workflows`: 46 sessions, 175 queue lines, 16 tasks with spend, 189 pool samples, 152.8 KB): `slice-pipeline` and `engine-task` now carry `driver: haiku / medium`, which the map and the replayed conversation read, and the queue section is the engine's pick over today's queue file rolled back to the window's start. `docs/media/scene.gif`, `docs/banner.png` and `docs/social-preview.png` (and its copy `docs/public/og.png`) are re-rendered from it. The window holds no pool `underuse:` line (the pool logged none before ENG-POOL-HELD-EQUALS-USED), so the replayed Pool tab shows only backfill and overrun marks; the banner's default-moment pin held without change. No code changed. Pin: `src/replay.test.ts` (the committed file declares the driver model for `slice-pipeline` and `engine-task`, every driver chip the map draws over the window and every replayed driver spawn reads haiku, never `driver · opus` or `driver · sonnet`), failing with the old file.
- site (ENG-SITE-AUTOHIDE-FOCUS-VISIBLE-ONLY): below 960 px the bar's keyboard-focus reveal keyed on `:focus-within`, so on a phone a TAPPED control in the bar (the theme switch, the hamburger after the menu closes) kept focus and the bar never hid again on scroll down; and focus only in the local nav revealed the local nav while the bar stayed hidden, leaving an empty gap above it. The reveal now follows `:focus-visible` (a tap shows no focus ring, so it holds nothing open) and shows both bars together whenever either holds keyboard focus: `:root.nav-autohide-hidden:has(.VPNav :focus-visible, .VPLocalNav :focus-visible)` clears the transform of both in `docs/.vitepress/theme/Layout.vue`. `src/site-mobile-nav-autohide-focus-visible.test.ts` (real browser, 390 px, built site) taps the hamburger and the theme switch then scrolls down (the bar hides) and Tabs into the local nav (both bars shown, no gap); it fails with the old `:focus-within` rule. `docs/development.md` describes the change; README and the bundled skill do not describe the site's header.
- site (ENG-SITE-FOOTER-LINK-NAVIGATES): a click on the footer's author link scrolled the landing to the top instead of opening https://axgord.github.io/. The link is same-origin with the site (axgord.github.io), so VitePress's client router took it for an in-site route outside the base and did not navigate. The link now carries `target="_self"` (the router skips any link with a `target` attribute; the tab stays the same) in `themeConfig.footer.copyright` of `docs/.vitepress/config.mts`. `src/site-footer-link-navigates.test.ts` serves the built site to a real browser as https://axgord.github.io/claude-workflow/ (requests fulfilled from the local build), clicks the link and asserts the location is https://axgord.github.io/ and that it was requested; `src/site-footer-author.test.ts` pins the config text with the attribute. README and the bundled skill do not describe the site's footer.
- pool gate (ENG-POOL-GATE-DENY-SURVIVES-TARGET-CONFIG): the hook resolved ONE config, the nearest `pool-gate.yaml` from the command's directory, so a command that `cd`s into a worktree carrying its own config (`cd /tmp/wf-X && git stash list`) was judged only by the target's config and the session project's `deny` rules (no `git stash`, no hand-run gates) no longer applied: a denied command passed. `poolGateOutput` now also finds the config of the call's cwd (the session's project) and, when it is a different file from the target's, evaluates its `deny` rules too; a deny in either config refuses. The two lookups fail alone: a malformed `pool-gate.yaml` on one side is ignored with one stderr line and the other side still applies (a malformed caller config leaves the target's rules, a malformed target config leaves the caller's denies and routes as a target with no config). Heavy-command routing and its share keep following the target's config alone (the caller's heavy rules are never applied to a target that has its own). `src/pool-gate-deny-survives-target.test.ts` pins it, both malformed-config directions included (red with the caller check removed: `cd <target> && git stash list` from the caller passes) and `src/pool-gate.test.ts`'s old case that a target's config wins over the caller's deny is now two: target routes, caller denies. `docs/action-states.md`, `docs/development.md` and the bundled `workflow-authoring` skill say so; README does not describe the gate.
- site (ENG-SITE-AUTOHIDE-FOCUS-REVEAL): below 960 px, after a scroll down had hidden the bar, Tab-focusing a link inside it left the focused element off-screen (measured at 390 px: the nav's top -64 with a nav link focused). `docs/.vitepress/theme/Layout.vue` now has `:root.nav-autohide-hidden .Layout .VPNav:focus-within` and `.VPLocalNav:focus-within` with `transform: none` inside the below-960 px block, so a bar holding focus is shown whatever the scroll said, with its normal transition; the scroll listener is unchanged and from 960 px nothing moves. `src/site-mobile-nav-autohide-focus.test.ts` (new) pins the rule's source and, in a real browser at 390 px on `/` and `/getting-started`, that after a scroll down a focused nav link leaves the nav's top at 0 with the link on-screen, and on `/getting-started` that the focused local nav button leaves the local nav right under the bar (red with the rule or its local-nav half removed). `docs/development.md` describes it; README and the bundled skill do not describe the site. Checked in headless Chromium only (Firefox and WebKit not run).
- engine-task and batch-task (ENG-UNAMENDED-FIXES-GUARD-FOLLOWUPS): two follow-ups of ENG-WORK-CHECK-REFUSES-UNAMENDED-AFTER-FIXES. (1) `work_check` of `.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml` printed the review in its refusal through `head -c 8000`, but the verdict and its FIXES list close the review, so a review over 8000 bytes lost the list the worker needed; it now prints the last 8000 bytes (`tail -c`). (2) The bundled `templates/batch-task.yaml`, which users run, had no such guard: its `work_check` passed a branch the worker never amended after a FIXES. It now reads the context `review_text` (the reviewer's one-line handback, set by the `review` prompt together with `reviewed_sha`, the sha of the worker's latest DONE), and when its verdict is not MERGE and `batch/<task>` is still `reviewed_sha` it exits 3 with the review text, so `work_unverified` shows the cause to send the worker; the `work` prompt says a fix round sends the cause first and never `check`s on entering. `src/work-check-unamended-fixes-followups.test.ts` (new) pins the long-review tail, the batch-task refusal, the accepted amended, MERGE and first-round cases, the short-sha case of both workflows and the prompt wording; both guards compare resolved commits (`git rev-parse --verify -q "$REVIEWED^{commit}"`), not strings, because drivers store abbreviated shas in `reviewed_sha` and a string compare failed open; the long-review, refusal and short-sha cases were red before. README and the bundled SKILL do not describe `work_check`; `docs/development.md` does.
- site (ENG-SITE-SOCIAL-PREVIEW): a link to https://axgord.github.io/claude-workflow/ in a social network or a messenger showed no preview image (the GitHub repository link does), because the site had no Open Graph or Twitter card tags and did not serve its social preview. (1) `scripts/render-banner.mjs` (`npm run docs:banner`) now also writes the 1280x640 social preview to `docs/public/og.png` (a `also` list on the variant: the same bytes as `docs/social-preview.png`, so a logo change reaches both and a stale image is caught by the existing stamp test). (2) `docs/.vitepress/config.mts` adds to every page's head `og:type`, `og:site_name`, `og:image` (an absolute https url) with `og:image:width`, `og:image:height` and `og:image:alt`, `twitter:card` `summary_large_image` and `twitter:image`, and `transformHead` adds each page's own `og:title`, `og:description` (its frontmatter description; the landing's is its hero), `og:url`, `twitter:title` and `twitter:description` (VitePress already writes `<meta name="description">`). `src/site-social-preview.test.ts` pins, on the built site, the tags on the landing and on a doc page with absolute urls, and that `og.png` is served with HTTP 200 as the social preview's bytes; red with `transformHead`, the card tag, the generator's `also` or `og.png` removed. `docs/development.md` documents it and names the validators to try after a deploy (opengraph.xyz, the LinkedIn Post Inspector). README and the bundled skill do not describe the site's head.
- readme (ENG-README-BANNER-LINKS-SITE): the README's top banner is a link to https://axgord.github.io/claude-workflow/, so a click on it opens the site on GitHub and on npm (alt text and width kept; no script generates that block). `src/site-banner.test.ts` pins the wrapped markup; red with the `<a>` removed. The bundled skill does not describe the README.
- site (ENG-SITE-FOOTER-AUTHOR): the site's footer names the author: `themeConfig.footer.copyright` in `docs/.vitepress/config.mts` is now `© 2026 <a href="https://axgord.github.io/">Alexander Gordeyko</a>` (VitePress renders it as HTML) instead of the bare project name, and VitePress's default rule is kept, the footer shows only on pages without a sidebar (the landing, not the doc pages). `src/site-footer-author.test.ts` pins the config text and, in a real browser on the built site, that the author link is visible, inside the viewport, hit by a click at its centre and not the page's background colour on the landing at 1280 and 390 px, light and dark, and that a doc page with a sidebar keeps the footer hidden (`display: none`). README and the bundled skill do not describe the site's footer.
- site (ENG-SITE-MOBILE-NAV-AUTOHIDE): below 960 px the header hides on a scroll down and shows again on any scroll up, on the landing and on the doc pages (the bar was fixed on the landing only, and in the flow on doc pages). `Layout.vue` owns the one passive, `requestAnimationFrame`-throttled scroll listener and flips `nav-autohide-hidden` on `<html>`; the bar is always shown at the page top, a move under 10 px decides nothing, and an open nav screen or sidebar keeps it shown. On doc pages the bar is fixed too (the layout pads under it) and VitePress's second bar, the local nav (`Menu` / `On this page`), sticks right under it and hides and shows with it, so no gap is left. Both move with `transform: translateY`, with no animation under `prefers-reduced-motion`; an anchor jump keeps VitePress's 134 px scroll offset, below both bars. From 960 px nothing changes. `src/site-mobile-nav-autohide.test.ts` runs the built site in a real browser: at 390 px on `/` and `/getting-started` the bars are off-screen after a scroll down and back, nav at top 0 and local nav under it, after a 30 px scroll up; a few px do not flip it; the top shows it; an open menu keeps it; reduced motion has no transition; an anchor lands below the bars; at 1280 px nothing moves; source pins fix the single passive rAF listener and the below-960 px scope.
- dashboard map and site replay (ENG-MAP-STATE-LABEL-OVERLAP): two state labels of a band could be drawn over each other (`blocked` and `project_gate_rebased` of `engine-task` in the site's demo, and the same layout feeds the live dashboard): a label is centred on its station, but a column was sized only for its own label and each label was cut to its own column, so a long name beside a short column spilled over the neighbour. A column now also makes room for the next station's name, and a label is cut (the whole name stays its tooltip) to the narrower of the two gaps beside its station, so the halves of two neighbours always fit in the gap between them. `src/map-state-label-overlap.test.ts` lays out the site's replay (`docs/public/replay/anyparse.json`) with the site's own fold and layout, at the site's 1280 px and with the room unbounded, and asserts no two station label boxes of any band intersect.
- engine-task (ENG-GATE-LEASE-ESLINT-AND-ASK): the gate held what it did not use and one `npm run check` ran outside any lease. `project_gate` and `project_gate_rebased` leased `threads: { min: 4, max: budget }` and held 13 of 14 (the pool's reading of that label over 145 leases: held 4.9, used 1.9) while `npm run check` used what its tools chose: vitest took about cpus-1 workers whatever `WF_THREADS` said, `check:ci` ran it under `env -i`, which dropped the grant, and `eslint . -f json` (one process, about 1.8 cores, no thread knob) ran bare in the worker's own `npm run check` (an unleased eslint beside the pool, seen at load ~50 on 2026-11-17). Fixed in three parts. (1) `vitest.config.ts` sets `maxWorkers` to `WF_THREADS` when it is a positive integer (unset: vitest's own default) and `scripts/check-ci.sh` carries it through its emptied environment. (2) One lease over the serial pipeline cannot match held to used, measured with `resource.getrusage` per step on a host at load 25 to 30 (cores = CPU seconds over wall seconds): tsc, biome and ast-grep take 0.2 to 0.3 s, eslint 8 to 10 s at 2.0 cores, vitest 5.7 cores at 8 workers, 4.75 at 6, 2.75 at 4 (wall 67, 72 and 120 s), `check:ci` 3.2 to 4.6, and the whole check 4.6 cores at 8 workers (146 s, held 8: 57% used), 4.4 uncapped (147 s), 2.7 at 4 workers (219 s), so beyond 6 workers the wall bought nothing. `npm run check` is now `check:static` (tsc, biome, eslint, ast-grep) then `check:tests` (vitest, the pane tests, `check:ci`), and `scripts/check-leased.sh` takes a 2-thread lease for the first and a 4-6 one for the second, one after the other (never nested: a lease held around a second `wf-run` waits on itself, and a full pool of such gates would never drain), plain `npm run check` when `WF_THREADS` is already set, one 4-6 lease for a tree on an older base that has no halves. After, the test half holds 6 and uses 4.5 (76%), the static half holds 2 and uses 2, the whole run 144 s. (3) `project_gate` and `project_gate_rebased` hold no `resource:` of their own and run `"$CHECK"` (the main checkout's `scripts/check-leased.sh`), and the `work` and `rebase_fix` prompts tell the worker to run that script from its worktree instead of a bare `npm run check`. `src/gate-vitest-honors-grant.test.ts` pins the config, the carry-over, the two halves, the gates (no `resource:`, the script), both prompts and the script's leases against a fake `wf-run`; `src/typescript7-toolchain.test.ts` follows the tsc 7 line to `check:static`. `docs/development.md`, `docs/action-states.md` and the workflow-authoring skill say that `env -i` drops the grant, that a range's `max` comes from a held-against-used reading and that a serial pipeline takes a lease per half.
- dashboard (ENG-DASHBOARD-FAVICON-TITLE): the web dashboard's tab had no icon (the site has one) and was still titled `MCP Workflow Engine`, while the site and the brand say `Claude Workflow`. The brand's data and drawings moved from `scripts/render-banner.mjs` into `scripts/brand.mjs` (no dependencies: `NAME`, the palettes, `logoMark`, `logoSvg`, `faviconSvg`; render-banner re-exports them, `docs/.vitepress/config.mts` takes `NAME` from it), and the dashboard (`src/dashboard.ts`) serves `/favicon.svg` (`image/svg+xml`) from `faviconSvg()`, the function that writes `docs/public/favicon.svg`, so a logo change reaches both and no copy exists (`docs/` is not in the package, `scripts/` is). `dashboard/index.html` has `<link rel="icon" href="/favicon.svg">` and `{{BRAND}} · Dashboard` as its title (the heading is `{{BRAND}}` too); the page route fills the placeholder from `NAME` for `/`, `/index.html` and every view path. `src/dashboard-favicon-title.test.ts` (new) pins the title, the heading, the icon link, the route's content type and that its body equals both the file and `faviconSvg()`, and that the config and render-banner import the one module (both routes were red before); `src/dashboard.test.ts` expects the new title. README and the bundled skill do not describe the tab; `docs/dashboard.md` and `docs/development.md` do. Checked in headless Chromium 159 (playwright; Firefox and WebKit not run) against the built dashboard: the tab title reads `Claude Workflow · Dashboard`, the heading `Claude Workflow`, the page's `<link rel="icon">` resolves to `/favicon.svg` of type `image/svg+xml`, and the served icon rendered in both colour schemes shows the logo; headless Chromium does not request a tab icon itself, so the icon is shown by loading the route, not by a tab strip.
- engine-task (ENG-LAND-REBASE-CONFLICT-AUTO-FIX): a land whose rebase onto a moved master conflicted (`project_rebase` exit 1) went to `land_failed` and waited for the parent, whose only answer was the one that state's prompt already named, `rebase_fix` (three times on 2026-11-17: a driver hand-back, a parent turn and a resume each). `project_rebase` now routes exit 1 to a new exec state `rebase_conflict`, which passes on to `rebase_fix` with no parent and no driver choice; its `max_visits: 2` is the bound per task, so the third conflict follows `on_exhausted` to `land_failed` as before, and so does any other land failure (build red, take-back, timeout, dirty main). `land_failed` no longer offers `rebase_fix` (past the bound its answer is `fix` or `close`). `rebase_fix`'s verify also drops `merge-base --is-ancestor master <branch>`: when master moved again with no conflict, the land's own rebase takes the lag (`project_rebase`, then `project_gate_rebased`), and only a new conflict comes back, counted against the same two rounds. `src/land-rebase-conflict-auto-fix.test.ts` runs the real engine over the real `engine-task.yaml` and a sandbox git repo: two conflicts reach `rebase_fix`, the third `land_failed`; a clean lag rebases in the land; the routing keys are pinned.
- engine-task (ENG-POOL-GATE-CONFIG-ENGINE-REPO): a worker's or a reviewer's own `npx vitest run src/<file>.test.ts`, `vitest run --maxWorkers=1` and `npm run check` in a `/tmp/wf-*` worktree ran with no pool lease (one at 113% CPU beside an idle pool, which broke threads granted = threads used): the pool gate hook reads `.claude/workflows/pool-gate.yaml` from the call's cwd up to the git root and the engine repo had none, so only the workflow's exec states (`repro_check`, `project_gate`, `project_gate_rebased`) took leases. The engine repo now tracks its own `.claude/workflows/pool-gate.yaml` (`.gitignore` un-ignores it beside `engine-task.yaml`, so every worktree has it). It leases every vitest form (`npx`, `npm exec`, `node_modules/.bin`, bare), `npm test`, `npm run check`, `npm run check:ci` and `scripts/check-ci.sh`, sized by use: `--maxWorkers=N` (1, 2, 4, 8) gets N threads, one named test file 1, two files 2, the rest `4-14`; each passes its share as `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=$WF_THREADS` (vitest's default is cpus minus one, so a smaller lease would still run 15 workers), and `scripts/check-ci.sh` forwards that one variable through its `env -i`. The gate now resolves the config for the directory the command runs in: a worker's call is `cd /tmp/wf-X && npx vitest run …` while the call's cwd is the caller's project, so the config came from the wrong repo (`commandDir` in `src/pool-gate.ts`: the cwd moved by leading `cd <dir>` segments joined by `&&`, `;` or a newline; a directory with no config (outside a git repo, or a repo without one) keeps the call cwd's config, so its leases and denies still apply (`cd /tmp && pkill node` is denied as before), `hooks/pool-gate.sh` sends a command starting with `cd` to node instead of exiting on the payload cwd's missing config; the read-only gate shares the path). A command under `wf-run` or with `WF_THREADS` set is not leased again; tsc, lint, scan and build (about a second) take none. Pins: `src/pool-gate-engine-repo.test.ts` (32 cases: the hook applied to a payload with an engine worktree's cwd routes each form through `wf-run` with the sized threads; no double lease; `.gitignore` does not ignore the file; `check-ci.sh` carries the variable; a payload cwd in a different git repo whose own `pool-gate.yaml` has no vitest rules plus `cd <worktree> && npx vitest run src/a.test.ts` is wrapped with `--threads 1`, red without `commandDir`; red with the yaml, the `.gitignore` line or the script line removed), `src/pool-gate.test.ts` (the real hook script with a leading `cd`: a target's own config wins; a repo without one and a directory outside any repo keep the caller's lease and deny) and `src/test-helpers.ts` `spawnChild` drops `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS` from a child that names `--maxWorkers` (the variable beats the flag: the child-vitest tests saw 7 and 8 workers under a leased `npm run check`; pinned in the first file), and `src/repro-check-base-relative.test.ts` (the .gitignore pin now expects `pool-gate.yaml` tracked and another file under `.claude/workflows/` ignored). The bundled `workflow-authoring` skill and `docs/action-states.md` now say where the config is looked up from; README does not describe it.
- site (ENG-SITE-MOBILE-MENU-DOCS-EXPANDED): in the hamburger menu (below 768 px) the Docs group was collapsed and needed a tap to show its pages. It is now always expanded: a `Docs` link to `/getting-started` with every page's link under it, no toggle (`DocsNav.vue`'s `screenMenu` branch renders VitePress's own `VPNavScreenMenuLink` and `VPNavScreenMenuGroupLink` instead of its collapsible `VPNavScreenMenuGroup`; the desktop Docs item is unchanged). `src/site-docs-nav-clickable.test.ts` replaces its 375 px case (which clicked the group open) with a 390 px one: open the menu, every link visible with no click on Docs, no collapsible group, and `Docs` navigates; it failed before.
- engine-task (ENG-REVIEW-VISITS-VERIFY-ONLY-FIX): a fix round that changed no code still entered `review`, so once its `max_visits` was spent the engine rerouted it to `blocked` and the parent read the diff to take `parent_edited` (a verify-only FIXES item answered by a CHANGELOG line, ENG-SITE-TERMINAL-WINDOW-BUTTONS 68fbe36 vs the reviewed 6459041; a `rebase_fix` after MERGE that kept only the conflicted hunk, ENG-SITE-THEME-SWITCH-TRANSITION d11ae36 vs e483293). `project_gate` now routes through a new `review_route` (`.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml`): the reviewed commit's patch and HEAD's patch over their own parents are compared with the prose paths (CHANGELOG.md, README.md, `docs/**/*.md`) left out, the site's own code under docs/ (`.vitepress/`, `.vue`, scripts) staying code as in `scripts/review-class.sh`, and only the +/- lines read (hunk headers, index lines and context move with a rebase). Equal code patches after a MERGE go to `project_land` (a `breaking: true` line to `breaking_ready`); after FIXES they go to a new `review_verify` (the same reviewer, asked only whether the amendment answers its own list; `max_visits: 2`, not a `review` visit). A `rebase_fix` after MERGE whose resolution changed code (HEAD's parent is no longer the reviewed commit's) goes to `review_verify` too, with the range-diff of the two patches named; any other round with a code change, or no review yet, still enters `review` and is bounded by its `max_visits`. `src/review-visits-verify-only-fix.test.ts` runs the real engine over the real workflow (gates and land stubbed) and pins the verify-only round, a real `git rebase master` that conflicts only in CHANGELOG.md while master moved a code line above the slice's hunk, a rebase resolution that changed code, the theme's code under docs/ staying reviewed, the code round that stays `blocked`, and the breaking line.
- site (ENG-SITE-MOBILE-NAV-PINNED): ENG-SITE-HERO-MOBILE-NO-FADE left the landing's bar below 960 px as VitePress's own `.VPNav`, which is `position: relative`, so on a phone it scrolled off with the page (user: the header scrolls with the page on the mobile version; measured at 390x844 on the deployed site: after `scrollTo(0, 600)` the `.VPNav` top was -600). `docs/.vitepress/theme/Hero.vue` now makes the bar `position: fixed` at every width under `.Layout.has-hero` (it stays VitePress's opaque bar below 960 px, the content scrolls under it) and gives `.VPContent` a top padding of `--vp-nav-height` below 960 px (VitePress adds it only from 960 px), so the first screen starts below the bar with no overlap. Review fix: the landing is `layout: home`, so VitePress classes the bar `home` and paints its background only for other pages and from 960 px (measured rgba(0,0,0,0) at 390 and 800 px after a 600 px scroll, light and dark), so the pinned bar was see-through; `.Layout.has-hero .VPNavBar` now has `background-color: var(--vp-nav-bg-color)` and its divider line `var(--vp-c-gutter)`, outside the desktop block, and from 960 px the `!important` transparent overrides still win (the desktop pins and tests are green). The no-fade and no-enlarged-title behaviour of ENG-SITE-HERO-MOBILE-NO-FADE is kept. Pins: `src/site-mobile-nav-pinned.test.ts` (new, runs whenever a browser is installed) scrolls to 600 px at 390 and 800 px and asserts the nav's top is 0 and its bottom equals `--vp-nav-height`, and the hero starts below the bar and, at 390 and 800 px, light and dark, that the bar's computed background equals `--vp-nav-bg-color` with alpha 1 (red on the previous tree: top -600 and a transparent background); `src/site-hero.test.ts` pins the two rules before the desktop block. `docs/development.md` describes it; Checked in the built site at 390 and 800 px (the tests serve a fresh `vitepress build` of docs/), in headless Chromium only (Firefox and WebKit not run); the deployed site is for the parent to check after the push. README and the bundled skill do not describe the site's landing.
- site (ENG-SITE-HERO-MOBILE-NO-FADE): since ENG-SITE-HERO-LAYOUT-PASS the landing's tagline, buttons, chips and diagram dissolved over 48 px of scroll before the bar, and on a phone, where the diagram stacks under the text, the diagram faded early and left part of the page blank (user: what scrolls away by itself needs no dissolving; the wanted part is the rearrangement). (1) No fade at any width (`docs/.vitepress/theme/Hero.vue`): the `hero-fade` class, `--hero-fade`, the `view()` scroll-driven animation, its keyframes and the IntersectionObserver fallback are gone; the content scrolls under the opaque bar as plain content, cut by the bar's line with no soft edge (0 px; a soft edge was optional and its only effect would be a faint band at the bar). (2) The merge of the nav and the hero (the enlarged title that condenses into the bar, the condensing search, the transparent bar fixed over the hero, the hero's room for the brand) is for the desktop layout only, from 960 px, the width at which the hero becomes two columns; below it the hero stacks and the bar is VitePress's own (not fixed, scrolls away with the page, plain title), so the phone has no enlarged title (the 1.6 and 2 scales and the 88 px offset are gone). All of the bar's rules and animations sit in one `@media (min-width: 960px)` block. (3) Tests: `src/site-hero-no-fade.test.ts` (new, runs whenever a browser is installed) scrolls in 16 px steps at 375, 414, 768, 960 and 1440 px and asserts opacity 1 for every tagline, button, chip and diagram with any part in the viewport (red on the previous tree: tagline 0.67 to 0 and a button 0.67 at 768 px); `src/site-hero.test.ts` pins that no fade class, variable, keyframes, `view()` timeline or observer margin remains and that every bar rule is inside the desktop block; the straddling-the-bar assertion of `src/site-hero-layout.test.ts` is gone (text under an opaque bar is the intended picture) and `src/site-hero-320.test.ts` now covers VitePress's plain title at 320 px; `src/site-scene-install-align.test.ts` now asserts (it only logged before) that the scene stage, the install block and the feature grid have the hero's left and right edges within 1 px at 375, 640, 768, 960, 1215, 1280, 1440 and 1920 px. Looked at in headless Chromium mid-scroll at 375, 768 and 1440 px: the diagram stays fully drawn until it leaves the viewport; the desktop condensed bar is as before. `docs/development.md` describes it; README and the bundled skill do not describe the site's landing. Not checked in Firefox or Safari; the deployed site is for the parent to check after the push.
- engine-task (ENG-WORKER-NO-SHARED-BROWSER-INSTALL): a worker's `npx playwright install chromium` from its worktree replaced chromium in the machine-wide `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright` while another slice's gate was launching it (`src/site-hero-320.test.ts`: no chromium, a red gate, a NEEDS-PARENT round). The engine-task worker prompt now forbids a bare `playwright install` and prescribes `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=<worktree>-browsers npx playwright install <browser>` (tests run with the same variable), so the shared cache is never written; `src/worker-browser-install-isolated.test.ts` pins the prompt text and, by `--dry-run`, that the prescribed command targets only the per-worktree directory. A second case: another slice's probe script launched firefox and webkit outside the pool and was orphaned (ppid 1) for an hour, and WebKit's Playwright.app put a blank window on the desktop despite `--headless`. The prompt now asks that a worker's browser launch runs under a lease, `<engine checkout>/scripts/wf-run --threads 1 -- <cmd>` (the prompt carries that path as `{{context.cwd}}/scripts/wf-run`, never a bare `wf-run` an agent's shell may not resolve), closes the browser in a `finally` and leaves no process behind, and `work_check` refuses (exit 3, listing the pids, never killing) a tree where a process reparented to init has the worktree as its cwd; the same test spawns such an orphan and pins the refusal. The `cleanup` exec removes the per-worktree `<worktree>-browsers` directory itself (the cleanup exec, not the worker, under the same exact-path guard that admits only `/tmp/wf-<TASK>` or `/private/tmp/wf-<TASK>`), so no browsers are left behind after a land; a cleanup test pins it.
- site (ENG-SITE-FEATURE-GRID-ALIGN): VitePress's own feature grid (the cards under the install block) kept its own gutters (24, 48 and 64 px from 640 and 960 px) and 1152 px cap, so at 640 to 1215 px its cards sat 16 to 32 px inside the hero, the replay's stage and the install block (at 640 px the cards at 48 to 592 px, the hero at 24 to 616; at 960 px 64 to 896 against 32 to 928). `docs/.vitepress/theme/Hero.vue` now overrides the grid's container from the theme (selectors under the page class `.Layout.has-hero`, no fork of VitePress): padding 24 px, 32 px from 768 px, and `max-width: var(--hero-content-w)`, so the four blocks share their left and right edges at 375, 640, 768, 960, 1215, 1280, 1440 and 1920 px. The CSS comments in `Hero.vue`, `Scene.vue` and `Install.vue` no longer say the blocks follow the feature grid's edges: they name the hero's and the bar's, and say the grid is set to the same. `src/site-feature-grid-align.test.ts` (new, runs whenever a browser is installed) measures the hero, the stage, the install block and the cards in a real browser at those eight widths; four (640, 768, 960, 1215 px) were red before the change. Looked at in headless Chromium (full-page screenshots at the eight widths): one column of edges.
- site (ENG-SITE-SCENE-INSTALL-ALIGN): after the hero and the bar shared `--hero-content-w` the landing still did not read as one block: the replay's stage (`Scene.vue`) and the install block (`Install.vue`) were capped at 1280 px with a 16 px gutter on a phone, so on a wide screen they stuck out 40 px past the hero on each side (stage at 24 to 1256 px at 1280 px, the hero at 64 to 1216) and at 375 px they were 8 px wider than it. Both now take `--hero-content-w` plus the hero's own gutters (24 px below 768 px, 32 px from there, as `Hero.vue`), so their left and right edges are the hero's at 375, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560 px (the bar's and the feature grid's too from 1280 px; the feature grid's gutter below 1280 px is ENG-SITE-FEATURE-GRID-ALIGN's). `src/site-scene-install-align.test.ts` (new, runs whenever a browser is installed) pins it; every case (7) was red before the change. Looked at in headless Chromium (full-page screenshots at 375, 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560 px): the stage, the transport and the install block share the hero's edges.
- engine-task (ENG-WORK-CHECK-REFUSES-UNAMENDED-AFTER-FIXES): after a review FIXES the haiku driver took `check` at `work` without sending the list to the worker, and `work_check` and the gate passed on the unchanged commit (HEAD == `reviewed_sha`), so the session spent a review visit with nothing fixed. `work_check` (`.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml`) now reads the review's verdict the way `review_filed` does and, when a review text exists, its verdict is anything but MERGE (a non-canonical line such as `Verdict: FIXES` or `**FIXES:**` goes to a fix round in `review_filed` too) and HEAD is still `reviewed_sha`, exits 3 with a line telling the driver to send the worker the list first, followed by the review text itself (up to 8000 bytes), because `work_unready` forwards only that stdout to the worker. The `work` prompt's fix-round wording could be read as `check` straight away on entering the state; it now says the cause goes to the worker BEFORE anything else, never `check` on entering, and `check` only on the worker's DONE for this round. `src/work-check-unamended-fixes.test.ts` (new) pins the refusal (canonical and non-canonical verdict lines), the list in the refusal's stdout, the accepted amended tree, the accepted MERGE and first-round cases and the prompt wording; the refusal case was red before. README and the bundled skill do not describe the engine's own slice workflow; `docs/development.md` does.
- site (ENG-SITE-THEME-SWITCH-TRANSITION): the site's light/dark switch flipped the whole page in one frame. `docs/.vitepress/theme/Layout.vue` (new; `index.ts` mounts it in place of `DefaultTheme.Layout`, the slots pass through) provides VitePress's `toggle-appearance` and flips `isDark` inside `document.startViewTransition` (after a `nextTick`, the documented recipe), so the old page cross-fades into the new one over 300 ms, the travel time of the switch's own knob (0.25 s); a circular reveal from the switch was not chosen, the cross-fade is the plainest reading of "one theme turns into the other while the knob moves". The demos (map, terminal window, status line, pool strip) follow `html.dark` in CSS only, so they change inside the same transition with no second flash. No transition where `startViewTransition` is missing or under `prefers-reduced-motion`: the plain flip. `src/site-theme-switch-transition.test.ts` (new) pins the source and, in headless Chromium, pauses the transition and captures the page at 0, 100, 200 and 300 ms (luminance strictly falling between the themes), the demos against a page loaded dark, and the reduced-motion and no-API flips; its transition cases were red with the provider removed. The Firefox and WebKit cases run when playwright's own builds are installed and skip otherwise; run against Firefox 159 and WebKit 27.2 (both have `startViewTransition`), the switch flips both ways under the real transition with no page error, and the no-API case covers the fallback.
- site (ENG-SITE-DOCS-NAV-CLICKABLE): a click on the nav's `Docs` did nothing (VitePress's dropdown is a button that only opens its menu): it is now a link to `/getting-started`, active on every docs page, with the page list beside it, opening on hover, on keyboard focus and on a click, Enter or tap of its caret (the caret only opens, so a click on it while hover already opened the list keeps it open; Esc, a tap outside, or the pointer or focus leaving closes it), and the hamburger screen still lists every page. VitePress has no link-plus-menu nav item, so this is a custom one (`docs/.vitepress/theme/DocsNav.vue`, `component: "DocsNav"` in `config.mts`; the desktop bar's flyout is its own, the mobile group is VitePress's). `src/site-docs-nav-clickable.test.ts` (new) pins the click, the hover list, the active state, the 375 px menu, and the caret (a tap at 1024 px, a click after hover, Enter after focus, Esc) in a real browser; its click case was red before. The landing's hero also sat at the layout's far edges on a wide screen (text at the left, diagram at the right, an empty middle): the hero and the nav bar now share `--hero-content-w` (the feature grid's 1152 px inside `--vp-layout-max-width`), centred, so the logo, the hero, the diagram and the feature cards share their edges at 1280, 1440, 1920 and 2560 px, and nothing changes below 1216 px. `src/site-hero-content-width.test.ts` (new) pins it, red before; `src/site-hero.test.ts` pins the shared width and the `DocsNav` item.
- site (ENG-SITE-HERO-TEST-HELPERS-SHARE): the real-browser site tests share one helper module, `src/site-browser-helpers.ts` (the repo root, the playwright browser lookup, and a server of the built docs site, whose builds take a cross-process lock: VitePress shares one `.temp` under the docs, so two builds at once broke each other; a lock left by a dead process, or by a crash before its pid was written, is taken over), instead of each copying it: `src/site-hero-320.test.ts` and `src/site-hero-layout.test.ts` no longer hold their own copies of the lookup and server, and `src/site-graph-no-inner-scroll.test.ts` takes the lookup from it, and `src/site-map-replay.test.ts` builds through it. `writeSiteWorkflows` now writes `docs/public/workflows.json` atomically (a temp file renamed onto it), so a nested vitest run's setup cannot hand another test file a cut file. `src/site-hero-320.test.ts` no longer prints a `console.log` line per run. `src/site-hero-320-quiet.test.ts` (new) pins both: the 320 px test run prints no `stdout |` block, and no `src/site-*.test.ts` defines its own `browserPath`, built-site server or docs build (`src/site-build-lock.test.ts` pins the lock; `src/site-generated-on-rebase.test.ts` pins the atomic write); it was red before the change.
- site (ENG-SITE-HERO-320-TITLE-OVERFLOW): at 320 px the landing's hero title (the nav's own title at scale 2, scroll top) was 331 px wide from a 24 px gutter, so its name ran to 355 px past the 320 px viewport. `--hero-brand-s` is 1.6 below 380 px (`docs/.vitepress/theme/Hero.vue`), which puts the title's right edge at 289 px; the scale from 2 to 1.6 changes nothing at 380 px and up. `src/site-hero-320.test.ts` (new, runs whenever a browser is installed, normal and reduced motion) pins the name's and title's right edges and the page's scroll width at 320 px; it was red before the change. The scroll-sampling `src/site-hero-layout.test.ts` now also samples 320 px.
- site (ENG-SITE-STATUSLINE-POOLDEMO-LIGHT): after ENG-LIGHT-TERMINAL-PANE-AND-SITE the landing's window and the dashboard demos were white under the light site theme, but the status-line demo (`StatusLine.vue`) and the Pool page's demo (`PoolDemo.vue`, with `PoolStrip.vue`'s `--ps-*` dark defaults) stayed dark. The terminal chrome palette moved out of `TerminalWindow.vue` into `docs/.vitepress/theme/terminal-palette.css` (imported once by `index.ts`; `--cc-*` plus an opaque `--cc-panel`), set on `.cc-window` and on the new `.term-surface`; both demos are `term-surface term-follows-site`, so their box, border and text follow `html.dark` and the pane inside already used the `--tc-*` palette; `PoolStrip` gets a light `--ps-*` set under `html:not(.dark) .term-follows-site` built from the same `--cc-*` variables (its dark defaults and the dashboard's `.dash` tokens unchanged, monospace font kept). Pinned in `src/site-terminal-light.test.ts` (one palette file, light and dark sets with the same names, no component redefines `--cc-*`; both demos are surfaces and hold no colour literal; the strip's three sets share their names). Looked at in Chrome (built site, previewed): the Pool page's demo and the status-line demo are white with dark text, a readable light strip and the pane's light tones under the light theme, and the dark panel unchanged under the dark one; the landing's window stays white under the light theme. README and the bundled skill do not describe these demos' colours.
- site (ENG-SITE-TERMINAL-WINDOW-BUTTONS): the three dots of the landing's Claude Code window were painted and did nothing, so a reader who clicked them to see the map under the window found them broken. They are now real `<button>`s (named by `aria-label`, reachable by keyboard) in `TerminalWindow.vue`: red (close) and yellow (minimise) hide the window into the Terminal button through the same `shared.terminal` flag the button and the `T` key flip (now one `setTerminal` of `replay-store.ts`), with a short shrink-and-fade toward the button (a `<Transition>` on the `v-show`, plain under `prefers-reduced-motion`); green and a double-click on the title bar (`toggleZoom`, `shared.zoom`) make the window fill the whole stage and back, and `Esc` leaves the zoom; hiding the window ends a zoom, so it returns in place. The macOS glyphs (`×`, `−`, `⤢`/`⤡`) show while the pointer is over the group or a button has focus. Pinned in `src/site-map-replay.test.ts` (the buttons, the shared switch, `Esc`, the transition and reduced motion in the sources; the zoom reset on hide by running the store's functions). `docs/dashboard.md` and `docs/development.md` describe the buttons. Looked at in a browser (vitepress dev, headless Chromium, 1400x1000): the window normal with the glyphs showing on hover in both site themes, hidden by a real click on red (dark) and on yellow (light) with the Terminal button bringing it back, and zoomed by a real click on green in both themes (the window's box matches the stage frame); Esc, a title-bar double-click and the T key were driven by dispatched events, not by a real pointer, and the minimise animation itself was not watched frame by frame.
- plan pane + site (ENG-LIGHT-TERMINAL-PANE-AND-SITE): the pane coloured its text with ANSI names (`cyan`, `cyanBright`, `yellow`, `magenta`, ...) and one fixed hex orange, which a terminal profile maps to its own palette, so on a white terminal the bright and yellow tones washed out; and the site's Claude Code window was dark in both site themes. The mod API (`Color` of the `claude-code` types) accepts a theme key, which follows the person's Claude Code theme (dark, light, colourblind, ANSI), so the pane now names every colour by a theme key through one table, `TONE` in `hooks/pane-view.ts` (`success`, `error`, `warning`, `suggestion`, `claude` for a working session and a high priority, `inactive`, `remember`, `autoAccept`): the theme chooses each for its own ground. On the site `TerminalWindow.vue` is a white terminal (light chrome, light glass, `--cc-*`) under `html:not(.dark)` and `PaneView.vue` carries a light and a dark `--tc-<key>` palette, so the replayed conversation lines and the pane switch with the site's theme; the status line and the Pool page keep their dark panel. Pinned by `src/site-terminal-light.test.ts` (no ANSI name or hex in a pane color; every color the pane draws on every tab, in the conversation and in the band is a `TONE` value; both palettes define every key and differ per key; the window's two variable sets match); `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` and `src/site-map-replay.test.ts` follow the new keys. The pane could not be screenshot in a real light terminal from a headless worker: the theme-key route rests on the API's contract, not on a picture. The bundled skill does not describe the pane's colours.
- site (ENG-SITE-HERO-LAYOUT-PASS): the landing's hero and nav were laid out piece by piece (user screenshots of the first version): the diagram dominated the hero, the search floated over it aligned to nothing, the condensing title crossed the search, the hero text slid under the bar and was cut mid-glyph at its bottom edge, the chips row was cut off ('Live m…'), and 'Docs' stood alone in the nav after the de-dup. They are now ONE layout over the whole scroll range (`docs/.vitepress/theme/Hero.vue`, `docs/.vitepress/config.mts`). (1) Diagram size: three sizes (60, 70, 80% of the 848 px it filled at 1440 px) were compared beside the text column; the diagram is capped at 594 px (70%, `--hero-dg-max`): the text block (260 px) leads, the diagram is 208 px, its labels stay readable (60% made them small, 80% made it nearly as tall as the text). (2) Search, expanded: on the bar's row, 300 px wide (was ~140), its left edge on the diagram's left edge: from 960 px the hero is a grid of a left text column (at least `--hero-text-min`, 520 px) and a right diagram column, and the bar's title wrapper has the same left column, so the content row starts on that line by construction; below 960 px it sits beside the right cluster (240 px); where the row is short (a 960 to 971 px viewport leaves 376 px for the search, Docs and the menu) the search shrinks, down to its condensed width, instead of pushing the menu out past the viewport edge (review fix: the content row now has `min-width: 0` and the search `flex: 0 1 auto`). (3) Search on condense: it narrows to 150 px and slides next to the cluster (the row is the search between two free spaces that share the width by `flex-grow`, 0/1 at the top and 1/0 condensed; no measuring). The title scales down below the bar first and rises into it second (`scale` and `translate` as separate scroll-driven animations), so the big title is never in the bar's row. (4) Reflow: the buttons and chips rows wrap (`flex-wrap`) and the grid's text column is never narrower than 520 px; the cut-off chips row was not reproduced at the widths measured (320 to 1920 px), so the test pins no overflow and every chip and button inside the hero at sixteen of them instead of a fix for a known cause. (5) Nav: one Docs menu that lists all nine sidebar pages (the landing has no sidebar; the hamburger screen shows the same), so no single link is left over; GitHub is the icon beside the theme switch; Search, Docs, theme, GitHub in both states. Review addition: the bar's surface is opaque (VitePress's own colour of the current theme, complete by half the travel), its divider last, and the hero's tagline, buttons, chips and the diagram (as one unit) fade out over 48 px of travel that ends where the element's top meets the bar's bottom edge, so nothing is drawn cut at the bar's line (fallback without scroll-driven animation: an IntersectionObserver on the same line toggles `hero-faded`). - plan pane (ENG-PANE-SINGLE-TASK-VIEW): with no work queue the pane drew only the session tree, one line per session. It now draws a single-task view with no tabs (`singleTaskBody` of `hooks/pane-view.ts`): the current session (`workflow[task] @ state`, time in it, visit, the states passed, the transitions it offers), what runs under it (the linked agent with its role, model and age, child sessions, an exec in flight with its command, elapsed and timeout (late only past the timeout stretched by the pool wait the engine counted, read from the run's `.wait` file), the last exec's exit; the child sessions of every root session), the pool (`held/budget`, load, waiting, this session's own leases marked, the other holders by label) and the last three transitions. A section with nothing to show is left out; a short pane folds them in `SINGLE_FOLD_ORDER` (last transitions, other holders, pool, what runs, the current session keeps its head line), pinned in `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx`. The pool is read by the full `GET /api/pool` once per refresh (15 s) while the pane is open, never per render, never closed, and not when the queue read failed. Spend is not drawn: the engine measures it from the agents' transcripts when a session ends, so a running session has none. `SessionRow` gains `model`, `agentAt`, `exec` and `lastExec` (the `snapshot` atom's shape tag is `snap-10`); the exec's command and timeout and the agent's model come from `/api/workflows`, and an unanswered `/api/workflows` read is now asked again on the next refresh instead of waiting five minutes. A queue session keeps its tabs. Needs `/reload-plugins` (the hooks module); the engine server is unchanged. `docs/dashboard.md` describes it.

- dashboard (ENG-DASHBOARD-LIGHT-THEME): the dashboard was dark only (`dashboard/tokens.css` declared `color-scheme: dark` and one token set) while the docs site has a light theme with a switch, so a white site showed dark demos. `tokens.css` now holds a light and a dark set under the same names (the only file with colour literals; light text and every state, model, stage, difficulty and actor colour keep 4.5:1 on the panel). The page follows `prefers-color-scheme`; a System / Light / Dark switch at the right end of the tab row pins a set with `data-theme` on `<html>` and remembers it in `localStorage` (`wf-theme`, every access guarded; a `#theme-boot` script in the head applies it before the first paint); each set sets its own `color-scheme`, so the Cost tab's date inputs and every native control follow, and the head's meta is `light dark`. The site's scoped `.dash` demos (`docs/.vitepress/theme/dashboard-css.ts`) put the light set on `.dash` and the dark set on `html.dark .dash`, dropping the system query, so VitePress's own switch flips them live. No colour is computed in JS (`graph.js`, `map.js`, `scene-dom.js` and the page script read tokens only; nothing changed there). Pins in `src/dashboard-style.test.ts`: both sets name the same tokens, the contrast table, the boot script and `applyTheme` (stored, cleared, blocked storage), the scoped site CSS built from the real module; `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts` (opt-in) drives the switch and reads the computed `color-scheme` of the page and the date inputs. Also `src/test-child-vitest.test.ts` ('runs the child on one worker at a time') failed under a loaded check (`expected 2 to be less than or equal to 1`): vitest replaces a worker per file and the old one lingers a moment while its successor starts, which the check counted as a second worker. Two workers now count as live together only when they overlap for more than 2 s (`HANDOVER_MS`); `--maxWorkers=4` in the child still fails it. The home page's scene follows the site theme as a whole (review fix): the pool strip under the map (`PoolStrip.vue`) and the stage frame were hard-coded dark under the now light map; the strip is a `.dash` element reading the dashboard tokens through `--ps-*` variables (dark defaults, so the Pool page's own dark terminal panel is unchanged), the frame reads VitePress's `--vp-c-bg`, the Claude Code window stays dark in both themes with its glass nearly opaque in the light one. - tests (ENG-CI-CHILD-VITEST-WORKER-CHAIN-LINUX): CI on master was red: `src/test-child-vitest.test.ts` 'runs the child on one worker at a time' failed `expected 0 to be greater than 0` on ubuntu (and `src/test-child-spawn-cleanup.test.ts` with it, which reruns that file as a child). The test found the inner vitest's workers by walking ppid through the probe's records, which hold node processes only; on Linux npm exec's `sh -c` stays as a non-node hop between the outer worker and the inner vitest and cuts the walk. The ancestry is now independent of the hops: the probe preload (new `src/test-child-vitest-probe.ts`, which also holds the `isWorker` / `isUnderOuterWorker` rule) makes the outermost `workers/forks.js` process set `WF_OUTER_WORKER=<its pid>` on itself, every descendant inherits it, and each record carries it as `outer`; a worker below the outer one is a record whose `outer` is set and is not its own pid. The one-worker assertion is unchanged. Pin: `src/test-child-vitest-chain.test.ts` builds a worker -> `sh -c` -> node chain with the check's own probe and rule (red with the ppid walk: `expected false to be true`). README and the bundled skill do not describe the engine's own tests.

- queue, replay (ENG-QUEUE-LINE-ADDED-AT): a queue line recorded no add time, so a line added after the replay's window showed in its next-line rows at the start. `queue_add` / `wf-queue --add` now write `added: <ISO time>` on the line (state like `base`: setting it by hand is refused on add and edit; `queue_edit` leaves it; `queue_list` / `describeQueue` lines carry `added`). `captureReplayQueue` writes it as the exported line's `added`, `snapshotAt` hides the line at a moment before it (a line without `added`, every line from before this change, shows from the start as before), and `assertReplayClean` checks it is an ISO time. `REPLAY_QUEUE_NOTE` (and the committed `docs/public/replay/anyparse.json` note, edited in place; the data gains `added` on the next re-export) now says a line is shown from the time it was added instead of that a later line cannot be rebuilt; the header of `scripts/export-replay.mjs` and `captureReplayQueue`'s comment say the same. README and the bundled skill do not describe the line's state keys. Pin: `src/queue-line-added-at.test.ts` (add writes an ISO `added`, a hand-set one is refused, an edit keeps it; a replay hides a line before its `added` and shows it after), each failing with its code removed.

- site (ENG-REPLAY-POOL-UNDERUSE-MARKS): the live Pool tab marks a lease that holds threads and runs far below them (`UNDERUSE ~N`, ENG-PANE-POOL-UNDERUSE), but the replay's `buildPool` dropped the pool log's `underuse:` lines (`PoolMarkKind` knew `backfill`, `overrun` and `starved`). `PoolMarkKind` gains `underuse`: the line is read like `overrun:` (threads held, busy cpus run, label), kept only when its label is on the replay's allowlist (`poolLabelAllowed`, the same check `assertReplayClean` repeats on the finished file), and `poolStateAt` sets `PoolLine.underuse` for it, so the replayed Pool tab draws the same `UNDERUSE ~N` as the pane. `docs/pool.md`, `docs/development.md`, the header of `scripts/export-replay.mjs` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill list `underuse:` beside `overrun:` and `backfill:` as the lines that name a lease. - queue (ENG-QUEUE-CONVERT-INTO-PENDING-DIFFICULTY): `wf-queue --convert <old.md> --into <new.yaml> --status pending` (and the same without `--into`, which prints) still wrote pending lines with no `difficulty:`: ENG-QUEUE-CONVERT-DIFFICULTY-CHECK covered only the append to an existing queue. `markdownToYaml` now runs the same `difficultyRequired` check (one shared `refuseNoDifficulty` with `appendMarkdown`) when `--status pending` is given: refused with `ID: difficulty: <1-10> is required on a pending line`, exit 2, no file written; no `--status` (the old `[ ]` rows keep their migration form) and `--status backlog` are unchanged. `docs/queue.md` and the header comment of `src/queue-cli.ts` say it. - engine (ENG-ABORT-HINT-DEAD-FALLBACK): `abortHint` in `src/takeover.ts` fell back to `s.parent_session_id` for the abort call's `caller_session_id` when no parent state was at hand, but `earlierVisitNote` returns before building a hint in that case, so the fallback never ran. It is dropped: `abortHint` takes the parent's state, and `earlierVisitNote` gives no note (so no hint) without it. `src/abort-hint-dead-fallback.test.ts` pins both halves. No behaviour change.
- plan pane (ENG-FAILED-ROW-COST-FULL-LEDGER): after ENG-FAILED-SPEND-FULL-LEDGER the Failed header summed the whole ledger but each failed row's `$cost` still read the 30-day `ledger-recent.jsonl`, so a line whose runs were older showed `—` while the header counted it and the rows no longer added up to the header. A failed row's cost now comes from the same `/api/ledger-rows` totals as the header, asked for every failed line (the older rows folded under `+N older` too); a line with a run that recorded no `spend.usd`, anywhere in the ledger, shows `—` (never `$0.00` or a partial sum), and the header leaves it out of its sums and names it (`· k unmeasured`; `ledgerTotalsOf` sets `isUnmeasured`). `docs/dashboard.md` and `docs/action-states.md` say so. - plan pane (ENG-PANE-POOL-UNDERUSE): the Pool tab marked a lease that runs far above its threads (`OVERRUN ~N`) but not one that holds threads and runs far below them, which the pool had recorded as UNDERUSE since ENG-POOL-HELD-EQUALS-USED and the dashboard's Pool tab already showed. `poolView` now reads the `underuse` flag of each `/api/pool` row beside `overrun` (`PoolLine.underuse`: the busy cpus the lease runs, else null), and the pane draws ` UNDERUSE ~N` (yellow, bold) after the lease's request, keeping its cells at a narrow pane like the other flags. The pool's own reading is used: the pane samples nothing. The replay's lines carry `underuse: null` (its log keeps no underuse line). The README count is fixed and the paragraph points at `docs/workflow-yaml.md`; the CLAUDE.md `wf-run.ts` row now says `--status` and `--usage` read neither the project's `settings.json` nor `WF_PRIORITY` (stale since ENG-WF-RUN-STATUS-SETTINGS); the ENG-REVIEW-CLASS-VITEPRESS-IS-CODE entry lists the test edits it omitted.
