- site, replay (ENG-REPLAY-RUNNING-AT-WINDOW-START): the replay's queue rewound to the window's start kept a line that was claimed before the window and landed inside it as `done`, so it was absent from the queue and a line with `after:` on it showed Next at the start instead of Waiting on it. `buildReplay` now also hands its `queue` callback the task ids of the root sessions claimed before the window's start and not ended at it (`runningAtStart`), and `captureReplayQueue(queue, ranSince, projects, runningAtStart)` sets those lines to `running` in the rolled-back queue, whatever their status is now; the pick then words the wait as `after: RUN (running)`. `scripts/export-replay.mjs` passes the new set. Not fixed: a line added to the queue AFTER the window still shows (the queue records no add time and the event log no `queue_add`); the running line's own end is not in the file, so its wait does not clear within the replay. Pin: `src/replay-running-at-window-start.test.ts` (a line claimed at minute 0 and landed at minute 30 of a window that starts at minute 5: its dependent is Waiting `after: RUN (running)`, not Next; the callback's two sets), failing with the `runningAtStart` mapping removed.
- site, banner (ENG-BANNER-DEFAULT-AT-LANDED): `scripts/render-banner.mjs` captured the scene at a fixed `DEFAULT_AT = 5400` s, which after the replay's spend re-export lands before any Landed row, so the banner and social preview showed none of the prices the replay now carries. The default moment is now `defaultAt(replay)`: the first second at which the plan pane shows at least `DEFAULT_LANDED_ROWS` (3) Landed rows with a `$` price, read from `docs/public/replay/anyparse.json` (a replay that never gets that far is refused instead of rendered without prices); `--at <replay s>` still overrides it. `docs/banner.png` and `docs/social-preview.png` re-rendered; `docs/development.md` says how the moment is chosen; `src/site-banner-default-at.test.ts` pins that the default moment shows a `✓ Landed ... $` row, is the first such second, and that a short replay is refused.
- tests (ENG-TEST-CHILD-VITEST-OUTPUT-AND-WORKERS): the hygiene test of ENG-TEST-GIT-NO-SIGN-GUARD, which runs a child `vitest`, parsed the report with `stdout.slice(indexOf("{"))`, so any `{` printed before the JSON broke it, and the child ran at vitest's default parallelism inside a gate that leased fewer threads. The child now writes its report with `--reporter=json --outputFile=<tmp>` and the test reads the file, and runs with `--maxWorkers=1` (as does the child of `src/ci-browser-red.test.ts`, the only other test spawning a child `vitest`). `src/test-child-vitest.test.ts` pins both: it runs the real hygiene test with a node preload that prints a `{` line and records every process, and asserts the report still parses and that at most one grandchild worker is live at a time.
- engine-task (ENG-WORK-CHECK-BASE-RELATIVE): `work_check` looked for the CHANGELOG entry in `git diff --name-only master..HEAD`, a diff against the MOVING master, so once another line landed (and changed `CHANGELOG.md`) while a slice worked, that landing's entry counted as the slice's own and a branch with no entry passed the check. It now diffs against the branch's own base (`git merge-base master HEAD`, moved by a rebase), like `repro_check` and `repro_route`. The other `master` uses in the workflow are commit counts (`git rev-list --count master..HEAD`, already relative to the merge-base) and the land step's `$from`..`$merged` diffs, so none else diffed against the moving master. Pinned in `src/work-check-base-relative.test.ts` (an entry landed on a moved master is not taken for the branch's; a branch's own entry passes).
- tests (ENG-TEST-GIT-NO-SIGN-GUARD): a test that ran `git commit` or `git tag` in a temp fixture failed with `gpg: signing failed` when the host's global git config signs and its gpg agent fails under parallel load. `vitest.config.ts` now loads the setup file `src/test-git-env.ts`, which sets `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` for every test, so no test signs whatever the host config says (a test sets its own identity, as under `npm run check:ci`). `src/test-git-no-sign.test.ts` pins it: every test file that runs git commit or tag is run under a global config that signs with a failing gpg and must pass. The stray-write half is `src/test-tmp-guard.test.ts` (ENG-TEST-ABORT-EXEC-GROUP-FLAKE).
- site (ENG-SITE-GENERATED-ON-REBASE): `docs/public/workflows.json` was a committed export of `templates/`, so any slice that edited a template left it stale and the next site slice's rebase failed on the equality test (the parent regenerated it by hand once). It is now a build product, not a committed file: removed from git and git-ignored, written by `writeSiteWorkflows` (`src/site-workflows.ts`, the one writer, used by `scripts/export-workflows.mjs`) in `npm run docs:prepare` (`npm run build && npm run docs:workflows`), which `docs:dev` and `docs:build` (so the Pages deploy) run first, and by a vitest `globalSetup` (`src/site-workflows-setup.ts`) at the start of every test run; picked over regenerating the committed copy in `npm run check` because a tracked generated file still dirties the tree and conflicts on every template edit, and no copy in git means none to go stale. `escapeHtml` of the shared graph module (`dashboard/graph.js`) printed `null` / `undefined` as `"null"` / `"undefined"` where the dashboard's former `textContent` printed nothing; both now render as empty. `docs/development.md` and `docs/dashboard.md` say it. Pins: `src/site-generated-on-rebase.test.ts` (`escapeHtml(null)` and `box(null)`; the file untracked and git-ignored; the scripts write it first; the setup is registered; the writer rewrites a stale file), each failing with its code removed.
- engine-task (ENG-REPRO-CHECK-BASE-RELATIVE): `repro_check` and `repro_route` listed the files a bug's worktree had changed with `git diff --name-only master`, against the MOVING master, so once another line landed during the reproduce step every file that landing changed read as `files differ from master before the bug is reproduced` and the step refused until the visit cap (ENG-TEST-GIT-NO-SIGN-GUARD, refused 3 times); `repro_route` could likewise record a skipped reproduction for a branch that had changed nothing. Both now diff against the branch's own base, `git merge-base master HEAD` (a rebase moves it with the branch), and the refusal says `files differ from the branch base`. Second defect: `engine-task.yaml` lived only in the git-ignored `.claude/workflows/` (the global ignore `**/.claude/`), so no engine-task slice could change it through its worktree. It is now a tracked file of this repo, `.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml`, through `.gitignore` exceptions that keep the rest of `.claude/` ignored; the project tier loads it as before, so the local untracked copy in the main checkout is retired by the land that brings the tracked one (the same path, overwritten). `batch-task` has no repro step, so it had no such diff. Pinned in `src/repro-check-base-relative.test.ts` (a moved master is not blamed on the worktree, the worktree's own source change still is, the file is not ignored).
- site (ENG-SITE-REPLAY-FOLLOWUPS): `npm run docs:preview` served `docs/.vitepress/dist`, which is git-ignored and survives between runs, so a preview after a source edit showed the old build. The script is now `vitepress build docs && vitepress preview docs` (the preview path is that script; the vitest build test writes into a fresh temp directory, and `docs:record` builds into `docs/.vitepress/dist` and previews it right after, so neither served an old build), noted in `docs/development.md` and pinned in `src/site-map-replay.test.ts`. CLAUDE.md's file map gains a row each for `src/replay.ts`, `scripts/export-replay.mjs` and `dashboard/scene-dom.js`. The recorder's `?record` hook (`window.__replay`, `Scene.vue`) stays in the production build on purpose: it exists only in a tab whose own URL carries `?record` and hands over nothing but `seek` and `durationMs` of the static, public recording, so it reaches no server and no other visitor; a pin holds that shape. The plan pane's Landed tree: a day row whose sum includes failed or closed lines (listed nowhere under it) now reads `(3 landed) $39.71 incl. 10 closed` instead of `(3) $39.71 incl. 10 closed`, so its count agrees with the rows under it; the heading's `Landed (n)` already counts exactly the landed rows.
- engine (ENG-ABORT-GUARD-FOLLOWUPS): ENG-ABORT-ROOT-GUARD review notes. (1) The abort hints the engine prints (the earlier-visit/earlier-step agent note in `src/takeover.ts`, the closing line of `resume`) read `abort({ session_id, force: true })`, which the guard refuses on a session with active descendants (`force` needs a `reason`, and the target is outside the caller's subtree); the notes now offer `abort({ session_id: '<id>', caller_session_id: '<its parent>' })`, which passes, and the `resume` footer says `caller_session_id: <your own session id>`. (2) A `transition` naming no `caller_session_id` stays open on purpose (a step ends no session; the engine's own prompts step other sessions without one) — said in `docs/concepts.md`, the bundled workflow-authoring skill and `src/session-scope.ts`. Pins: `src/abort-hint-followable.test.ts` (the hint executed verbatim is not refused), `src/agent-link.test.ts` (hint text).
- exec deadline (ENG-DEADLINE-CLOCK-RESTART-GAP): `DeadlineClock` started counting when it was created, so a run adopted after a restart did not count the session's pool wait between the last write of `<rc>.wait` and the takeover (a 3 s wait nobody ticked for left a 2 s timeout to kill a command that waited in the pool). The adopting clock now counts from the file's mtime (the run's start when there is no file), so the gap is in the deadline; the stale `adopt` comment is fixed. The `.wait` file was rewritten on every tick (each second) while the session had a waiter: it is now written when the count has grown by a whole second since the last write (`WAIT_STORE_STEP_MS`), which the mtime seed keeps exact. Pins: `src/deadline-clock-restart-gap.test.ts` (the gap counts; the write rate), `src/exec-timeout-pool-wait.test.ts`. `docs/action-states.md` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill say it.
- plan pane (ENG-FAILED-SPEND-FULL-LEDGER): the Failed header's spend read only `ledger-recent.jsonl` (the last 30 days), so a failed line whose runs were older showed `not in the ledger` and the total undercounted (a line with runs on both sides of the window counted only the recent ones). The plugin API refuses a read of the full ledger (over 4 MiB), so the engine now answers `GET /api/ledger-rows?tasks=<ids>` from the whole `ledger.jsonl` (`Storage.readLedgerTaskRows`, indexed once per change of the file's size or mtime) and the pane sums those rows for the failed lines it counts, asking again only when `ledger.jsonl` or the ids move; `not in the ledger` now means no row anywhere, and an unanswered call falls back on the recent window. Pins: `src/failed-spend-full-ledger.test.ts`, `src/ledger-recent.test.ts`, `src/dashboard.test.ts`.
- review class (ENG-REVIEW-CLASS-VITEPRESS-IS-CODE): `scripts/review-class.sh` classed everything under root `docs/` as light, including the site's own code (`docs/.vitepress/` config, theme and Vue components), so a change touching only `docs/.vitepress/config.ts` got the light reviewer. A path under `docs/` / `specs/` that sits in `.vitepress/` or is a `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.mts`/`.cts`/`.js`/`.jsx`/`.mjs`/`.cjs`/`.vue` file is now code (checked before the light regex; `REVIEW_CODE_PATHS` overrides it); prose `.md` under `docs/` stays light. Pinned by `src/review-class-vitepress.test.ts`; `docs/queue.md` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill say it. The same commit edited two existing tests: the fixture git helpers in `src/batch-template.test.ts` and `src/cleanup-prunable-worktree.test.ts` now pass `-c commit.gpgsign=false -c tag.gpgsign=false`, and the `review-class.sh` test in `src/batch-template.test.ts` has a 60 s timeout.
- tests (ENG-TEST-ABORT-EXEC-GROUP-FLAKE): engine tests that run real processes (git, sh, `wf-run`) timed out under host load in four separate gates (`batch-template` "review-class.sh", `changelog-land-conflict`, `driver-block-agent-type`, `pool` "--efficiency runs under taskpolicy"; `engine` "a parent's abort kills a child's in-flight foreground exec group" failed once): the suite's fixed `testTimeout` of 15 s is 3x the unloaded time of the slowest of them, and a load of 8 busy processes per cpu stretches it 5x. `vitest.config.ts` `testTimeout` is now 60 s (a hang guard, not a speed bound: a hung test still ends), and the 62 explicit per-test timeouts under 60 s in `pool`, `fanout`, `exec-timeout-pool-wait`, `executor`, `suspend`, `tools`, `pool-gate`, `pool-usage` and `wf-run-status-settings` tests are removed so the guard reaches them (the dashboard-browser tests keep theirs: they are 30 s or more). The `engine` tests that waited for their `sleep`'s pid file through a fixed 50 x 20 ms loop (a late file read as `ENOENT`) and for its death through a fixed 1 s now wait through `waitUntil` (a 30 s deadline, the wait ends on the event; the pid file is read once its line is complete), the `executor` timeout-kill test's death wait is 30 s, and the `pool-usage` "the meter" test polls `pool.json` for the lease meter instead of sleeping 300 ms. A test whose setup timed out under load also wrote `b.txt` into the repo cwd (an empty worktree path): `batch-template` and `driver-block-agent-type` now pass the worktree through `assertUnderTmp` (`src/test-helpers.ts`: refuses an empty path or one outside the temp dir) before writing. Pins: `src/test-timeout-under-load.test.ts` asserts the config's `testTimeout` is at least 60 s (fails at 15 s); `WF_LOAD_REPRO=1` also runs the `review-class.sh` case loop beside 8 busy processes per cpu inside that timeout (red at 15 s; opt-in because the busy processes slow every test file beside it); `src/test-tmp-guard.test.ts` (the guard refuses an empty, relative, cwd or outside path; both test files check every `wt` through it). README and the bundled skill do not describe the test timeout.
- site, replay (ENG-REPLAY-QUEUE-STATE-REEXPORT): the committed replay was not re-exported after ENG-REPLAY-QUEUE-STATE, so the landing's plan pane still showed `Waiting 0`. `docs/public/replay/anyparse.json` is re-exported with the same window as before (`--from 2026-11-16T09:30:00Z --hours 4`, `--workflows` the engine's `.claude/workflows`: 46 sessions, 164 queue lines, 16 tasks with spend, 189 pool samples, 150.0 KB) and now carries the `queue` section the engine's pick built over the queue file rolled back to the window's start; `docs/media/scene.gif` is re-recorded from it. - queue (ENG-QUEUE-CONVERT-DIFFICULTY-CHECK): `wf-queue --convert <old.md> --into <existing.yaml> --status pending` appended through `rewriteQueue` without the pending-line difficulty check that `queue_add` got in ENG-QUEUE-ADD-DIFFICULTY-REQUIRED, so it could still write a pending line with no `difficulty:` (a `?????` row in the plan pane). `appendMarkdown` now runs the same `difficultyRequired` check on every appended line, for the write and for `--dry-run`: refused with `ID: difficulty: <1-10> is required on a pending line`, exit 2, the file unchanged; `--status backlog` (the default) still appends. The `--add` error text and the header comment of `src/queue-cli.ts` showed `{id: X, touches: [a], text: …}`, which a pending queue now refuses; their example carries `difficulty: 3`. `docs/queue.md` says it. - wf-run (ENG-WF-RUN-STATUS-SETTINGS): `wf-run --status` with no `--priority` also read `.claude/workflows/settings.json`, so a malformed file made `--status` exit 64 although status needs no priority. The project's priority is now read only on the paths that queue a command; `--status` and `--usage` read neither it nor `WF_PRIORITY`. - site, map (ENG-REPLAY-DRIVER-MODEL): the replay and the map showed `driver · opus` / `driver · sonnet` for a task's driver, which runs on its workflow's `driver:` model (haiku / medium) (User 2026-11-16). `EventLog._modelInForce` took the parent's stage row of the linked step, else the session's own `context.model`; a task session linked by the dispatcher (its `dispatch` state reads no stage) fell to the task's EXECUTE model from its difficulty. The `agent_link` / `agent_respawn` event of a session whose workflow declares `driver:` now records the driver block's `model` and `effort` (`EventLog.setDriverResolver`, set by the engine from the session's workflow). Events already written keep their field: `conversationAt` (the replay's parent conversation) reads the workflow's `driver:` of `ReplayFile.workflows` for the root's link, and the map's driver chip already did. The committed `docs/public/replay/anyparse.json` is not re-exported (the export reads the live event log), and its old events render right through that read. `docs/dashboard.md` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill say what changed. - plan pane (ENG-PANE-LANDED-AFTER-RESTART): a sibling project's Landed section drew every land found in git as `????? ※ — ?` (no difficulty pips, start or cost) after a reboot, although the ledger held each row. The ledger is read from disk and keyed by task id, so the restart was not the cause: the git-found rows joined only the `slice-pipeline` / `engine-task` runs of the ledger, and that project's queue runs its own task workflow. A git land now falls back to the ledger's landed run of the task in any workflow (the figures the ledger-only rows already used, one helper `landFigures` for both). Pinned by `src/pane-landed-after-restart.test.ts` (the real `claude plugin test` harness), documented in `docs/dashboard.md`.
- batch-task (ENG-CLEANUP-PRUNABLE-WORKTREE): after a reboot wiped /tmp, the landed task's `cleanup` exec failed with `cannot delete branch 'batch/<task>' used by worktree at ...` because the worktree directory was gone but git still listed it as `prunable` and kept the branch held. `cleanup` now runs `git worktree prune` (it drops only entries whose directory is missing) before `git branch -d`, so a vanished worktree directory is not a failure. Pinned by `src/cleanup-prunable-worktree.test.ts`, which removes the worktree directory and runs the template's `cleanup` command.
- docs (ENG-README-CLEANUP): the README was ~2700 words with two single-line walls of 4400+ characters (the last Features bullet on the agent types, the paragraph about the site, the replay and the recording) and mixed what a first-time reader needs with what a contributor needs (User 2026-11-16). It is now a landing: the banner, the badges, one sentence, the demo GIF, Why (three problems), Features (eight one-line bullets, each linking its page), Install, a Quick start (a four-state YAML workflow with an `exec` state, and how to run it), the docs table with short cells, Contributing (one link) and License; no line of prose over 120 characters. Nothing was deleted, only moved: the Development commands, the Architecture file table and the site / replay / recording paragraph (split into short sections: the replay, the stage, the graphs, the pool in the scene, recording, the brand, the video, the deploy) are the new page `docs/development.md` (in the site's sidebar; `docs/dashboard.md` points to it); the Agent types bullet is two sections of `docs/queue.md` (`#### Agent types`, `#### The read-only Bash gate`); the engine mechanics the Features bullets named (sub-workflow stack of depth 10, snapshot isolation, runtime overlays, the SessionStart hook) are items 5-8 of `docs/concepts.md` § How It Works. Pins in `src/docs-site.test.ts`: the README's sections and their order, no prose line over 120 characters, three or four Why items and six to eight Features bullets each linking one site page, every site link names an existing page and heading, the docs table lists every page, the Quick start workflow loads through `Loader` with three or four states and an `exec`, and what left the README is on its page; the headings that moved (`## Commands`, `## Architecture`, `## The site`, the two agent sections) are in the one-page-per-heading table; `src/site-banner.test.ts` now reads `npm run docs:banner` from `docs/development.md`.
- site, replay (ENG-REPLAY-QUEUE-STATE): the landing's plan pane showed `Waiting 0` and an empty or short Next although the real queue always holds lines; `snapshotAt` built the queue only from sessions that ran inside the window (`waiting: []` hard-coded, Next = the lines that start later), and the event log does not carry the queue (User 2026-11-16: «На сайте в демо в "Waiting 0" - пусто»). (1) `src/queue.ts`: `planPick` can say what keeps a line out as ids (`PickOpts.blocks`: `PickBlock` = the queue lines its `after:` waits for, whether part of that wait no line's end clears, the line it may not run beside and how); the `reasons` text and every pick result are unchanged. (2) `src/replay-queue.ts` (new, node side): `captureReplayQueue(queue, tasks, projects)` runs the engine's own pick (`planPick`, `describeQueue`: the code `wf-queue` and `queue_next` run) over the queue file rolled back to the window's start (every line that ran at or after the window's start, from the event log's root sessions of those projects, the window's own and those that ran and landed after it, and any still running, set back to pending; the window size lifted) and cuts it to ONE record per pending line of `--project` / `--also` (the basename of its resolved repo) in the pick's visiting order: id, difficulty, priority, kind and a wait as ids and enums (`after`, `fixed` = `X (failed)` or `manual condition`, `against` = how and which line, `hold` = `parked` / `parent-run (model —)` / `overlaps a line outside the replay`), never a line's text, notes, touches or prose `after:`. (3) `scripts/export-replay.mjs` calls it with the window's tasks (`--queue FILE|none`, default `<project-dir>/.claude/batch/queue.yaml`; `buildReplay` takes `BuildOptions.queue`, a callback) and writes `ReplayFile.queue` with a `note` that a line added to the queue after the window cannot be rebuilt; `assertReplayClean` refuses a queue section with a field the export does not write, an id that is not a plain id, a reason outside the fixed set, a wait on a line the section does not list, or another note. (4) `snapshotAt` replays it (`queueAt`): a line that has not started waits for the lines its `after:` names to land and for the line it may not run beside to end, shown with the pick's own words (`after: R (running)`, `overlaps X`); with nothing left it is Next, in the pick's order; with Next empty, the lines whose only wait is a running line are the unblocked ones the pane lists; a parked line only counts in Pending; the lines move Waiting, then Next, then Running as the sessions start and end. A file with no `queue` section (written before this) plays as it did: the lines that start later are Next, Waiting is empty. Pins in `src/replay-queue.test.ts` (the pick's blocks, the rollback and the allowlist, the clean check, the replay of Waiting -> Next -> Running, `buildReplay`, the export script). The committed replay and `scene.gif` were re-exported by ENG-REPLAY-QUEUE-STATE-REEXPORT.
- site, replay (ENG-REPLAY-SPEND-REEXPORT): the committed replay and scene.gif were not re-exported after ENG-REPLAY-SPEND, so the landing still showed `—` in every Landed row (User 2026-11-16: «заметил, что тут цен нехватает? Почему?»). `docs/public/replay/anyparse.json` is re-exported (`--from 2026-11-16T09:30:00Z --hours 4`: 46 sessions, 5 engine-task lanes, 16 tasks with spend, 189 pool samples, 129.8 KB) and `docs/media/scene.gif` re-recorded from it; the Landed rows read `$x.xx` and the heading its sum. The export gained `--workflows DIR,DIR`: the engine's `engine-task` lives in the engine's own `.claude/workflows`, not in the replay project's, so the file carried the engine lanes' sessions with no stations and the map could not draw them (`file.workflows` had no `engine-task`); a workflow already loaded keeps its own entry. `npm run docs:record` now starts at replay second 10500 (its Landed rows are there; at the old 5400 none had landed yet). README, `docs/dashboard.md` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill name the option. Pins: `src/replay.test.ts` (the committed file: every landed row carries spend and the Landed heading a sum, spend only for the replay's own tasks, at least one completed engine-task root of the engine's project with stations; the export script with `--workflows` gives an `engine-task` its stations), each failing with the file or the option removed.
- site, replay (ENG-REPLAY-SPEND): every Landed row of the landing's scene showed `—` where the live pane shows $, and only anyparse took part in the map. (1) The exported replay carried no spend: the live pane reads each task's `spend.usd` from `ledger.jsonl`, which `scripts/export-replay.mjs` never read. It now also reads the ledger (`--ledger FILE|none`, default `<state dir>/ledger.jsonl`) and `buildReplay` (`BuildOptions.ledger`) writes `ReplayFile.spend`, the real `spend.usd` per task id summed over every run of the line as the pane's `ledgerSpendOf` sums it (`ledgerSpend`: rows of `slice-pipeline` / `engine-task` with an end; a line any of whose runs recorded none is left out whole, never `$0`), under the SAME allowlist as the events: only the tasks of the exported sessions, a row of any other task is never read into the result, and `assertReplayClean` refuses a write holding a spend of a task that is not the replay's, a non-number or a negative. `snapshotAt` feeds it to the pane: a Landed and a Failed row carry `usd`, `Snapshot.days` holds the day sums (the landed lines plus the measured failed and closed ones that ended that day) and `landedUsd` the Landed heading's sum, so the scene's pane draws `$x.xx` and the day row its sum as the live pane does; a task with no measured spend stays `—`. (2) The allowlist is two projects now: `--project anyparse` and `--also mcp-workflow-engine` (default; `none` for the first alone, comma separated for more), `BuildOptions.also`, `ReplayFile.projects` (absent: `project` alone), for the events, the pool's land locks (`<project>-land` of each kept project keeps its name, every other lock is anonymous) and the spend alike, so the engine-task lanes the anyparse dispatcher drives show on the map and in the pane; `buildReplay` also keeps a finished root with no child session when it is a task run (`task` set, workflow `slice-pipeline` / `engine-task`): an engine-task works alone, and the old rule (a root with children) dropped every one; every other project stays out of the file. (3) `docs/media/scene.mp4` is removed (nothing read it: the README shows `scene.gif`, the site plays the scene live, `video/render.mjs` draws its own frames); `npm run docs:record` writes only `docs/media/scene.gif`, and `-- --mp4` the clip into the git-ignored `video/out/scene.mp4`. The README no longer calls `scene.gif` "the banner above". README, `docs/dashboard.md` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill say what changed. Pins: `src/replay-spend.test.ts` (`ledgerSpend` and its agreement with the pane's `ledgerSpendOf`, the allowlist, the `also` project kept and a third one not, the self-check on a foreign spend, the engine's land lock, a Landed row with `$1.50` and one with `—`, the day sums and `landedUsd` equal to the pane's `daySpends` / `landedUsd` over the same ledger), `src/replay.test.ts` (the export script with a ledger and the engine's project, in an isolated state directory), `src/site-deploy-gif.test.ts` (`--mp4`, no committed mp4), each failing with its code removed.
- pool (ENG-POOL-HELD-EQUALS-USED): a lease is its caller's declaration, but the pool showed only a lease that ran far MORE than it held (`overrun`); a lease that held 4 threads and ran 0 cores for half a minute (a serial phase under a parallel lease, a gate row between its trees) was invisible, and so was the sum over a day. The pool now reports it, with admission, priority and order unchanged. (1) UNDERUSE: a measured lease whose tree runs at least `max(1, threads/2)` cpus below its threads for 30 s gets one `underuse: pid N holds 4 threads, runs ~0 — <label>` line in `pool.log` (`PoolEntry.underuse_since` / `underuse`; a lease that runs its threads again restarts the grace). (2) `wf-run --status` / `pool_status` show used against held for every measured lease: `holds 4 threads, runs ~3`, flagged `(OVERRUN)` or `(UNDERUSE)` (`EntryMarks.use`); `GET /api/pool` carries `runs` and `PoolRow.underuse`, and the dashboard's Pool tab draws an `UNDERUSE ~N` plaque (it shows `runs` only in the OVERRUN and UNDERUSE plaques). (3) `wf-run --usage [--since 90m|24h|2d]` (new `src/pool-usage.ts`): each lease carries a meter (`PoolEntry.meter`: thread-ms held, run, idle and over, advanced at most every 10 s so a pass does not rewrite the pool and wake every waiter); a lease that ends through release appends a record to `<pool dir>/usage.jsonl` (rotated past 1 MiB), and `--usage` prints per label key: `n`, mean `held` and `used`, `idle thr-min`, `over thr-min` and the sampled share, most idle first. (4) A lease holder now passes every 5 s while its command runs (an unref'd timer from `attach`, cleared at release), so a lease nobody polls is sampled too. Not recorded: a lease reaped as an orphan. The callers' own lease sizes are the callers' to fix. Tests: `src/pool-underuse.test.ts`, `src/pool-usage.test.ts`.
- removed (ENG-REMOVE-STATUSLINE-SCRIPT): `scripts/statusline-workflows.sh` is deleted, with its note in `docs/dashboard.md`. README: the `docs:banner` paragraph.
- site (ENG-SITE-GRAPH-NO-INNER-SCROLL): the landing's "The workflows" view was a box with `max-height: 760px; overflow: auto`, a scroll inside a scrolling page. `WorkflowGraphs.vue` now draws the SVG at the width of the section (`width: 100%`, never above its natural width, as before) and `height: auto`, so the box is as tall as the viewBox gives at that width and `overflow` is `visible`: the page scrolls through the graph, a plain mouse wheel is never captured (the component has no wheel handler; the dashboard's graph has no pan or zoom either), switching the workflow in the picker resizes the box to the new graph, and at phone width a wide graph scales down with no horizontal page scroll. Pins: `src/site-graph-no-inner-scroll.test.ts` (real component in headless Chromium at 1100 and 390 px: no overflow scroll, no max-height, box height follows the viewBox aspect, picker switch, no horizontal page scroll; and its source has no wheel handler or scroll box), each failing with its code removed. Note: the line spoke of "the existing zoom"; there is none, so a graph wider than a phone is small and is read with the browser's own pinch zoom.
- worker (ENG-WORKER-NO-LIVE-BG-AT-TURN-END): a worker that ended its turn with its one-line DONE while its own `run_in_background` wait loop was still alive never fired its task notification (the harness notifies only when the agent stops with no live background children), and its driver idled for hours. The shipped texts now say: (1) `batch-task` worker and reviewer prompts: never end a turn with live background work, stop or await every background task first; (2) every wait loop ends on failure too (the job's exit, a FAILED marker, a timeout), not on the success marker alone; (3) `task-driver` and `agents/driver.md` (one rule in both): a notice that the agent waits on its own background work is answered by telling it to finish or kill that work and repeat its line; a WAITING driver woken by a later enforce prompt or parent message, after the agent's hand-back and before its notification (never on the hand-back turn itself), probes it once (the driver has no clock, so the trigger is a signal, not 30 min). `agent-liveness.md` and the task-delegation SKILL carry the same rule; pinned by `src/worker-no-live-bg-at-turn-end.test.ts`.
- site (ENG-SITE-BANNER-RENDER): `docs/banner.png` (the README's banner) and `docs/social-preview.png` dated from 2026-03-01: they said "16 Workflows" (`templates/` ships 21), carried the old tagline without the pool, the queue or the live map, and a hand-drawn graph; the README itself still said "20 bundled workflows". `scripts/render-banner.mjs` (`npm run docs:banner [-- --at <replay s>]`, playwright over `scripts/scene-capture.mjs`) now renders both: the site's name and tagline read from the hero of `docs/index.md`, chips (FSM engine, N workflows counted from `templates/*.yaml`, thread pool, task queue, live map) and, on the right, a real frame of the landing's scene (map, plan pane, pool strip) at a chosen replay moment, as `docs/banner.png` (1200x400) and `docs/social-preview.png` (1280x640, GitHub's social preview size). `src/site-banner.test.ts` pins the chip count (and the README's number) to the template count, the tagline to the hero, the two PNG sizes and the script's wiring. README: "21 bundled workflows", the `docs:banner` paragraph.
- site (ENG-SITE-POOL-DEMO): the landing's scene could not show the thread pool (its Pool tab said "the replay records no pool") and the pool had no page of its own. (1) `scripts/export-replay.mjs` also reads `pool.log` (and `pool.log.1`; `--pool DIR|none`) for the replay's window, `buildReplay` takes the pool (`BuildOptions.pool`) and only chooses a window the log covers (and prefers one with waiters); `src/replay.ts` `buildPool` writes `ReplayFile.pool` (`frames`: a sample per row, held / budget / load / external / leases / waiting, the asks as thread counts, and the leases and waiters the log named) under an ALLOWLIST, the pool being shared by every project on the machine: a lease keeps its label only when it names a slice or session of the replay (`poolLabelAllowed`: plain words, one a replay name), every lock but the project's own `<project>-land` is made anonymous, every other lease is a thread count in one row, "leases the log does not name (this project or another)" (a sample lists no holders; a name appears only on the sample after an `overrun:` or `backfill:` line), no pid, path or priority is written; `assertReplayClean` checks the finished file (every label on the allowlist, no `/Users/`, `/home/`, `/private/` or home path, no pid field, no foreign lock) and the export refuses to write on any violation. `exec_end` rows keep `wait_ms` when it is 3 s or more. (2) The scene: the Pool tab is the mod's own `poolBody` fed `poolStateAt` (budget, held, load, the leases the log named and one row for the rest, the waiters in admission order, the land lock; the log carries no priority or age, so the shared row leaves them out when `priority` is `''` / `ageMs` is negative), the tab label reads `poolCountsAt`, a thin `PoolStrip.vue` under the map (`poolStrip` paths, threads held against the budget, the waiters as a band, a cursor) shares the scene's clock, and a lane whose exec waits for a lease (`poolWaiting`: the exec's `wait_ms` from its start, the pipeline it runs under with it) passes the map's own `pool` option, so its plaque reads "waiting for the pool". The replay was re-exported (a fresh window both logs cover) and `scene.gif` / `scene.mp4` re-recorded. (3) Docs: a new page `docs/pool.md` (Thread pool: why, a diagram, leases, budget, priority, locks, backfill, overrun, `wf-run`, `config.yaml`, with the live `PoolDemo.vue`), in the sidebar, linked from `getting-started` (a new paragraph), `index` and `action-states` as the reference; README, `docs/dashboard.md` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill say what changed. `RESERVED_CORES` of `src/pool.ts` is exported (the export's default threshold). Pins: `src/replay-pool.test.ts` (the allowlist, the self-check refusing a foreign label, a path, a pid and a foreign lock, the window coverage, `poolStateAt`, `poolWaiting`, `poolStrip`, the committed file, the scene and the page), each failing with its code removed.
- driver (ENG-DRIVER-EFFORT-MEDIUM): the shipped driver ran haiku at `effort: low`, and a haiku/low driver made an off-script `start()` + `abort` that cascade-abandoned a whole campaign. As an experiment the driver now runs at `effort: medium`: `agents/driver.md` frontmatter, bundled `batch-task`'s `driver:` block, the README / `docs/queue.md` / workflow-authoring skill text and the fixtures that mirror it (`src/driver.test.ts` reads the real `batch-task`'s rendered DRIVER call). Its tool list is cut to what a driver prompt calls: `start` (the worker's session), `status`, `transition`, `context_set`, `link_agent`, `queue_mark`, `queue_edit` plus Agent / SendMessage / Read / ToolSearch / SubagentHandback; `abort`, `delete`, `create`, `modify`, `resume`, `set_priority`, `list`, `sessions`, `pool_status`, `suspended` and `queue_next` / `queue_add` / `queue_header` are gone, so a driver cannot do the incident's call. A project that extends the type (`.claude/agents`) or whose driver prompts name one of the dropped tools must list it itself. Pins: `src/driver-effort-tools.test.ts`.
- site (ENG-SITE-LIVE-GRAPHS): the landing's "What you see" showed three blurry PNG screenshots. The workflow graphs are now live: `dashboard/graph.js` (new, pure, served as `/graph.js`) holds what `index.html` kept inline (`renderGraph` with its dagre layout, the state kinds and routes, the state panel `stateDetailHtml`, the Workflows view `workflowViewHtml` and its delegated clicks `workflowClick`), the dashboard imports it and its inline `onclick` handlers and selection globals are gone (the selection is read from the DOM), and the site's `WorkflowGraphs.vue` imports the same file with the `@dagrejs/dagre` package: every bundled workflow as SVG (sharp at any DPI) behind a picker (master and coding first), a click on a node or a row opens the state's prompt and transitions as on the Workflows tab, a sub-workflow node opens that workflow; no server. The definitions are `docs/public/workflows.json`, written from `templates/` by `npm run build && npm run docs:workflows` (`scripts/export-workflows.mjs`); `describeWorkflows` (new `src/workflow-describe.ts`) is the one description `/api/workflows` and the export share. The status line is the Claude Code mod's own band: `statusBandTree` (`hooks/pane-view.ts`, a new `PaneAction` `pane` for its `[plan]` button) replaces the JSX in `plan-pane.tsx`, and `StatusLine.vue` draws it from `snapshotAt` of the replay at a moment the reader picks (it opens on the busiest one). The dashboard's tokens and rules, scoped, come from one helper (`dashboard-css.ts`) for the map, the graphs and the page's other blocks. The status line is the plan-pane mod's band (shown with the pane closed), not `scripts/statusline-workflows.sh` (kept for setups without the mod, now a short note): `dashboard.md`'s Status Line section describes the band. `StatusLine.vue` draws the band above the prompt box, as the mod does, and `WorkflowGraphs.vue` has a `← <workflow>` button back to the page's workflow after a sub-workflow click (the concepts page has no picker). The install steps moved from the markdown to `Install.vue`, in the hero's after-slot right behind the scene and above the features. The three PNGs under `docs/public/screenshots/` are deleted; `concepts.md` shows the coding graph and `dashboard.md` the status line with the same components (an HTML block of its own on a line escapes the config's `v-pre`). The dashboard has no pan or zoom on a graph (the SVG scales to its width), so the site has none either. Pins: `src/site-live-graphs.test.ts` (one module on both sides and none inline, the module pure, the markup, the exported file current against the templates, the band, the landing), and a real-browser case on the Workflows tab in `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts`. A real-browser run found the plan card's edges still calling the moved `pointsToPath`; they use the module's now. Also: the pool test "a flexible waiter that queued behind a lock waits its wait_full window from the lock's release" polled every 5 s, so a lost `pool.json` watch event under a loaded suite run made the wait exactly the poll (5007 ms, over its 3000 ms bound); it polls every 500 ms now and still proves the window starts at the lock's release.
- engine (ENG-ABORT-ROOT-GUARD): a stray `start({workflow})` from a sub-agent returned the campaign ROOT's id (the idempotent start of a process that already had a root), and its `abort` of that id cascade-abandoned the whole tree (dispatcher, 5 running slices and their workers). Three changes, `src/session-scope.ts`: (1) a `start` without `parent_session_id` while the process has an active root is refused, naming the root by workflow @ state and never its id (`force: "<reason>"` starts a second root on purpose, logged); a start with `parent_session_id` is unchanged. After a `/compact` a parent that called `start` again now gets the refusal and reads its session with `status` or the hook's active-sessions list. (2) `abort` of a root or of a session with active descendants is refused unless `caller_session_id` (own session id) is the target or an ancestor of it, or `force: true` + `reason`; with `caller_session_id` any target outside the caller's subtree (an ancestor, a sibling) is refused the same way; the refusal is checked before the early process kill, so nothing is killed. `transition` takes `caller_session_id` / `reason` and refuses a target outside the caller's subtree. (3) every cascade abort logs `abort: session <id> by <caller> ends <n> session(s)` to stderr (plus the forced reason). A guard against a stray call, not an adversary: the server cannot tell its callers apart, so a call naming no caller is judged by the target alone. Pins in `src/abort-root-guard.test.ts`.
- site, plan pane (ENG-SITE-SCENE-STAGE): the landing's plan pane was a hand-written Vue imitation (`TerminalPane.vue`) that looked unlike the real mod, the play / speed / scrub controls sat on the map pane only, and the map scrolled sideways when it did not fit. Now what the pane draws is ONE pure module, `hooks/pane-view.ts`: `paneTree` turns a Snapshot (with the pane's tab, folds, open card, clock and size) into a tree of box / text / button / link, and the conversation lines the mod draws (agent spawn, end notification, a driver's hand-back, a tool group's engine moves) are its `spawnLine` / `endLine` / `handBackLine` / `movesLine`. The mod (`hooks/plan-pane.tsx`) draws those trees with Claude Code's elements (a press hands its action — fold, Landed day, tab, card — back to the mod's atoms); the site draws the same trees in the DOM (`PaneView.vue`). `src/replay.ts` `planAt` / `difficultyCells` gave way to `snapshotAt` (the mod's Snapshot built from the replay's events: live sessions, the queue — Next / Running / done / failed, kind marks, the window = the most pipelines at once — and the lands; no cost, no sha) and `conversationAt` (the parent's own lines: starts, driver spawns, questions and answers, lands, ends; no user text; `conversationTail` keys the window's last lines by their index in the whole conversation, since two lines can share a millisecond, kind and label). The scene is one 16:9 stage (1920x1080 scaled to the page width): the map fills it and never scrolls (`fitView`: scaled to the frame by its viewBox; `cameraTop`: when the lanes are taller, a camera eases onto the running ones; `followsBottom` is gone), the Claude Code window is drawn over it translucent with a backdrop blur (the conversation strip and the docked plan pane, folds and tabs working), and one transport bar under the stage drives both (`replay-store.ts` owns the clock; a Terminal button and `T` hide the window). The recorders (`scripts/scene-capture.mjs`, `docs:record`, `video/render.mjs`) pin that same stage to the 1920x1080 frame at scale 1, so site and video match; `docs/media/scene.gif` / `scene.mp4` re-recorded.
- site (ENG-SITE-VOICED-VIDEO): a generated, re-renderable voiced intro video, `npm run video` -> `video/out/intro.mp4` (1920x1080, English narration, burned subtitles) and `intro.srt`. `video/script.md` is the narration (`[p01] text`, one phrase per line: problem, how the engine solves it, the live scene, how to start; about 2:20) and `video/storyboard.json` ties each phrase to a card or to a stretch of the anyparse replay (`from` and `speed`, or `from` and `to`; `fps`, `gap`). `video/tts.py` voices phrases with local free models, Kokoro by default (Apache-2.0, `video/.venv`, `video/requirements.txt`) or Chatterbox (MIT) cloning a sample the user supplies (`video/voice-sample.*`, git-ignored), one WAV per phrase in `video/cache/` keyed by a hash of engine, voice and text, so an edited phrase alone is voiced again. `video/render.mjs` plans the timeline from the WAV lengths (the video is exactly as long as the speech), draws the scene and the cards frame by frame in headless Chromium, lays the audio, writes the SRT (muxed as a text track too) and encodes with ffmpeg; `--check` validates script against storyboard and replay length without rendering, `--only <segment>` renders a part, `--no-burn` leaves the caption off the frames. The scene setup (build the site, serve it, the `?record` seek hook, the 1920x1080 restyle) moved from `scripts/record-scene.mjs` to `scripts/scene-capture.mjs`, shared by both recorders. `out/`, `cache/`, `.venv/` and the voice sample are git-ignored. Tests in `src/voiced-video.test.ts`.
- site (ENG-SITE-DEPLOY-GIF): the landing now shows a scene, a terminal imitation of the plan pane (`TerminalPane.vue`: Running / Next / Landed, difficulty bars, state, model, review rounds; computed by the new pure `planAt` / `difficultyCells` of `src/replay.ts`) on the left and the live map replay on the right, one clock through a shared store. `npm run docs:record` (`scripts/record-scene.mjs`, `playwright` devDependency + its Chromium, ffmpeg) plays the scene frame by frame in headless Chromium through the page's `?record` seek hook and encodes `docs/media/scene.mp4` (1920x1080, for the later voiced video) and `docs/media/scene.gif` (960 px, under 4 MB, shown in the README). `.github/workflows/pages.yml` builds the site and deploys it to GitHub Pages on a push to `master`. The plan imitation has no cost column: the replay carries no spend.
- exec (ENG-EXEC-TIMEOUT-EXCLUDES-POOL-WAIT): an exec's `timeout` counted the pool wait that happens inside its command (the command leases per branch through `wf-run`, so the exec holds no lease itself): a slice-worker baseline exec with a 30 min timeout was killed twice while its `wf-run` waited STARVED in the pool for 28 min, no work ran, and the slice went to blocked. Now the exec's deadline stretches by the time its session has a waiter queued in the pool (`wf-run` carries `WF_SESSION_ID`; `Pool.waitingSince` reads the earliest live waiter of a session from `pool.json` with no admission pass, leaving out the engine's own waiters (its pid: a sibling fanout shard's or exec's lease wait is not the command's); `DeadlineClock` in `executor.ts` polls it each second and sums the wait), up to a ceiling of 4x the `timeout`, so a pool that never grants still ends the exec with 124. The summed wait is the result's `wait_ms` (history, ledger, `{{wait_ms}}`) and is taken off `duration_ms`; the counted wait is kept in the run's `<rc>.wait` file as it grows, so a taken-over exec (`adopt`) after a restart starts from it. A waiter of another session stretches nothing; an exec with no session context keeps the plain timer. `staleAfter` of an exec's default now floors at `timeout` x `POOL_WAIT_CEILING_FACTOR` (4, in `types.ts`) + 300 s, so STALE never fires inside the stretched deadline. Pins: `src/exec-timeout-pool-wait.test.ts` (2 s pool wait under a 1 s timeout completes with `wait_ms`; 2 s of work under 1 s times out; a wait that never ends is cut at 4x; another session's waiter and a sibling shard's own lease wait stretch nothing; an adopted run with a recorded prior wait is not killed at the plain deadline), `src/stale.test.ts` (a 1800 s exec is not STALE at 3700 s). `docs/action-states.md` and the bundled workflow-authoring skill say it. Sibling left open: the same budget inside `dev slice-gate` rows is anyparse's, not the engine's.
- site (ENG-SITE-MAP-REPLAY): the documentation site's landing page plays the live workflow map as a replay, with no server. `scripts/export-replay.mjs` (`npm run build && npm run docs:replay -- --project-dir <checkout>`) cuts a window of whole pipelines (a few hours; a land, a split, review rounds, several lanes at once; best-scoring window whose written text, in bytes, is under `--max-kb`, default 300) of ONE project (default `anyparse`, the open-source one) from the recorded event log into `docs/public/replay/anyparse.json`; `buildReplay` in the new `src/replay.ts` keeps that project's sessions and their children only, so another project's session, task, path and id never enter the file, drops an ask's free text, writes the queue path without a home directory and keeps only the keys the map reads (rows `[ms, kind, session, data]`). The new `docs/.vitepress/theme/MapReplay.vue` (the home hero's after-slot, in the theme, not in the `v-pre` markdown) feeds the expanded events, on a clock (play / pause, 1x to 60x, scrub; starts in view, not under reduced motion; follows the newest lane until the reader scrolls up), to the dashboard's own `fold` + `layout` (imported from `src/`, no copy) and draws the scene with `reconcile`, which moved from `dashboard/map.js` into the new `dashboard/scene-dom.js` so the page and the site share one renderer; the dashboard's `tokens.css` and `components.css` are scoped to the block (`@scope`), so lanes advance, branches open, lands fold, hover and click work as on `/map`. The hero image of the landing is replaced by it. The VitePress config lets the build import `src/` and `dashboard/`. Pins: `src/replay.test.ts` (one project only and no private string in the output, the window and size limit, the clock, the committed file under 300 KB with a land, a split, review rounds and 3+ lanes at once, the script run end to end), `src/site-map-replay.test.ts` (the component runs the map's fold/layout/renderer, the player controls, no server, `docs:build` renders it), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (`/scene-dom.js` is served and map.js imports it). README and `docs/dashboard.md` say it.
- docs (ENG-SITE-SCAFFOLD): a documentation site, VitePress in `docs/` (devDependency `vitepress`; `npm run docs:dev` / `docs:build` / `docs:preview`; base `/claude-workflow/` for the GitHub project page, local search, dead links fail the build, English). The README was the only documentation, 1169 lines; its sections now live on pages, each on exactly one: `getting-started` (Quick Start with its install channels, CLAUDE.md snippet, permissions, Configuration), `concepts` (How It Works, Bundled Workflows, Bundled Skills, MCP Tools), `workflow-yaml` (Workflow YAML, Three-Tier Loading incl. `extends:`, when a project should have its own workflow), `action-states` (all of Action States), `queue` (Task queue, the model table, the task driver, Calibration report), `dashboard` (Plan pane, Dashboard, REST API, Status Line); new `faq` and a landing page `index` (pitch, the problem, screenshots, install). The README keeps the banner, pitch, Features, a short Install, a table linking the pages, Development with Architecture, and the license. Screenshots moved to `docs/public/screenshots/`. The text contains `{{mustache}}` examples, so the config marks every block `v-pre`. No deploy workflow yet (ENG-SITE-DEPLOY-GIF); the landing has a slot for the map replay (ENG-SITE-MAP-REPLAY). Pins: `src/docs-site.test.ts` (each former README heading is on exactly one page; the config's base, search, dead-link check and `v-pre`; the scripts; the pages exist and are in the sidebar). The bundled skills do not describe the docs.
- dashboard (ENG-TIMELINE-READABLE): the Timeline tab drew one bar per child of a root with children (else per childless root) and named child rows by workflow, so a queue run under a dispatcher that sat below another root drew every pipeline as sub-rows of one bar (about a hundred rows labelled `slice-pipeline` / `slice-recon` / `slice-worker` / `slice-review`, most empty in view), while runs without a dispatcher drew one bar per task. Now: (1) each task pipeline (a session with a `slice` / `task` id in its context or ledger line; a helper beneath it that carries the same id is a row, not a bar) is its own bar named by that id, whether or not a dispatcher started it; the dispatcher keeps one `parent · <workflow>` row at the bottom (`kind: "parent"`), and a session no task hangs under keeps the older rule. (2) A bar is folded to one row (the task's states as segments); a click on `▸` unfolds its agent sessions, and the fold survives the 2 s refresh (the markup is patched in place; the choice lives in the page). (3) Unfolded rows are named by role, model and round (`recon · sonnet`, `worker r2 · opus`, `review`: `role` = the workflow without the pipeline's prefix, `model` = the family from the ledger line or the session, `round` = the ordinal among the bar's rows of that role). (4) A row with no segment in the day (server) or the shown window (page) is not drawn; buttons `fit` / `1 h` / `6 h` / `day`, labels and the right column sticky on horizontal scroll, a now-line on today. (5) The bar's right column: duration, review rounds (entries into the state `review`: `visits.review`, else the review states, as `wf-calibration` counts; or the review sessions started, the larger), `$` (ledger `spend` + `parent_spend`, plus the `parent_spend` of member lines of the same task, `—` when not recorded), outcome `landed` (completed through a state named `land` or `<x>_land`, the Cost tab's rule) / `closed` / `failed` / `running`; a bar with no task is labelled by its workflow. (6) Project chips from the bars' projects (none selected = all). `/api/timeline` bars carry `kind`, `label`, `result`, `project`, `duration_ms`, `rounds`, `usd`; rows carry `role`, `model`, `round`, `name`. A session whose stack frame names no state is no longer drawn as a segment of `undefined`. Pins: `src/timeline.test.ts` (bars per task with and without a dispatcher, the helper that inherits a task id, row names, dropped rows, duration / rounds / spend / project / result, the older rule), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (folded by default, the right column, unfold, zoom width, chips, now-line only on today, the local custom properties), `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts` (opt-in: fold survives the refresh with the same nodes, zoom, sticky label and stats column, now-line, project filter).
- map (ENG-MAP-PER-COLUMN-WIDTH): every column of a band took the widest need of any column (one worker branch made all of them ~230 px). `columnNeeds` now gives each column its own width: its station name, the branches hung from it (`branchStation`, the placement `layLane` uses), its share of a loop label across it; between `G.minCol` (now 64) and `G.col` (230); x positions are a prefix sum. When the page is short of room the columns shrink proportionally, none under `G.fitCol` (120) or its own width if narrower. The `landed earlier:` strip fitted a count (`earlierShown`), so its chips ran past the band card: it now shows the chips that fit the band's width, the rest in `+N`, and a task that ran twice shows once, by its latest run. Pins: `src/map-layout.test.ts` (own-column widths, a long name widens only its column, the strip stays inside the band, the duplicate). Open: the ~370 px gap between a band's first two lanes does not exist on the recorded anyparse session set (every `slice-pipeline` band at about 330 moments, the page 900 to 2400 px wide): the largest space between two lanes is 112 px (one `laneGap` plus a section label and a rail of five ask rows), the largest slack inside a lane between its pass lines is 48 px, and no column pair is more than 185 px apart, so nothing reserves it; it is not in the events (a live session's `ask_parent` text and the session files' own state are not recorded there), so it stays unfitted; the bundled skill does not describe the map. Band size on that set (last recorded moment, one page width each): at 85caa86 / at HEAD 1300 px: 1566 x 1716 / 1446 x 1716; 1600 px: 1620 x 1716 / 1677 x 1716; 2400 px: 2412 x 1716 / 1900 x 1716 (the 1600 px band is 57 px wider at HEAD: columns keep their own width, none under 120 px, where the base cut all to one share).
- engine (ENG-MAP-HIDE-IDLE-BAND): the map drew a band for as long as any lane of it existed, so a band whose lanes had all landed (only `landed earlier:` left) stayed on the page. `layout()` now skips a band with no running lane whose newest lane ended more than two hours ago (`MapOptions.hideBandAfterMs`, default 7200000); a running or recently ended lane brings it back, the one-hour fold into `landed earlier:` is unchanged, and the band of the highlighted lane (`/map/<id>`) is always drawn. Pins: `src/map-layout.test.ts` (3 h idle: one band; 1 h: two; the option; the highlight). README says it; the bundled skill does not describe the map.
- plan pane (ENG-PLAN-PANE-WAITING-POOL-FAILED): three changes. (1) The Waiting tab listed its lines in file order; it now sorts them in the picker's own admission order (priority urgent, high, normal, low, then file order), so the top row is the line that goes next once its wait clears. `describeQueue` gives every pending line `admitOrder` (its place in that order, from the one comparator `planPick` visits lines by), and `queueView` sorts `waiting` by it. (2) The Pool tab label reads `Pool <held>/<budget>` like `Plan <running>/<window>`; the pool was read only while the tab was open, so a refresh with the pane open on another tab now calls `GET /api/pool?counts=1`, which answers `{budget, held}` from one plain pool pass with no 0.6 s measuring window (the open Pool tab's full read gives the counts itself). (3) The Failed header shows the spend of the lines it counts: `✗ Failed (28) · $41.20 (parent $6.10)` sums the ledger rows of every run of each recent failed line (agents' `spend.usd` plus `parent_spend.usd`; a row nested under a same-task row by `parent_session_id`, such as a recon child of the pipeline, adds only its parent spend, since the parent row's `spend` already covers it), and says `k not in the ledger` for lines with no row (they add 0). Pins: `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (Waiting order in `queueView` and on the tab, the label and its counts read, `ledgerTotalsOf` / `failedSpend` / `failedHeading` and the header), `src/queue.test.ts` (`admitOrder`), `src/dashboard.test.ts` (`?counts=1`).
- engine (ENG-PROJECT-DEFAULT-PRIORITY): the pool priority level lived only on a session (`start(priority)`, inherited from a parent), so a project that wants one level for all its tasks had to pass it by hand every time. A project can now keep it in `<project root>/.claude/workflows/settings.json` (a worktree resolves to its main checkout, as the queue does; the workflow loader reads only `*.yaml`, so the file is not a workflow): `{ "priority": "urgent" }`, one key `priority` = `low` / `normal` / `high` / `urgent`. `start()` reads it from the session's cwd when neither an explicit `priority` nor a parent gives one (explicit > parent > file > `normal`), and `wf-run` outside a session (no `--priority`, no `WF_PRIORITY`) takes the same level from its cwd's project instead of 0 and keeps it as `WF_PRIORITY_LEVEL`, so `set_priority` still re-ranks it. A child session still inherits its parent, never the file. No file: unchanged. A file that is not JSON, names an unknown key or a level outside the four makes `start` throw naming the file (a silently ignored typo would run every task at the wrong level); `wf-run` exits 64, and `wf-run --fail-open` prints one stderr line and runs at 0. New `src/project-settings.ts` (`projectDefaultPriority`). Pins in `src/pool.test.ts` (a root takes the file, a worktree resolves, an explicit level and a parent beat it, a bad file throws but is never read under an explicit level; a bare `wf-run` takes it, `WF_PRIORITY` and `--priority` win, a bad file exits 64 / is ignored under `--fail-open`). README, the bundled workflow-authoring skill and CLAUDE.md (the `wf-run.ts` row, a `project-settings.ts` row, the priority-levels paragraph) follow. Deploy note: build + restart every Claude session (the code changes `start`).
- queue (ENG-QUEUE-ADD-DIFFICULTY-REQUIRED): two pending lines queued by `queue_add` carried no `difficulty:` and showed `?????` in the plan pane, with no model-table row. `queue_add` / `wf-queue --add` now refuse a line whose status is pending and that has no `difficulty:` (`ID: difficulty: <1-10> is required on a pending line`); `queue_edit` / `--edit` refuse an edit that leaves a pending line without one, and `queue_mark` `promoted` refuses a backlog line without one. Backlog, `parked`, finished and `model: —` (parent-run) lines need none, and a line already in the file without one stays editable (the refusal is on the write that makes it so). Census at the change: 1 of 130 pending lines without `difficulty:` in the anyparse queue, 0 of 7 in the golf queue.
- driver (ENG-DRIVER-HANDBACK-AFTER-NEEDS-PARENT): the WAITING-only answer to a `[handback-send-enforce]` prompt also caught a driver that had written NEEDS-PARENT as plain text, so the parent never got the hand-back. It now applies only while the driver's last line is a WAITING line; after NEEDS-PARENT, LANDED or CLOSED the prompt IS the request and the driver calls `SubagentHandback` with that line. The "Stop and hand back" heading says `hand back ONE line (SubagentHandback)` instead of `reply`. `agents/driver.md`, `templates/drivers/task-driver.md`, README. Pin: `src/driver-handback-after-needs-parent.test.ts`. Goes live at a restart.
- build (ENG-TS7-MIGRATE): `src/calibration.test.ts` rebuilt `build/` with `npx tsc -p .`, and `.bin/tsc` is the 5.9 `typescript`, so the suite (locally after `npm run check`, and in `publish.yml` before `npm publish`) overwrote the TypeScript 7 build; it now runs `node node_modules/typescript7/bin/tsc -p .`. Pin: `src/typescript7-toolchain.test.ts` (no src test runs a bare `tsc` / `npx tsc`; fails with the old call back).
- build (ENG-TS7-MIGRATE): the engine compiles with TypeScript 7 (the native port): `build`, `dev` and `check` run `node node_modules/typescript7/bin/tsc`, `typescript7` being an npm alias (`npm:typescript@^7.0.2`); the emitted JS is identical to 5.9.3 (only the source maps differ) and `tsconfig.json` needed no change. `typescript` itself stays `^5.9.3`: typescript-eslint 8 peers `<6.1.0` and uses the JS API, `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts`, `src/dashboard-next-unblocked.test.ts` and `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` call `ts.transpileModule`, and typescript-language-server needs `node_modules/typescript/lib/tsserver.js`, none of which 7 ships (`tsgo --lsp` is the 7 language server path). A `typescript` override for typescript-eslint alone does not work (npm hoists 7 over it), so the alias is on the 7 side. Pin: `src/typescript7-toolchain.test.ts` (the scripts name typescript7 and it reports 7; `typescript` resolves to 5.x with `transpileModule`). README says it.
- dashboard (ENG-DASHBOARD-NEXT-UNBLOCKED): the Plan tab's `next:` strip was built from `pickOrder` only, so it stayed empty while an exclusive line ran, unlike the plan pane. When no line is admittable it now lists the lines `/api/queue` marks with `unblockOrder`, in that order (the same rows as the pane); the Ready group is unchanged and nothing is re-derived in the page. Pin: `src/dashboard-next-unblocked.test.ts`. Goes live at a restart.
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-NEXT-HELD-REASON): while an exclusive line ran, the picker held every other line of its repo and Next showed `Next (0)` / `nothing ready`, so the person could not see what ran next. When no line is admittable and lines run, Next now lists what the running lines unblock: the pending lines kept out only by them (overlap, exclusive, exclusive hold, `after:` a running line), in the picker's order (priority, then file order), with the same cap and `+N more` fold; a line still blocked once they end (parked, backlog, `after:` a pending line, parent-run, a pause) is not listed, and `nothing ready` stays when nothing qualifies. The list comes from the picker's own pass over the queue with the running lines done (`describeQueue` marks those lines with `unblockOrder`; the pane re-derives nothing). Pins: `src/queue.test.ts`, `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx`, `src/pane-next-held-reason.test.ts`. Goes live at a restart.
- engine (ENG-SESSION-CLI-LOADER-ONE-OWNER): `session-cli.ts`'s `cliLoader` repeated `index.ts`'s workflow-directory resolution (`WORKFLOW_DIR`, the cwd's `.claude/workflows`, bundled `templates`), two copies of one question; `index.ts` cannot be imported (it starts the server), so the logic moved to the new `src/workflow-dirs.ts` (`resolveDir`, `workflowDirs`, `workflowLoader`) which the server and the CLI both call. `session-cli.ts`'s `main` is split so `cliReport(argv)` returns what the hook prints. Pin: `src/session-cli-loader-one-owner.test.ts` (the CLI's loader equals the server's for a project with a local `.claude/workflows`; `pickup` through `cliReport` judges routes by a workflow only that project declares; neither entry point repeats the resolution).
- takeover (ENG-TAKEOVER-ROUTE-PREDICATE-LEFTOVERS): two spots still used the old route rule. The CLI's SessionStart pickup report (`session-cli.ts`) used no routing-step test, so a worker linked at `work` was called an earlier step's agent at `plan_spawn`; `formatResume` applied the resumed session's test to grandchildren of other sessions. `ResumeResult.isRoute` is now `routeOf(parent)` and `subAgentLines` takes `routeOf`: each child is judged by its own parent's workflow (`Engine.routeTest` is public); `pickupReport` takes `routeOf` and the CLI builds it from an Engine over the server's workflow dirs. Pin: `src/takeover-route-predicate-leftovers.test.ts` (both fail with the change removed). README and the bundled skill do not describe the predicate.
- takeover (ENG-TAKEOVER-EARLIER-VISIT-NOTE): after a restart at `plan_spawn` the resume / takeover text called a worker linked at `work` "an earlier step's agent — not woken", contradicting the LINKED AGENTS block of `status`, which lists it as this visit's. `earlierVisitNote` now uses `listedStanding` (the rule of `linkedAgentLines`): at a `_spawn` state the running agent linked at the state before it is this visit's and gets the wake line. Routing steps between the two states (an exec `plan_route`) are skipped: `ResumeResult.isRoute` (built from the parent frame's state definitions, as for `linkedAgentLines`) goes through `formatResume` / `subAgentLines` / `earlierVisitNote` into `listedStanding`; the storage-only `pickupReport` still passes none. Pin: `src/takeover-earlier-visit-spawn.test.ts` (direct and through an exec `plan_route`; the wake line is asserted; fails with the change removed). README and the bundled skill do not describe the note.
- driver (ENG-DRIVER-ENFORCE-MIDRUN-WAIT): a harness `[handback-send-enforce]` prompt reached a driver while its worker still ran, and one haiku driver complied with NEEDS-PARENT; the re-spawned driver's `status` at `plan_spawn` listed no linked agents (the worker was linked at `work`, and `linkStanding` ignores a link made at another state), so it spawned a SECOND worker on the same session and worktree. `agents/driver.md` and `templates/drivers/task-driver.md` now answer such a prompt, while P has not ended, with the WAITING line only; `linkedAgentLines` also lists, when the parent stands at a `_spawn` state, the running agent linked at the state the parent stood at just before it (exec routing steps such as `plan_route` skipped, the child's session started there; a recon agent or a warm reviewer linked elsewhere is never listed) (`→ live (its session still runs): SendMessage it, spawn no second agent`), and the DRIVER block's prompt says so. README and the bundled `workflow-authoring` skill updated. Pin: `src/driver-midrun-wait.test.ts` (link at `work`, transition to `plan_spawn` directly or through an exec `plan_route`, status lists the agent; an ended, non-spawn-state, recon_spawn-linked or review_warm-linked agent is not listed; both driver files carry the rules).
- queue (ENG-TOUCHES-DIR-ONLY-WARN): under `overlap: files` a directory entry in `touches:` conflicts with nothing, so a directory-only line was admitted beside lines editing files under it, silently. `queue_add` / `queue_edit` now warn for each such entry (`<entry> is a directory: under overlap: files it conflicts with nothing — name the files`); warn only, nothing is refused; `overlap: paths` (default) warns nothing. Pin: `src/touches-dir-only-warn.test.ts` (fails with the warning removed). README says it.
- queue (ENG-PICK-OVERTAKE-EXCLUSIVE-NOTICE): a pick printed only the admitted list, so a same-repo line admitted ahead of a held exclusive line (higher priority, visited first) hid the hold; one exclusive line was overtaken 4 times before anyone noticed. `planPick` now returns `overtakes` and the pick summary (`queue_next`, `wf-queue`) prints `<ID> (<prio>) admitted ahead of exclusive <X> (<prio>)` for each such line. No admission changes. Pin: `src/pick-overtake-notice.test.ts` (fails with the notice removed). README says it.
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-LANDS-BRANCH): the Landed tree read `git log … main` only and only commits with a `Slice:` trailer, so a repo whose lands go to its checked-out integration branch showed `✓ Landed · 14d (0)` after a day of lands, and lands made before the trailer existed were missing. It now reads the queue header's `land_branch:` (served as `landBranch` by /api/queue), else the queue repo's checked-out branch, else `main`, and names the branch in the heading when it is not `main`; a landed task run of the ledger inside the window that git does not show is added, each slice once, of any workflow but only for a line of this queue or its archive (/api/queue serves `archivedIds`). Pins in `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` and `src/queue.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-TASK-LAND-FAILED-TEXT): the README row of `project_land` said `land_timeout` retries once while its `max_visits: 2` allows two visits, each re-running `project_land` (the third move stops through `stop_state`): the prose now says twice. `batch-task.yaml`'s `land_failed` was already driver-safe (a9d2824): it asks the driver for no git command and points to the output above. `.claude/workflows/engine-task.yaml` (untracked, globally ignored, outside this branch) still asks the driver for git in `land_failed` (its exit 4 and 124 bullets) and still has `project_land` `on_timeout: land_failed` and `stop: stopped`; that edit is the parent's: an engine-task `land_timeout` exec on `{{context.branch}}` / the main checkout, and `stop` through `stop_state`. Pin: `src/land-timeout-retries.test.ts` (README's stated retries equal the loaded `batch-task`'s `land_timeout` `max_visits`). `src/ci-browser-red.test.ts` now matches the word `FAIL`, not the substring, so a checkout path that contains `FAILED` no longer reddens it.
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-WAITING-PRIORITY-GLYPH): the priority glyph (`⇈` urgent, `↑` high, `↓` low) showed only in Next, so no arrow was visible while an exclusive line held the repo and every pending line sat in Waiting. `queueView`'s `waiting` rows carry `priority` and the Waiting tab draws the glyph right after the id, one space, as Next does; a normal line keeps none. Pin: `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (a waiting high line shows `ID ↑`, a normal one no glyph). README says it.
- engine (ENG-DRIVER-BLOCK-AGENT-TYPE): the DRIVER block `start` renders named no `subagent_type`, so a dispatcher that copied it spawned a general-purpose agent (full tool set, Edit / Write / Bash) instead of the plugin's `workflow:driver` (workflow tools plus Agent / SendMessage / Read only). The block now carries `subagent_type: "workflow:driver"`; a workflow's `driver:` takes an optional `agent_type` to name another type (a heir inherits it; empty is refused). `driverPrompt` is unchanged: its text is pinned by tests and the agent definition holds the standing rules, not the per-session ones. The default type has no Bash, so the shipped `batch-task` no longer asks its driver for git: the on-tree verify after `work` is the exec `work_check` (the worker's `check` transition; a red one goes to the prompt `work_unverified`: `fix` / `blocked`), `project_land` prints the repo state on exit 4, a land timeout goes to the exec `land_timeout` (retry once when unmerged and clean, else stop), and `stop` goes through the exec `stop_state` whose `repo_state` the `stopped` prompt shows; the quoted agent type is JSON-escaped. A heir that replaces `work`, `project_land` or `stopped` keeps its own routes. Pins: `src/driver-block-agent-type.test.ts` (rendered call, loader, no shell command in a driver prompt, the new execs run on a real repo), `src/batch-template.test.ts`, `src/model-table.test.ts`.
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-MORE-ROW-STAYS): opening a capped list (`+N more` in Sessions, Waiting, Next) moved its toggle row to the list's end. The toggle now stays after the first `cap` lines and the rest list below it. Pin: `src/pane-more-row-stays.test.ts` (toggle row index identical open vs closed). README says it.
- engine (ENG-EXCLUSIVE-HOLD-SAME-PASS): the exclusive hold of `planPick` read only lines running before the pass, so a line admitted earlier in the same pass (empty window, a high line overlapping the exclusive) let later same-repo lines in and the exclusive starved. A line admitted in the pass now counts as running for the hold; `exclusive_hold: no` is unchanged.

- engine (ENG-QUEUE-BASE-REPO): `queue_edit` / `wf-queue --edit` that changes only a line's `repo:` kept the old `base`, a HEAD of the previous repo; an edit that sets `repo:` to another value now re-stamps `base` (and `text_hash`) from the new repo's HEAD, as an edit of text, touches or owner does (a repo that is not git drops `base`). Setting the same `repo:` again, a priority, a model or a note still leaves both. README, the bundled workflow-authoring skill and the `queue_edit` description say so. Pin: `src/queue-base-repo.test.ts` (add, then edit only `repo:`: `base` is the new repo's HEAD).

- map (ENG-MAP-LABEL-SPAN): `columnNeed` counted a loop label's whole width against one column, so a label over a loop spanning several columns widened every column to the full label. The width is now shared by the columns the loop crosses (the narrowest span of a name's loops; one column as before). Pin: `src/map-label-span.test.ts` (fails with the division removed). README and the bundled skill do not describe the column sizing.
- ledger (ENG-LEDGER-PER-AGENT-SPEND): the ledger kept spend per SESSION, so `wf-calibration`'s `$` column stayed `-` on nearly every stage row. `link_agent` now also records each call in the session's `agent_links` (time, the parent's active state, the first model-table stage that state spawns on), under any parent, `driver:` or not; `measureSpend` splits each agent's transcript at those calls (a worker linked again for a fix round counts its fix turns at `fixes`; a call at a state that spawns no stage keeps the stage before it) and writes `spend.by_agent` on the ledger line: `{agent, state?, stage?, model?, usd?, input, output, cache_read, cache_write}` per agent, state and stage, `usd` absent for an agent that could not be read or priced, the rows summing to `spend.usd`. `calibrate` puts each row on the stage row it was linked at (no stage — e.g. a driver linked at the dispatcher's `dispatch` — or a stage the task did not run: a row of stage `-`, never another stage's) and fills the `$` and `$/task` columns (`-` with no record, `>=` when an agent there is unmeasured); the JSON rows gain `usd`, `spent_tasks`, `unmeasured`. A line from before the record has no `by_agent` and keeps `-`. Pins: `src/ledger-per-agent-spend.test.ts` (a worker, a re-linked worker and a reviewer at different states yield rows with their own $ that sum to the task's; stage inherited across a link that spawns none; an unreadable agent is a row without `usd`; a pipeline run end to end through the engine gives `execute`, `fix` and `review` rows of `wf-calibration`).
- engine (ENG-POOL-PARTIAL-CURVE-EST): a flexible exec whose ledger had runs at one size only (a range `curveOf` cannot cover) got no `est_curve`, so its lease carried no `est_ms` and backfill behind it never saw its end. `Estimates.known` lists the sizes with an estimate; the engine's `estimateCurve` and `wf-run --est-by-threads` hand the pool that partial map when no curve fits, and the pool gives the granted lease `est_ms` from it (the size's own time, else the nearest measured size's as is; the larger on a tie). The size pick keeps the `wait_full` fallback. Pins: `src/pool-partial-curve.test.ts` (pool grant, no measured size, `Estimates.known`, an exec; each fails with `partialEstimate` / `known` removed). README and the workflow-authoring skill say it.
- engine (ENG-PRUNE-ORPHAN-CHILD): the terminal-session prune kept a terminal child forever when its parent's session file was gone (deleted by hand). A parent with no session file now counts as terminal, so the orphan ages out as usual; a child of a live parent is still kept. Pin: `src/prune-orphan-child.test.ts` (fails with the missing-parent clause removed). README and the bundled skill do not describe the prune.
- driver (ENG-DRIVER-DOC-ONE-SOURCE): drivers are spawned as `workflow:driver` with `P = <sid>` and follow `agents/driver.md`, never a project's own driver doc, so rules added only there were dead text (a decider's spend was missing from the ledger). Ported into `agents/driver.md` and `templates/drivers/task-driver.md`: `link_agent` of the decider after DECIDED/ESCALATE; the `start`-reply rule; the `WAITING` last line; `[Subagent hand-back]` takes no tokens; probe a silent agent once; no transition in `blocked`/`recon_blocked`/`reject`/`regate_failed`/`triage`, only the parent picks `close`/`stop`/`regate`/...; an "After a restart" section. Pin: `src/driver-doc-one-source.test.ts` (decider section names `link_agent`; fails with it removed). README and the bundled skill do not enumerate the driver rules.
- calibration (ENG-CALIBRATION-BAND-FINAL): `wf-calibration` banned a row by the claim difficulty, so a slice raised to 7+ showed as `4-6`, and put the task's `$` on the first stage row. It bands by the FINAL `difficulty` now; a new `claim` column counts the tasks whose final difficulty is >=2 off the claim. `$` and `$/task` are `-` until a stage's own spend is recorded (ENG-LEDGER-PER-AGENT-SPEND), never a task total on one row. Pins: `src/calibration-band-final.test.ts`, `src/calibration.test.ts`.
- repo (ENG-CHANGELOG-NO-LAND-CONFLICT): every land after master moved conflicted in `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## Unreleased` (two branches each prepending a bullet at the same place) and was resolved by hand. `.gitattributes` now sets `CHANGELOG.md merge=union`: git's built-in union merge keeps both sides' lines, which is right for an append-only list of one-line bullets, and applies to rebase, merge and cherry-pick with no tool or per-entry files. Cost: no conflict is ever raised there, so an edit to an existing entry on two branches yields both versions. Pin: `src/changelog-land-conflict.test.ts` (two branches adding a bullet rebase cleanly; fails with `.gitattributes` removed). README and the bundled skill do not describe the changelog.
