- engine (ENG-POOL-LOAD-SAMPLES, TIME-BUDGET-1112): `pool.log` wrote only `orphan:` / `overrun:` / `starved:` / `backfill:` lines, so 4.5 slot-h per 10 h spent waiting on threads had no record of whether the CPUs were busy. While a lease or waiter exists, a pass 60 s after the last writes one `sample:` line (load, external, held/budget, busy cpus of the leases' trees, leases, waiters with their asked/min threads, the first four). The clock is `last_sample` in `pool.json` (one rewrite a minute, shared across processes; the first pass starts it without a line, an empty pool drops it); the file stays bounded by the existing 1 MiB rotation to `pool.log.1`. Pins: `src/pool-samples.test.ts` (line format; waiter cap; a due pass logs once and moves the clock; a younger sample logs nothing; first pass / empty pool; a future stamp restarts; rotation).
- engine (ENG-POOL-BACKFILL-ESTIMATE, curator 83 P2): backfill never fired: no waiter or lease carried `est_ms` (0 `backfill:` lines in `pool.log` ever; a `wf-run` sets it only with `--est`, the executor skips flexible and shard leases), while 32 of 105 starved waiters since 10-30 would have fit the free threads behind a bigger head (three 1-thread haxe builds waited 15 min with 2 threads free). The pool now records each cleanly finished lease's run time in `pool.json` `history` by its label key (the label with slice ids, task ids and commit hashes replaced by `*`; the last 10 runs of at most 200 keys; `Lease.release({ failed })` skips a failed run, `wf-run` and the exec executor pass it, a lock-only lease records nothing), and an entry with no `est_ms` of its own (a request without `--est`, a lease) takes the p90 of its key's samples as `est_ms` (`estimateOf` in `schedule`): fewer than 3 samples is no estimate, strict order as before. A head whose leases have no estimate is still never passed; `backfill_off` still trips at 2× the estimate, however it was taken. Pins: `src/pool-history.test.ts` (label key, window and p90, strict order without history or below 3 samples, a head over unestimated leases not passed, own estimate wins, `backfill_off` on a history estimate, recording on release and not on failure or lock-only, the reproduction through a real `Pool` with `pool.log` `backfill:`), `src/wf-run-history.test.ts` (exit 0 recorded, non-zero not).
- driver (ENG-DRIVER-SPAWN-UNTYPED): the spawn rule (ENG-DRIVER-NO-GUESSED-TYPE) now says why: the agent a driver spawns drives its own session and needs the general-purpose tools, while `workflow:driver` has no Bash/Edit and `workflow:recon` / `workflow:reviewer` have no workflow tools (a typed worker and reviewer of a META-VAR-NAME-BREAK pipeline could not reach their sessions). Same text in `templates/drivers/task-driver.md` and `agents/driver.md`; pin extended in `src/driver-no-guessed-type.test.ts` (fails with the sentence removed). README and the bundled skill do not enumerate the driver rules.
- engine (ENG-EXCLUSIVE-HOLDS-REPO): an `exclusive: true` line starved: the picker kept admitting non-exclusive lines of its repo beside the running ones, so it started only in a window that happened to be empty (4 exclusive anyparse lines waited from 2026-11-11). `planPick` now lets a pending exclusive line that would be admitted but for running lines of its own repo (its `after:` done, not parked, not parent-run, not backlog) HOLD the repo: no line of that repo that comes after it in priority and file order is admitted while it waits, so the running ones end and it starts; lines of other repos and lines before it are not held, and a hand `queue_mark … running` ignores the hold. `--explain` / `queue_next explain` print `held for exclusive <ID>` on the held lines. The header key `exclusive_hold: no` turns the hold off (`yes` or absent = on); `queue_header({ exclusive_hold: "yes"|"no"|null })` and `wf-queue <q> --header-set exclusive_hold=no` write it (`exclusive_hold=` removes), through `setQueueHeaderKey`, which now takes a string value for this key. Pins in `src/queue.test.ts` (a 5-slot window with 4 running same-repo lines admits only other-repo lines until they end, then the exclusive; the key off = the old behaviour; the lines before the exclusive; a not-admissible exclusive holds nothing; the hand mark; the explain text; the key's set / remove / refuse; the CLI) and `src/tools.test.ts` (the tool). README, the bundled workflow-authoring skill, `templates/batch.yaml` and the `queue_next` / `queue_header` tool descriptions follow. Goes live at a restart.
- calibration (ENG-CALIBRATION-DATA-EMPTY): `wf-calibration` put a staged pipeline's review rounds and all tokens on the first stage row (recon), so review/execute/fix rows read `rounds 0`, `tokens 0`. Rounds now land on the `review` row; a token key naming a stage goes to it, `worker` to execute (else fix, plan), the remainder to the first row (the ledger holds no per-agent tokens or spend, so `agent_ids` cannot split finer). New `$` and `$/task` columns carry the task-level `spend.usd` on the first row (`-` when unmeasured, never 0); findings go to the review row. `--help` added (`--since` already existed). Pin: `src/calibration-data-empty.test.ts`. README and the bundled skill do not describe the row layout.
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-PRIORITY-NO-PAD): a Next row padded its id to the widest shown before the priority glyph, so a short id was followed by a run of spaces. The glyph now follows the id after one space (`<ID> ↑`); no other row padded. Pins: `src/pane-priority-no-pad.test.ts` (short id beside a long one), `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (the Next row).
- engine (ENG-POOL-FLEX-BY-ETA, TIME-BUDGET-1112): a flexible waiter chose its size by `wait_full` (a fixed window) and the room free at that instant, never by when the running leases end; a flexible lease had no `est_ms`, so `headAt` could not see when it ends — 29 of 30 lands ran 1 shard (suite ~650 s at 1 shard, ~240 s at 3). Now a flexible request carries the step's estimated run on every size min..max (`est_curve`: the ledger's p90 per size, a size without runs read off `t(k) = a + b/k` fitted through the sizes that have them — `Estimates.curve`, `curveOf` in `src/estimate.ts`; the executor's `ExecContext.estimateCurve`; for `wf-run`: `--est-by-threads K:SEC,…`) and `schedule()` takes, for each size k, `start(k) + estimate(k)` — start now when k fits, else `headAt` of k over the running leases' estimated ends — and the size that ends earliest (the larger on a tie): now on the free room, or it waits as the head for exactly that size, closing the threads behind it. A size whose threads free at an unknown time is not a candidate; behind another head only a size ending before the head starts is backfilled. The granted lease carries that size's `est_ms`, so the next waiter sees when it ends. No estimate for some size: the `wait_full` rule as before (`wait_full` is not used when a curve is present). `wf-run` prints a note and falls back when `--est-by-threads` cannot cover the range. Pins in `src/flex-eta.test.ts` (waits when more threads free soon enough; starts now when they do not; unknown-end leases not waited for; no/incomplete curve falls back; tie; behind a head; an exec carries the granted size's estimate), `src/estimate.test.ts` (`curveOf`, `Estimates.curve`) and `src/pool.test.ts` (the CLI flag). The gate's own flexible rows (anyparse `devkit.Lease`) must pass `--est-by-threads` to use it.
- engine (ENG-POOL-WAIT-FULL-FROM-LOCK, TIME-BUDGET-1112): a flexible waiter's `wait_full` window ran from its arrival, so a land that queued 10 min on lock `anyparse-land` found the window long over and took its min threads (one shard) the instant the lock freed — 29 of 30 lands over a 10 h night. A waiter that asks for locks now stores its window as `full_ms` (pool.json) and `schedule()` stamps `full_until = now + full_ms` on the first pass where its locks are free (not held, not claimed by a waiter ahead), then drops `full_ms`; a waiter without locks is unchanged (`full_until` = arrival + window). `acquire` wakes at the stamped window's end. README and the workflow-authoring skill say it. Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-RESTORE-REARCHIVE): `wf-queue <q> --restore <ID>` did not keep an old done line: the next pick's auto-archive (`archive_after_days`) read its `ended:` (older than N days) and moved it out again. A restore now writes `restored: YYYY-MM-DD` (today) on a done / failed line, and the archive counts a line's age from the later of `ended` and `restored`, so the line stays N days after the restore; `ended` stays as archived (the plan pane and the cost days still read it). `restored` is removed with `ended` by a mark out of done / failed, and read-only for `queue_add` / `queue_edit` like `ended`. README, the `queue_next` / `queue_add` / `queue_edit` tool descriptions and the key list in `src/queue.ts` name it. Pin in `src/queue-restore-rearchive.test.ts`.
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-PRIORITY-AFTER-NAME): the priority glyph of a Next row (`⇈` `↑` `↓`, a blank cell for normal) now follows the id instead of preceding it, the ids padded to the widest shown so the glyphs form one column; the two-line form puts the glyph after the id on its second line. The dashboard's plan rows already had it after the id. Pins: `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (glyph after the id and aligned, none before the id, the narrow form, the fold rows).
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-LANDED-ROW-BROKEN): every Landed row read `????? ◆ — ?    -09:31` (no start time, no $, no difficulty bar): `ledger.jsonl` grew past 4 MiB, the plugin API rejects a `$.fs.read` over that, and the pane's read fell back to an empty ledger. Not caused by a rename or the rollup; the same code at 6995bf0 reads the same. `Storage.appendLedger` now also rewrites `ledger-recent.jsonl` (task and parent-spend lines that ended within 30 days, cut to the fields the pane reads, capped at 3 MiB by dropping the oldest lines, tmp + rename, under the ledger lock; the live ledger's last 14 days alone are over 4 MiB uncut) and the pane reads it, falling back to `ledger.jsonl` when absent. Open: the file appears at the next ended session, so the live pane stays broken until then; the bundled skills do not enumerate ledger files. Pins: `src/plan-pane-landed-row.test.ts` (a real `claude plugin test` pane over an over-4 MiB ledger shows start time and $), `src/ledger-recent.test.ts` (window, and over 4 MiB of fresh lines the file stays under the cap with the newest row's `spend.usd` and `started_at`).
- driver (ENG-DRIVER-NO-GUESSED-TYPE): a driver spawned a recon as `workflow:recon` on its own guess; that read-only type has no workflow tools, so the session was never driven. The "Spawn ONE worker/reviewer" rule in `templates/drivers/task-driver.md` and `agents/driver.md` now says: pass `subagent_type` only when the prompt names one, omit it otherwise, never choose a type by the role's name. Pin: `src/driver-no-guessed-type.test.ts`. README and the bundled skill do not enumerate the driver rules.
- dashboard map (ENG-MAP-NARROWER): the live map was wider than its content: every column took a fixed 230 px and the plaque stood 80 px past the last station. A band's columns are now as wide as its content needs (`columnNeed` in `src/map-layout.ts`: the station names, the branch chips, the first four steps of a branch with their labels, the loop labels; the cap stays 230 px, the floor 120 px) and the plaque gap is 56 px, so nothing is cut that was not cut before. On the 7-station test fixture the map is 1650 px wide instead of 1890 (columns 194 px); the branch chip text (`sonnet ▮▮▮▯ high · worker`) is the width that binds. Pinned in `src/map-layout.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-HEADER-KEYS): the header key `archive_after_days` (ENG-QUEUE-AUTO-ARCHIVE) had no tool that writes it, and the queue may not be edited by hand. `queue_header` now takes `archive_after_days` (a whole number ≥ 1, or `null` to remove the key) beside `batch_size`; `batch_size` and `reason` are optional but go together, a call may set the window, the key or both, and a call with neither is refused. `wf-queue <q> --header-set archive_after_days=N [--reason TEXT]` does the same (`archive_after_days=` removes; the reason is echoed, not written, since the header's `reason:` is the window's). Both run under the queue lock through `setQueueHeaderKey` (`src/queue.ts`); a key outside `SETTABLE_HEADER_KEYS` (`batch_size` and `reason` included: the window has its own form) and a value that is not a whole number ≥ 1 are refused, nothing written. Setting the key leaves `batch_size`, `reason` and every other header key as they were. Pins in `src/queue.test.ts` (set, change, remove, unknown key, bad value, the window untouched; the CLI) and `src/tools.test.ts` (the tool). README, the bundled workflow-authoring skill and CLAUDE.md (the `queue.ts` / `queue-cli.ts` rows, and the "Session cwd and ids" row now lists `WF_PRIORITY` / `WF_PRIORITY_LEVEL` / `AGENT_CONTEXT`) follow.
- engine (ENG-AGENT-CONTEXT-ENV): an exec state gets env `AGENT_CONTEXT`, the context size in tokens (input + cache read + cache write) of the last assistant turn of the agent `link_agent` recorded for the exec's session, read from that agent's transcript (`lastTurnContext` / `agentContextOf` in `src/spend.ts`: only the transcript's tail is read, widened until the last turn's own lines are whole, a turn counted once at its largest counters) when the exec spawns. Empty when no agent is linked or its transcript cannot be read; a re-linked agent gives the new agent's value; an inherited `AGENT_CONTEXT` is dropped like `WF_THREADS`. Why: a slice worker crossed 250k tokens inside one visit with nothing checking it; an exec can now route a hand-off to a fresh agent (`[ "${AGENT_CONTEXT:-0}" -gt 250000 ]`). Pins in `src/agent-context.test.ts`. README and the workflow-authoring skill name it.
- plan pane, ledger (ENG-PANE-LANDED-SPEND): the Landed heading carries money, `✓ Landed · 14d (434) · $1234 · parent $310 (25%)`. `$1234` is the sum of the ledger's real `spend.usd` over the landed lines the count counts (each line once; a line with no spend row adds nothing; no `unpriced` marker; no figure when no line is measured), `parent` is the top-level Claude Code session's own spend over the same 14 days, only of the conversations of the queue's repo (a session of another project adds nothing) and `(25%)` its share of the landed sum (no share without a landed sum; no `parent` while the ledger holds none). The parent's spend was in no ledger line, so the engine records it: a task's ledger line (a line with a `task`, of a session that knows its conversation) gets `parent_spend` `{conversation, project, from, to, offset, usd, input, output, cache_read, cache_write}` = the conversation's own turns (`<projects>/<project>/<conversation>.jsonl`, not its sub-agents'; `project` is that directory's name, which the pane matches to the queue repo by Claude Code's naming, every character but a letter or digit a dash: `projectDirOf`) priced by the same `turnUsd` table as an agent's turn (`measureParentSpend`, `src/spend.ts`; no second price table). A conversation's lines tile its time: each starts just after the latest `to` the ledger already holds for that conversation (the first at the session's start), so a turn is in one line, and the transcript is read from the byte `offset` the previous line recorded (a complete-line boundary; a half-written last line waits for the next read, an offset past a rewritten file reads from the top), so a task's end costs the new turns, not the whole conversation; ledger appends of ended sessions run one at a time so the stretches cannot overlap. A transcript that cannot be read, an unpriced model or an empty stretch records no `parent_spend` (the next line covers the stretch). Open: the parent's turns after the last task end of the window are in no line yet, and parent spend before the first recorded task of a conversation is not counted. The pane's section headers are cut with `…` at the pane width instead of wrapping. Pins: `src/parent-spend.test.ts` (window, once-only tiling, the read starting at the recorded offset with the earlier part no longer read, a half-written line, unreadable / unpriced / empty, the engine writing the lines), `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (`landedUsd`, `parentUsd` with a foreign-project stretch, `projectDirOf`, `ledgerParentOf`, `landedHeading` with the 25% share arithmetic, the rendered heading, a narrow pane). README and the workflow-authoring skill say so.
- plan pane (ENG-PANE-MORE-EXPAND): the `+N more` rows that end a capped list expand on a click (the pane's mod API takes input: `Button` with `onPress`, as the section headers already use). Next's `+N more can start (dependencies done), in the picker's order`, the Waiting tab's `+N more` and the Sessions tab's `+N more` are one fold row (`moreRow`): closed (`▸`) by default, open (`▾`) it lists every folded line after the shown ones in the picker's order, each in the same row form (bar, kind, priority glyph, id; Waiting: its reason), and a second click caps the list again. The state is in the `folded` atom (keys `next-more`, `waiting-more`, `sessions-more`, all in `FOLDED_BY_DEFAULT`), so it survives a refresh and a reopened pane; the one-line / two-line form of Next is measured over the rows drawn. New export `foldFlipped` (also used by the section headers and `+N older`). Pins in `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (each fails with the expansion code removed): Next closed row and open list in the picker's order with the glyph column, survival over a 15 s refresh and a remount, Waiting's open and closed list, `foldFlipped`; the Waiting cap test now reads `▸ +N more`. The Sessions row shares the mechanism and has no render pin of its own (the test harness serves one session file).
- engine (ENG-EXCLUSIVE-PER-REPO): an `exclusive: true` line excluded every other line of the queue, also lines of another repo (a running exclusive anyparse line blocked all engine lines). It now overlaps only the lines of its own repo (the line's `repo:`, else the queue's project); a line of another repo is admitted by the usual rules, an exclusive line of another repo waits for nothing but its own repo. README, the workflow-authoring skill and the `queue_next` description say so. Pin: `src/exclusive-per-repo.test.ts` (own repo refused, other repo admitted, exclusive of another repo admitted).
- plan pane, dashboard (ENG-PRIORITY-ICON): a queue line's priority is a coloured one-cell glyph instead of `[priority]` text: urgent `⇈` red, high `↑` orange, low `↓` grey, normal no glyph. In the pane's Next rows the glyph sits in a fixed cell before the id (a blank cell for normal, so ids stay aligned; the two-line form puts it after the kind mark); the dashboard's plan rows use the same glyphs with colour tokens `--prio-urgent/high/low`. Exports `prioGlyph` / `PRIO_GLYPHS` (`hooks/plan-pane.tsx`). Pins: `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (glyph map, no `[priority]` text, blank cell for normal, narrow form), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-AUTO-ARCHIVE): the queue archive is automatic and has no agent tool. A queue header key `archive_after_days: N` (written by hand in the header; absent = no archiving, other projects unchanged) makes every pick (`queue_next` / `wf-queue`, not a dry run), under the pick's own lock and before admitting, move the done / failed lines that ended over N days ago to `<queue>.archive.yaml` by the rules `archiveQueue` already had (a failed line an open line's `after:` names stays, a line with no `ended:` stays); the summary says `archived K old line(s)`; a non-numeric value refuses the pick. The MCP tool `queue_archive` is removed (21 -> 20 tools; the driver agent's tool list drops it too, 21 -> 20 `mcp__plugin_workflow_wf__*` entries; the pane's icon map, README, CLAUDE.md and the workflow-authoring skill no longer name it); the manual forms stay on the CLI: `wf-queue --archive --older-than <d> [--include-unended] [--dry-run]` and `--restore <ID>`. Pins in `src/queue.test.ts` (pick archives before admitting; no key = none; dry run = none; bad value refuses and writes nothing) and `src/tools.test.ts` (no `queue_archive` tool, none in `agents/driver.md`).
- engine (ENG-LEDGER-SPEND-ROLLUP): a pipeline's ledger `spend` missed the agents of its child sessions: the terminal-session cap (`pruneTerminal`, `prunePolicy`) deleted each ended child before the pipeline finalized, so `subtreeOf` no longer saw it. A terminal child whose parent is not itself terminal (running, or not yet written) is now kept by the prune; it becomes prunable once its parent ends. Open: a child whose parent file was deleted by hand stays. Pin: `src/ledger-spend-rollup.test.ts` (real Storage prune + `subtreeOf` + `measureSpend`; fails with the filter removed).
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-ARCHIVE-TAILS): three tails of the queue archive. (1) `wf-queue --convert old.md --into <queue>` ignored the archive, so a converted line could take an archived id (two files holding one id); `rewriteQueue` (and the `--dry-run` form) now refuses a line it adds with an archived id, `A is already in <queue>.archive.yaml (archived)`, as `queue_add` does; a line a crash left in both files stays readable. (2) New `wf-queue <q> --restore <ID>` (`restoreQueue`; CLI only, no MCP tool): moves one id from the archive back into the queue, whole, with its status and `ended:`, under the lock, queue first then archive (a line already in both files loses only the archive's copy; an id the archive does not hold is refused), so an open line's `after:` on an archived failed line has a way back. (3) After a crash left a line in both files, the re-archive skipped the append and kept the archive's stale status and text; the queue's copy now replaces the archive's when they differ (identical copies still leave the archive byte for byte). README and the bundled workflow-authoring skill list `--restore`. Pins: `src/queue-archive-tails.test.ts` (convert refuses an archived id, re-archive keeps the fresher copy, restore moves a failed line back and the waiter is a plain queue line again, restore of an id in both files, an unknown id refused, the CLI form, no `queue_restore` tool).
- engine (ENG-CI-BROWSER-RED): CI went red on the real-browser tests of `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts` (`ENOENT … DevToolsActivePort`): the opt-in was `CHROME_BIN` alone, and the GitHub runner image exports it, so the tests ran against a Chrome that does not start in that sandbox. The opt-in is now `WF_BROWSER_TESTS=1` plus `CHROME_BIN`; a Chrome that exits or never writes its port file fails with `Chrome (<path>) did not start: …` instead of a raw ENOENT. The launch keeps `--use-mock-keychain --password-store=basic`. Pin: `src/ci-browser-red.test.ts` (CHROME_BIN alone skips; opted in with a Chrome that cannot start gives the clear error).
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-BASE): a queue line records where it was written. `queue_add` (and `wf-queue --add`) writes `base` (the commit HEAD of the line's repo: `repo:`, else the queue's project) and `text_hash` (the first 12 hex digits of the sha256 of text + touches + owner, `textHashOf` / `lineTextHash` in `src/queue.ts`); `queue_edit` rewrites both, at the HEAD of that moment, only when the edit changes the text, touches or owner (setting the same brief again, a priority, a model or a note leaves them). A repo that is not git (or has no commit) leaves `base` out with no error, `text_hash` is still written. Both are known state keys (no unknown-key notice): `queue_add` / `queue_edit` refuse them as input, a mark keeps them, a line written before has neither and stays valid (readers treat it as unknown), an older reader ignores them as unknown keys. `GET /api/queue` lines carry `base` / `textHash` when present. The line's `base` is not the header's `base:` list of repos. README, the bundled workflow-authoring skill and the `queue_add` / `queue_edit` descriptions list the keys. Pins: `src/queue.test.ts` ("queue line base: and text_hash:": add writes both, the line's own repo, not git, an edit of text / touches / owner rewrites and priority / model / note / the same brief do not, a line written before stays unknown until its brief is edited, a mark keeps both and both refuse them as input, `wf-queue --add` / `--edit`). The anyparse consumer is `dev recon-facts` (RECON-FACTS-EXEC).
- map: a plaque's difficulty bar is drawn exactly into its room (`textLength` + `lengthAdjust: spacingAndGlyphs`, `BAR_FIT` in `src/map-layout.ts`), on lane plaques and the next: strip: the font's `▰▱` are wider than the average glyph the room is measured with, so the kind mark after the bar sat on its end. Pin in `src/map-layout.test.ts`; not looked at in a browser.
- plan pane: a Landed day row no longer says `· k/n measured`; it shows the sum (`—` when no line is measured) and `incl. N failed, M closed`. User 2026-11-10: the count was noise in the pane. Pins in `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` follow.
- engine (ENG-PANE-COST-TAILS): four tails of the plan pane's Landed days. (1) `daySpends` matched a failed or closed line to a day by `DD.MM` alone, so a line that ended a year ago on the same `DD.MM` added to today's sum; it now compares the full date (`daySpends(lands, spends, now)`, the year read by `landMs`). (2) A day folded while it was today flipped open the next day, since the fold list held a flip of the default (`lands-DD.MM`) and the default moved at midnight; the list now holds an explicit `+lands-DD.MM` / `-lands-DD.MM` for a day the person clicked (`landDayOpen`, `landDayToggled`; the `folded` atom is shape `folded-2`, so an old list is dropped). (3) `landTwo` and the cost column measured the rows of folded days, so a hidden long row could put the visible ones on two lines; only the rows of open days are measured. (4) The task-notification row scanned all 14 days of lands for its slice; `recentLand` scans only the lands within two days of the agent's own time (`seen.at`, the refresh that last saw it; the lands are newest first), so a row drawn days later still reads `✓ landed`. The refresh drops a signed fold key whose day left the 14-day window (`pruneLandFolds`; a failed git read, with no lands, drops nothing). Pins: `src/pane-cost-tails.test.ts` and, under `claude plugin test`, `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx`.
- engine (ENG-MAP-NEXT-STRIP-QUEUE): the live map's `next:` strip merged the ready lines of every queue into one nameless row, so a ready line could not be told from another queue's. Now each queue has its own row, `next: <owner>/<file>` (named the way the band headings name it, rows sorted like the bands, `MapOptions.next` items carry `queue`, the file a line was read from; `dashboard/map.js` passes it; `MapLayout.next` is a list of `MapStrip`, each with `queue`); a line with no queue keeps the bare `next:`. And a root session with its own task and children no longer counts as a dispatcher whose children become lanes: a dispatcher is a taskless root with children, or a taskless session whose children have tasks; a root with a task is one lane and its children are its branches. Pins: `src/map-next-strip-queue.test.ts` (two queues give two named groups, one queue one named row, a tasked root with a child is one lane), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (map.js passes the queue file).
- engine (ENG-LEDGER-SPEND-TAILS): a ledger line's `spend` missed a respawned agent: `link_agent` overwrote the session's `agent_id`, so the first worker's transcript was counted only as `unnamed`. The session keeps every agent it was linked to (`SessionState.linked_agents`, appended by `Engine.linkAgent`, kept by an exec's routed write beside `agent_id`), and `agentsOf` (`src/spend.ts`) reads all of them, an agent named twice once, so each turn is summed once. The `link_agent` description, README and the bundled workflow-authoring skill say so. Pins: `src/spend.test.ts` (a session with a respawned worker sums both transcripts and equals an independent sum from the raw token counts; linked as the latest only, the first is lost), `src/agent-link.test.ts` (a respawn joins `linked_agents`, linking again adds nothing), `src/suspend.test.ts` (a link during an exec survives the routed write). Not run on a live ledger line (the live state directory is out of this slice's reach).
- engine (ENG-DASH-DATE-PICKER-DARK): the dashboard's native controls were drawn light on the dark page: the Cost tab's `from` / `to` date inputs rendered a white field, and their calendar popup a white month grid with a grey frame, because the page never told the browser its theme (a native control follows the CSS `color-scheme`, default light). `dashboard/tokens.css` `:root` declares `color-scheme: dark` for the whole page (so also selects and scrollbars, now or when added) and `dashboard/index.html` carries `<meta name="color-scheme" content="dark">` so the first paint is dark too. The Timeline tab's day navigation (`.tl-days`) is plain links, not native controls, so nothing there changed. Pins: `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (the `:root` declaration, the meta, no stylesheet or page turns a light scheme on, the date inputs are in the page), `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts` (opt-in by `CHROME_BIN`: the computed `color-scheme` of the page and both inputs is `dark`).
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-ARCHIVE): a queue file no longer has to grow without end. New `queue_archive({queue?, file?, older_than_days, include_unended?, dry_run?})` and `wf-queue <q> --archive --older-than <days> [--include-unended] [--dry-run]` move `done` and `failed` lines whose `ended:` is older than the cutoff to `<queue>.archive.yaml` beside the queue (`archivePath`), appending under the queue's lock, each line moved whole. A line with no `ended:` stays unless `include_unended`; a failed line an open line's `after:` names stays. The pick, `queue_add`'s checks and the cycle check resolve an `after:` id through the archive (`Queue.archived`, id to status; done passes, failed waits as `X (failed, archived)`); `queue_add` refuses an archived id, `queue_mark` names it archived. The archive is written before the queue, so a crash between leaves a line in both and the next run removes it from the queue only. On a copy of anyparse's queue (1223224 bytes, 646 finished lines, none yet dated): `--older-than 0 --include-unended` gave a queue of 196419 bytes and an archive of 1026812, the pick unchanged, 0.56 s. Pins: `src/queue.test.ts` ("queue archive": archived `after:` admitted, archived failed waits and a named failed line stays, re-add refused, a second run moves nothing, a moved line equal byte for byte, unended and dry run, the age edge, the crash heal), `src/tools.test.ts` (the tool is registered and not read-only).
- engine (ENG-PANE-FAILED-RECENT): the plan pane's Failed section no longer grows without end. `queue_mark` writes a new task key `ended` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, local) when a line reaches done or failed and removes it when the line leaves those (a retry, so the next end is dated again); `queue_add` and `queue_edit` refuse to set it (it is known state, so no unknown-key notice), `/api/queue` carries it. The pane's Failed lists the lines that ended in the last 2 days, its header and the `failed` count are of those, and one dim row `+N older` (folded) opens the rest; a line with no `ended` counts as older. Pins: `src/queue.test.ts` (ended written on done and failed, removed by retry, refused by add and edit), `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (today recent, 3 days ago and undated older, the edge at 2 days, the `+N older` row).
- engine (ENG-MAP-PLACESTEPS-CAP-PIN): two holes in `src/map-layout.ts` closed. (1) `placeSteps`'s loop starts at the cap (`Math.min(cap, steps.length)`, `cap` = room / `G.stepMin` + 1); with the cap removed from the loop start (`m = steps.length`) the suite stayed green, a crowded branch of short names then drew more steps than its room allows. Pin: `src/map-layout.test.ts` ("never more than the cap, even when every name is short enough for more": 20 one-letter steps give 5 at 200 px and 3 at 120 px); the mutant fails it. (2) `kindMark` tested `k in KIND_MARKS`, so a `kind` naming a prototype property gave a function (`constructor`) or an object (`__proto__`) instead of the glyph (the kind is lowercased first, so `toString` already read as feature); it uses `Object.hasOwn` now, as `taskLineOf` beside it does, and such a kind reads as feature `◆`. Pin: the `kindMark` test in `src/map-layout.test.ts`; `k in KIND_MARKS` fails it.
- engine (ENG-PANE-AGENT-ROW): an Agent call of a workflow pipeline drew the harness's row (`⏺ workflow:driver(drive ENG-X) Haiku 5.5`) with the plugin's line beside it only after the session was linked, and the link was never found live: the hook read the agent's id from a text reply, while the stored result of an Agent call is an object (`agentId`, `resolvedModel`). Now such a call draws ONE line in the style of the engine-call group's line instead of the harness's row: `⚙ [ENG-X] driver · haiku 5.5 · <workflow> @ <state> › <leaf state> <age> lane ↗` (role, the queue line's id, model, state, age, lane link). Before the session is linked the line is read off the call's own input (`workflow:*` subagent type, description `drive <ID>`, model alias); after the pipeline ended, `ended at <state>` or `✓ landed <sha>`. A call of no workflow, an expanded row and a failed or interrupted spawn keep the harness's row; the call's input is never rewritten. A spawn cannot join the tool group's line (the harness folds only reads, searches and listings), so it is the next line, drawn alike. New exports `spawnOf`, `agentIdOf`, `modelOf` (`hooks/plan-pane.tsx`). Tests: `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (linked, not yet linked, no workflow, expanded, failed / interrupted, the three readers). Takes effect after a Claude restart.
- engine (ENG-MAP-COLLAPSE-REPEATS): the live map folds a lane's repeated rounds (a dispatcher looping `pick` -> stations -> `slot_free` dozens of times drew a full row per round). Consecutive rounds of one shape (the same stations and exec verdicts, the same branches by workflow, outcome and states, the same back-edge name; durations do not count) draw as the first round, its loop reading `↺ slot_free ×64 · 1m–3m`. A round that differs (a red exec, another path, another branch) stays and splits the run; a round with an ask, a branch still running, and the running (last) round are never folded. A click on the loop opens the group and folds it again; the open groups live in `sessionStorage` (`wf-map-open`) and in `MapOptions.open`, keyed `<session>:g<first round>`, so a live update and F5 keep them. `MapPass.index`, `MapLoop.group`, `MapOptions.open` are new. Pins: `src/map-layout.test.ts` ("repeated rounds ...": 5 identical rounds draw one with ×5, a red round gives two groups, the running round, the open state and a live update, the lane height, the click target), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (the page's click handler and storage).
- engine (ENG-TURN-RATES): `TURN_RATES_USD_PER_MTOK` (`src/calibration.ts`) priced Opus at $15/$75 and Sonnet / Haiku at older rates, so a ledger row's `spend`, the Cost tab and the pane overstated (the same usage of one line: $9.62 against $3.46). The table is keyed by model id now, the longest key contained in the id wins (`claude-opus-5-5` $4/$20, `claude-opus-4-8` $5/$25, `claude-opus-4-1` $15/$75, `claude-sonnet-5-5` $2/$10, `claude-sonnet-4-6` $3/$15, `claude-haiku-5-5` $.10/$.50, `claude-haiku-4-5` $1/$5, Fable 5.1 and later $10/$50 with cache reads at $.25, Fable 5 the same with cache reads at $1), cache read and 5-minute write at each model's own rate, a 1-hour write at twice input; a bare family key (`opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`, `fable`) prices an unknown id of a known family as that family's newest, and an unknown model stays unmeasured. Rates are the Anthropic list as of 2026-10-06; Haiku 5.5's prompts over 100K tokens ($.50/$2.50) are not modelled. Pins: `src/turn-rates.test.ts`, `src/spend.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-PANE-COST): the plan pane shows money. Each Landed and Failed row carries the line's cost in $ right after the kind glyph (the difficulty bar stays first), from the ledger's real `spend.usd` summed over every run of the line; a line any of whose runs recorded no spend, or with no ledger row, shows `—`, never `$0.00`. Each day row of Landed carries the day's sum next to its count: the day's landed lines plus the failed and closed lines whose latest run ended that day (a line counted once), says `incl. N failed, M closed` when it includes them and `· k/n measured` when not every line is measured (`— · 0/n measured` for a day with none). Landed now covers 14 days (the Cost tab's range; `git log --since=14 days ago`, up to 2000 lands) instead of 24 hours; today is open, the older days start folded, and the status band's `✓ n` still counts the last 24 hours (`Snapshot.recent`). The cost is in the section's own one-line / two-line form (ENG-PANE-ADAPTIVE): a figure is right-aligned to the section's widest, never cut, and the id is cut before it; a day header is cut to the width. New in `hooks/plan-pane.tsx`: `ledgerSpendOf`, `daySpends`, `dayText`, `usdText`, `landMs`, `dayOf` (the snapshot shape is `snap-7`; `Land.usd`, `FailedLine.usd`, `DaySpend`, `Snapshot.days` in `types/index.d.ts`). Pins in `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx`: a row with and without a cost, a day sum over landed, failed and closed lines, a partly measured day and one with none, a narrow pane (the cost kept, the id cut), the 14-day window and folded older days, the band's 24 hours; `src/plan-pane-adaptive.test.ts` keeps its widths with the cost column.
- engine (ENG-PANE-ADAPTIVE): the plan pane's rows no longer wrap at the terminal's edge. A row too long for the pane (`bodyColumns`, measured in cells with wide glyphs counted) fell to column 0 for that row alone (Running `review_spawn › read 3m`, Landed `CANON-3e-1-EXPLICIT-WRAP-NO-SOURCE`). Now a section lays out as one: when every row fits, the one-line form stays; when ANY row of Running, Next, Landed, Failed, the Waiting tab or the Sessions tree does not, EVERY row of it takes two lines, the second indented (Running: bar, kind, id / state, next step, age; Next: bar, kind, priority / id; Landed: bar, kind, times, sha / id; Failed: bar, kind, id, date / reason; Waiting: bar, kind, id / reason; Sessions: workflow and task / state and age). A text still too long for its line is cut with `…` (every first line too: Landed's times and sha, Next's priority, Failed's id and date). The height caps count lines: a two-line Waiting or Sessions row takes two of the pane's rows (half as many rows show, `+N more` counts the rest), and Next's share of the pane is computed from the lines Running and Next take. A Failed row's full reason is measured now, so a section with one long reason is two-line (the one-line form cut the reason to fit before). `fitsOneLine` in `hooks/plan-pane.tsx` decides; the width is read at each refresh, so a resized pane flips the form. Pins: `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (Failed, Next, Waiting, Sessions: one line when fit, two when one is too long, the width change) and `src/plan-pane-adaptive.test.ts` (Running and Landed in the real `claude plugin test` harness: all fit, one too long, the flip 60 / 38 / 60 on the same rows, a 22-cell pane; Waiting's height cap in `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx`).
- engine (ENG-MAP-GROUP-PROJECT-QUEUE): the live map reads top-down: bands are ordered by project, then queue (a project's sessions with no queue last), then workflow, and every heading reads `project › queue · workflow`. A `session_start` event and a seeded session carry `queue` (`context.queue_file`), a later change of it logs a `queue` event; a child takes its nearest ancestor's. A dispatcher started under a conversation (a taskless session whose children have tasks) is now a dispatcher: before, it drew as ONE lane with every queue line as its branch; now each line is a lane named by its id, with its own children as branches.
- engine (ENG-PANE-POOL-TAB): the plan pane has a Pool tab (`4`) beside Plan, Sessions and Waiting: budget, held threads, load, external load, locks, the `STARVED` note, each lease (what it holds, time held, `OVERRUN ~N`; project, session, step, label cut to the pane width) and each waiter in admission order with its wait and `STARVED`. It reads the dashboard's existing `GET /api/pool` (no new route, no second reader of `pool.json`) and costs nothing while closed: the call is made only by a refresh whose open tab is Pool (the pane is open, a queue session) and once on a press of the tab or when the pane opens on it; pins count the fetches on every other tab and with the pane closed, and a pane opened on the Pool tab reads with its first refresh (`paneOpen` is set before that refresh). One call is about 0.6 s on the engine (the pool's measuring window) with two `ps -A` runs of about 25 ms. Every line is cut to the pane width (the flags keep their cells); the pool read is the last step of a refresh and a pane opened on the Pool tab comes up about 0.6 s later. When only `/api/pool` fails the tab shows one dim `Pool: …` line; with the dashboard down the queue fails too, the pane drops to the Sessions tree with the red `queue: …` line and no tab row, and the pool is not called (pinned), as is a `ui.open` that throws (`paneOpen` reset, no later pool call). `src/dashboard.test.ts` holds the pane's fixture of the answer (`hooks/fixtures/api-pool.ts`) to the answer's keys.
- engine (ENG-DASH-TIMELINE-STRIPES-HANG): the Timeline tab's legend re-declared `.tl-seg` as `position: static`, so its pool-wait swatch (`.tl-wait`, absolute, `left/right: 0`) was laid out against the page and painted its diagonal stripes over the title, tabs and text. The "hang" is that layer too: measured in headless Chrome on a day of 18 sessions with a pool wait each, with the rule as it was all 11 controls (title, tab links, day links) sat under the full-viewport `.tl-wait` and took no clicks; `/api/timeline` answered in about 3 ms and the page showed no long task or re-render loop, so it is not a paint or refresh cost and does not depend on the row count. The legend swatch is `position: relative` now. Pinned in `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts` (opt-in, `CHROME_BIN`: the legend's `.tl-wait` stays inside its 14x10 swatch, `elementFromPoint` at the centre of each control returns the control, at first render and after several ticks, no long task over 200 ms) and, without a browser, in `src/timeline-stripes.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-LEDGER-REAL-SPEND): a ledger line records `spend`, the real cost of the task: at the session's end the transcripts of every agent that worked on it (the session's `link_agent` id and the context keys `agent_id_<stage>` / `<role>_agent`, over the session and its descendants) are read, each turn priced by its own model at input, output, cache-read and cache-write rates (new `TURN_RATES_USD_PER_MTOK` / `turnUsd` in `src/calibration.ts`, beside the blended table), a turn once and only inside the task's window (new `src/spend.ts`). An unreadable transcript or unpriced model leaves `usd` absent and the id in `unmeasured`: unmeasured, never $0. `buildCost` and the Cost tab take that figure when present, else the old `tokens*` estimate. Agents no key or link names are counted in `spend.unnamed`; a zero-usage `<synthetic>` turn costs $0.
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-KIND-ICON): a queue line's `kind` shows wherever a line or a task lane is drawn, right after the difficulty bar (the bar stays first): bug `※`, research `⚑`, feature `◆`, text glyphs, none an emoji; a line with no `kind` reads as feature, and a lane or row with no known queue line keeps a blank cell of the same width. One helper, `kindMark` beside `difficultyBar` in `src/map-layout.ts`, answers the glyph. `GET /api/queue` lines carry `kind` (always, `feature` when absent) and `mark`; the plan pane reads `mark` (`QueueView.marks`, shown on the Plan, Waiting, Landed and Failed rows; a land not in the queue is blank), the dashboard's Plan tab (rows, side card, `next:` / `landed earlier:` strips) calls `Layout.kindMark`, and the live map takes a lane's glyph from the queue line its `task` names (`MapOptions.kinds`, fed by `dashboard/map.js`; `MapPlaque.kind`, `next:` / `landed earlier:` items carry `kind`). The plaque's room for the bar grows by the glyph's cell, the 340 px limit holds. Pins: `src/map-layout.test.ts`, `src/queue.test.ts`, `src/dashboard-style.test.ts`, `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx` (the recorded `/api/queue` copy now carries `kind` and `mark`).
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-KIND): a queue task has an optional key `kind`: `bug`, `feature` or `research` (absent or `-` = `feature`, today's behaviour). `queue_add` / `queue_edit` refuse any other value as they refuse a bad `difficulty` (`kind: "x" is not one of bug, feature, research`), and the pick does not admit a hand-edited line with one. `queue_next`'s admitted line carries `| kind: <kind>` (always, `feature` when absent) and the exec form `wf-queue` prints it as a seventh column, so a task workflow can read it the way it reads `difficulty`; the workflow steps that use it are not part of this change. `TASK_KEYS` / `KINDS` in `src/queue.ts`, README and the bundled workflow-authoring skill list the key.
- engine (ENG-MAP-PLAQUE-DIFFICULTY-BAR): the live map's lane plaques draw the difficulty bar first (`▰▰▰▱▱` from `difficultyBar`, in the difficulty colour even when the plaque's border is a state colour), then the id, state and age; a lane with no known difficulty draws no bar and keeps the width blank, so ids stay aligned. The 340 px plaque limit holds: the bar's room comes out of the id's. `MapPlaque` carries `bar` / `barC`; the `next:` strips use the same `BAR_W`. Pins: `src/map-layout.test.ts` (bar on a done / running / asking lane, blank width, a long id inside the limit; `placeSteps` keeps as many steps as fit, killing a loop that starts at 3).
- engine (ENG-CHECK-IGNORE-PER-ENTRY): the `touches:` ignore check failed open: `git check-ignore --stdin` is fatal on one entry past a symlink, and the whole repo's set was dropped, so every git-ignored entry was admitted as tracked. `gitIgnoredSet` (`src/queue.ts`) now keeps what git printed and asks one entry at a time about the rest (exit other than 0/1), so a bad entry costs only itself. Pinned in `src/queue.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-BATCH-TRANSITION-LIMIT): a long batch campaign hit `Max transitions (1000) reached` mid-dispatch and even `stop` was refused. Bundled `batch` now has `max_transitions: 100000`. A move that only closes the run is exempt from the cap everywhere (agent transition, skill-gate auto-move, action route): its target is terminal, or no route leads back to it and one leads on to a terminal (`isClosingMove`, `src/stations.ts`), so `stop` -> `report` -> `finish` works at the cap while a loop move is still refused. Within 5% of a workflow's cap the status / transition reply carries `WARNING: N of M transitions used ...` (`capWarning`) naming what to do. Pinned in `src/engine.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-BATCH-TASK-READONLY-SPAWNS): bundled `batch-task`'s `review` spawns its reviewer as `workflow:reviewer` (Agent `subagent_type`), the read-only type; batch-task has no recon spawn. That type has no workflow tools, so the reviewer follows no `file-review` session any more (no `start`, no `review_session`, no `link_agent` for it) and ends with a `SubagentHandback` call carrying the `VERDICT` line. Its Write tool is limited to scratch space, and the log dir `<worktree>.log` sits beside the repo, so the reviewer's `review.md` moves to `/tmp/wf-batch-logs/<ID>/review.md` (the fix round, the decider and the `lessons` copy read it there; a re-spawned driver at `review` has no current reviewer and always spawns a fresh one, whatever review.md holds; `report.md` / `handoff.md` stay in `<worktree>.log`). Pinned in `src/batch-template.test.ts` (the spawn, and the review path through `writeToolReason`) and `src/driver.test.ts`. A heir of `batch-task` that overrides `review` switches by its owner.
- engine (ENG-READONLY-GATE-TAILS): two tails of the read-only gate. (1) The rewrite's `permissionDecision: allow` no longer refuses a grep / rg filter behind a pipe because of a flag's value: `hxq lint a.hx | grep -C 3 x` (also `-A`, `-B`, `-m`, `-e`, `rg -g`, `rg --context N`…; not `grep -d` / `--directories` or grep's `--context`, which can read files) is judged by the words that are not a flag's value (`searcherFileOperands`, per tool: grep and rg differ in which letters take a value), and `-e` / `--regexp` supply the pattern, so a file operand is still found (`grep -C 3 x f`, `grep -T x f` stay refused). (2) Two forms that passed are refused: nested braces (`{{},-i}f`: `matchingBrace` counts nesting, but not escaped or quoted braces) and a redirect whose descriptor word is not whole (bare `>&1foo` writes the file `1foo`; `2>&1foo` and `>&-foo` write none in bash but are refused as well; `>&2`, `>&-`, `>&2-` stay duplicates). README, the `workflow-authoring` skill and the pins in `src/read-only-gate.test.ts` say so.
- engine (ENG-DASH-COST): a Cost tab (`/cost`): per finished task of a day range (today by default, `from` / `to` date inputs) its outcome (landed = completed through a `land` state, `land_failed` not counting / closed = completed without landing / failed / unknown = completed with no visits recorded), review rounds, tokens and USD, and the stages-as-run each with its model, tokens and USD; the range's totals and $ per landed; a bar trend of $ per landed over the last 14 days; a note with the price table and the date it was updated. `GET /api/cost?from=&to=` returns `buildCost` (`src/calibration.ts`). Prices are new in the engine: `PRICES_USD_PER_MTOK` (blended USD per million tokens by model family) with `PRICES_UPDATED` in `src/calibration.ts`, the one owner; a model matching no family counts tokens at no price. Only ledger lines with a `task` are pipelines. Tokens show per key: `tokens_<stage>` at that stage's model, `tokens_worker` as one unsplit row at the task's model, the rest of the total as a `-` row; a line with no tokens is unmeasured (`n/a`) and stays out of the USD-per-landed denominator. Pins: `src/calibration.test.ts`, `src/dashboard.test.ts`, `src/dashboard-style.test.ts`, `src/dashboard-routes.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-DASH-POOL): a Pool tab (`/pool`): budget, held, load, external, held locks, the pool-wide STARVED line, each lease (pid, threads/locks, priority, time held, project, session linked to `/session/<id>`, step, label, OVERRUN plaque) and each waiter in admission order with its wait and STARVED plaque, refreshed every 2 s. `GET /api/pool` returns `poolSnapshot(await pool.measuredStatus())`, the data `wf-run --status` / `pool_status` print, through the pool module (`poolSnapshot` in `src/pool.ts`; `buildApp` takes an optional `Pool`). Pins: `src/pool.test.ts`, `src/dashboard.test.ts`, `src/dashboard-style.test.ts`, `src/dashboard-routes.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-TIMELINE-LEDGER-SEGMENTS): a ledger line records `segs`, the session's state stretches (`{s, a, b, w?}`, epoch ms, `w` = pool waits flat), and the Timeline tab draws a pruned session from them (children with their own lines as sub-rows); a line from before stays one undivided bar. Readers tolerate the field (`parseLedger` takes any JSON line; no reader lists keys). Pins: `src/timeline.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-DASH-TIMELINE): a Timeline tab (`/timeline`, `/timeline/<YYYY-MM-DD>`): one bar per task pipeline of the day, segments by state from the session's history and its child sessions' (sub-rows), coloured by stage group (recon / plan / work / gate / review / land / wait: a name match, `work` by default, `$STATE_DIR/timeline-groups.conf` overrides a state), pool-lease waits hatched, hover = state, start, duration, an hour axis. `GET /api/timeline?day=&root=` (`buildTimeline` in `src/timeline.ts`) reads the session files and the ledger (`Storage.readLedger`; `parseLedger` moved to `src/ledger.ts`); a pruned session is one undivided ledger bar. A sub-workflow `push` history entry now carries `to` (the sub-workflow's first state) beside `workflow`, so every state's entry time is in the history; the ledger's `transitions` / `visits` ignore it. The Plan tab's `overlaps X` reason adds `(ready, admitted ahead in the same pass)` when X is a ready line, not a running one. Pins: `src/timeline.test.ts` (push `to`, segments, waits, ledger, API), `src/engine.test.ts` (push entry), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (timeline render, plan wording), `src/dashboard-routes.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-DASH-QUEUE-GRAPH): the Plan tab's rows are clickable: a side card shows the line's full text and a mini dependency graph (dagre, as the workflow graphs) of the lines its `after:` names and the lines that wait for it, each node coloured by state (done / running / ready / waiting / failed); a node click selects that line. The card is sticky beside the list (below it on a narrow window); the graph model is the pure `queueGraph` / `queueNodeKind` in `src/map-layout.ts`, fed by `afterIds` on `/api/queue` lines (`describeQueue` resolves them with the picker's tokenizer, which `afterCycle` shares, so a known id inside parentheses draws no edge). A link inside a row is not swallowed. Pins: `src/map-layout.test.ts`, `src/queue.test.ts`, `src/dashboard-style.test.ts`, and a Plan case in `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts` (opt-in by `CHROME_BIN`).
- engine (ENG-MAP-BAND-TAILS): three tails of the map's bands. Sessions in git worktrees of one repo are one band: `projectNameOf` (`src/pool.ts`, on `projectRoot`, the queue's own resolution) names the project, stored in the session context at start (`context.project`, so a removed worktree keeps its checkout's name), for the event log's `session_start` and for `/api/sessions` (a `project` field `seedEvents` reads), so `wf-SLICE` worktrees no longer split a workflow into one band each. Station names are fitted to their column like the steps (`label` and `title` on a band's stations; the SVG text carries a `<title>` with the whole name when cut), so long names no longer touch, and README's "every text of a band" holds. `placeSteps` is exported and its count-shrink loop is pinned. Pins: `src/pool.test.ts`, `src/map-layout.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-MAP-ID-PIN): the `/map/<session id>` path of the scroll work has a pin, and F5 on the map no longer lands on another lane. A reload returned the reader by pixel offset into a first scene drawn from the session files, which is shorter than the one the event log completes (landed lanes of pruned sessions come on top), so the page clamped the offset and the reader landed on a different lane. The map now saves the top lane's key and its offset with the position (`noteScroll` in `dashboard/stable.js`, merged into the `pagehide` record) and puts that lane back from the first scene on, again for each scene until the event log is in; a wheel, touch, key or click of the reader ends it. Found by the new pin: the glide to a `/map/<id>` lane was cut short by the next scene (the status line's "live" text shifted everything by one line, and the anchor hold's instant scroll cancelled the smooth one), so the lane ended off-centre; `.map-status` now keeps its line height and a scene that lands during the glide re-aims it. The glide's window (one second) opens only at its start, a re-aim does not renew it, and the reader's wheel, touch, key or click ends it. Pins (`src/dashboard-browser.test.ts`, opt-in by `CHROME_BIN`): F5 returns to the same lane when the log makes the scene taller, and to a lane only the log has; `/map/<id>` with log-only lanes above the target ends centred; a stream of events never glides a `/map/<id>` reader back to the lane, and a wheel before the log lands leaves the view where the reader put it; `/map/<id>` opens with the lane centred and highlighted, a reader at the bottom is not carried to a new lane, the pill counts the event and returns to the plain map, F5 keeps the reader's place and does not scroll to the lane again.
- engine (ENG-ASK-PIN-CLOCK): the pin "the same text written again while open is one ask" (`src/event-log.test.ts`) no longer depends on the millisecond boundary: the two writes happen at different, fixed times (fake `Date`), and the same ask logged again at a later time is folded, so the pin fails against a fold that keys an ask on its time. No engine change.
- engine (ENG-START-REPLY-ID): `start({ …, reply: "id" })` answers `SESSION: <id> @ <state>` without that state's prompt, the TRANSITIONS list and the workflow rules (a `driver:` workflow's DRIVER block still follows); a parent that starts a child only to hand it to its own agent no longer receives, and re-reads, a prompt that agent reads with `status`. Only `"id"` is accepted; omitted, the reply is unchanged. Pin: `src/tools.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-LEDGER-STAGES-RAN): a ledger line's `stages` lists only the stages a state read: when the context holds `stages_first`, its rows are the whole list, so a stage absent from the line did not run (a session closed at the plan no longer shows execute / review rows from the final difficulty). Without `stages_first` every `model_<x>` / `effort_<x>` pair still stands. `stages_first` now also records the execute and review spawns that read the bare keys (`stagesSpawned` in `src/model-table.ts`: `context.model` / `context.effort` in a prompt state = execute, `context.review_model` / `context.review_effort` = review; an exec's env reads none), and the bundled `batch-task` claim prompt no longer prints `model` / `effort`, so claiming records no stage. Pins: `src/calibration.test.ts`, and a `batch-task` heir run through the engine in `src/model-table.test.ts` (execute at work, review at review, neither at claim).
- engine (ENG-DASH-STYLE-TAILS): the dashboard has ONE difficulty plaque, `difficultyBar` in `src/map-layout.ts` (the pane's five-cell `▰▰▰▱▱`, `?????` when unknown), drawn first in every Plan-tab row and `next:` strip item and in the map's `next:` / `landed earlier:` rows (the old `d6` flag, `planDifficulty` and the unused `ui.difficulty` pill are gone). A session whose active step is a state tagged `ask: parent` reads `waiting for parent` in yellow (`st-wait`) in the session list, the detail and the plan's Running rows (yellow dot, no glow); README says so. Pins: `src/dashboard-style.test.ts`, `src/map-layout.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-STATIONS-WARN): `station: true` on a failure terminal, or on several terminals, no longer passes silently: the loader writes one stderr line naming the workflow and the states, and the stations ignore the bad mark (several marks: all of them). `stationWarnings` in `src/stations.ts`; pinned in `src/stations.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-UNKNOWN-KEYS): a task key this server does not know (written by newer code, e.g. `priority:` for an older server) no longer makes the whole queue unreadable: it is kept on write, inert for the pick, and named in `queue_next` / `queue_mark` replies (`unknown key priority on X: ignored by this server`). `queue_add` / `queue_edit` still refuse an unknown key in their own input. Tests: `src/queue.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-TOUCHES-TRACKED): a repo-bound queue line whose `touches:` entry is git-ignored in its repo (`git check-ignore -q`; a live `.claude/` workflow has no copy to pin) is parent-run for the pick: not admitted, and `queue_next`'s skipped list names the path. `queue_add` / `queue_edit` (and `wf-queue --add/--edit`) warn on such an entry and on a file entry whose directory does not exist. A hand mark is unaffected. Tests: `src/queue.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-MARK-RUNNING-ADMIT): `queue_mark` to `running` (pending→running; `wf-queue --running`) refuses a line the picker would not admit (overlap, `after:`, `exclusive`, `breaking`, `parked`) and names why; `force: "<reason>"` (`--force`) overrides and writes the reason into the line's notes. The window, a pause and `model: —` do not count: a hand mark is how parent-run lines start. Retry is unchanged. Tests: `src/queue.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-PANE-FAILED-LIST): the plan pane's Plan tab has a `✗ Failed (n)` section after Landed, FOLDED by default (fold key `failed`; a click unfolds it): every `failed` queue line, newest first, as `bar id DD.MM reason`. The reason is the first line of the line's last note (a failed mark writes its reason there; `no note` when it has none), cut to the pane body width; the date is that note's (`date unknown` when the line has no dated note, such lines last). `/api/queue` lines carry `lastNote` (that first line) beside `lastNoteDate`; the pane snapshot shape is `snap-5`.
- engine (ENG-BREAKING-LAND): a task key `breaking: true` (a file format or protocol change with no compat for running processes): the picker admits it only into an empty window and admits nothing else while it runs. The hand-back to the parent (`NEEDS-PARENT reason=breaking land ready`) is a route of the project's task workflow, not the engine's; bundled `batch-task` lands a breaking line as usual. Pins in `queue.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-PRIORITY-SURFACES): the dashboard Plan tab and the plan pane list ready lines in the picker's order (priority, then file order) with the priority beside the id (`/api/queue` lines carry `pickOrder` and a non-normal `priority`); the `queue_add` / `queue_edit` descriptions name `priority` and its refusal. Pinned by a fixture queue, a `renderPlan` case and a mounted-pane assertion.
- engine (ENG-READONLY-ENV-ALLOW): `read-only-commands.yaml` takes `envAllowed`, a list of variable names a `NAME=value cmd` prefix may set for a read-only agent (`APQ_TEST=unit.x node bin/test.js`); the reader behind it is judged as usual, an unlisted name is refused, and `GIT_*`, `LD_*`, `DYLD_*`, `PAGER`, `PATH`, `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `IFS` stay refused even when listed.
- engine (READONLY-AGENTS-REPORTS): `workflow:recon` / `workflow:reviewer` get a Write tool that a PreToolUse(Write) hook limits to the task's report directory (absolute path in scratch space with a `<task>.log` / `<task>-logs` directory, outside any git work tree, no `..`), so a task workflow can have them write recon.md / review.md while read-only stays a guard (a write in the worktree is refused, pinned). No Edit tool. Workflows' own spawns switch by their owners. Takes effect after a Claude restart.
- engine (ENG-READONLY-LIMITS): the read-only gate says what it is — a guard against ACCIDENTAL writes, not an adversary; the guarantees (no Edit/Write, the project's CLI read-only env, the allow list) and the named out-of-scope paths are in the README, the read-only agents' prompts and the hook header (now describing the allow model and `read-only-commands.yaml`). New refusals, each pinned: brace expansion (`{a,b}`, `{1..3}`), `printf -v`, `$` in a command word (`$D/ls`), a variable or glob as a redirect target, and the descriptor-less writes `>&file` / `<>`. The `permissionDecision: allow` on a rewritten call (env export, pool wrap) needs every segment to be a project `read` CLI, a no-file default or a stdin filter behind a pipe without a file operand (`unharmlessSegment`; never behind `xargs`, never with `--files0-from`, a numeric grep word is an operand); a call that needs the rewrite beside any other segment is denied with `run it in its own call` (an unallowed rewrite is refused by auto mode; the env is not dropped silently). Tests: `src/read-only-gate.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-PANE-LANDED-TIMES): the plan pane's Landed list is a tree: a foldable `▦ DD.MM (n)` row per day (fold key `lands-DD.MM`, stale keys dropped), under it `bar start-end sha id` per land. The difficulty bar is drawn first on every pane row that has one. Start and difficulty come from the ledger, which now records `task` (the session's `slice`/`task`); `?` when a land has no ledger row. Needs a Claude restart.
- engine (ENG-PLAN-TO-QUEUE): a multi-task plan reaches the task queue. `planning` states two levels (a TASK is tested, reviewed and landed on its own and leaves main green; a STEP shares its task's gate) and writes a `## Tasks` table (`id`, `after`, `touches`, `difficulty`) plus the steps under each task; the Workflow section gains a last `Tasks: <N>` line (the Resume line stays byte-exact, the start hook's five-line window unchanged). master's `route_execution` reads it: one task runs in the plan's execution workflow as before, two or more go to the new `enqueue_plan` (checks `touches` / `after`, `queue_add`s self-contained lines, sets the window, then `batch` or a project's batch heir; `replan` returns to planning; a task with one unverifiable `touches` path gets none; a heir of batch, found by `extends: batch`, keeps its header; a failed pass parks its lines); `batch`'s `plan_queue` resumes a queue handed over in context `queue_file`. `src/templates.test.ts` pins the plan section, the master routes and a two-task plan's pick.
- engine (ENG-AGENTS-PAYLOAD-FACT): the read-only gate's payload is a measured fact: a live `workflow:recon` agent's write-class Bash call was refused, so the hook payload carries the qualified `agent_type`; the pre-rename `workflow:wf-recon` / `workflow:wf-reviewer` entries are removed from `DEFAULT_READ_ONLY_AGENTS` (README states the probe).
- engine (ENG-AGENTS-NO-PREFIX): the shipped agent types lose the `wf-` prefix (the plugin namespace says whose they are): `agents/driver.md`, `recon.md`, `reviewer.md`, spawned as `workflow:driver` / `workflow:recon` / `workflow:reviewer` (after a Claude restart). The read-only gate compares the payload `agent_type` exactly against the qualified defaults `workflow:recon`, `workflow:reviewer`: a bare `reviewer` or `other:reviewer` is not caught; a project's `agents` list still names its own.

- engine (ENG-MAP-BANDS-TASKS-ONLY): the live map draws bands for task workflows only: a workflow-level `map: false` (schema, `extends`, `/api/workflows`) keeps a childless root session, a conversation, and a dispatcher's child lane of such a workflow, off the map; `master` and `file-review` carry it. `session_start` events carry `project` (the cwd's directory name); a workflow whose sessions run in several projects is one band per project, titled `<workflow> · <project>`.
- engine (ENG-DASH-SCROLL-STABLE): the dashboard refreshes in place and holds the view. `dashboard/stable.js` `patchHtml` morphs new markup into the live nodes (sessions, workflows, a session's detail, the plan: scroll, focus, text selection and animations survive the 2 s refresh, where `innerHTML` replaced them); the live map no longer scrolls on an ordinary event (follow mode scrolls to the bottom only when a NEW lane appears and the reader was at the bottom), keeps the lane at the top of the view where it is while content above it changes (the scene jumps instead of sliding for that frame, the scroll moves by the lane's shift), and the pill scrolls down itself; F5 returns to the same scroll position on every view (`pagehide` -> `sessionStorage`, restored once the first content stands). Pins: `src/dashboard-browser.test.ts` runs the block view and the map (no scroll on an event, new lane only from the bottom, top-lane anchor, F5 at mid-page and at the bottom) in a headless Chrome when `CHROME_BIN` names one (opt-in; `npm run check` skips it).
- engine (ENG-MAP-ASK-ANY-STATE): the live map records a question to the parent from any state, not only a state tagged `ask: parent`: a driver sets the context key `ask_parent` (one `context_set`; `context_set` history lines now carry the key as `detail`) at a NEEDS-PARENT hand-back, and `openAsk(session)` (`src/event-fold.ts`) reads the open question off the session alone — open while the key holds text and no move or end came after the write that set it. The session's next move or end (the parent's own `transition` on a child: a regate, a close) or the key cleared to `""` is the `answer`; a stale key is ignored. `seedEvents` seeds an open ask so a restart keeps the lane lit; `fold` keeps the question's text and merges a driver's ask with an `ask: parent` state's own. The map draws an open ask glowing with the question as its tooltip. The bundled driver instructions set the key before NEEDS-PARENT and clear it on resume.
- engine (ENG-MAP-LABEL-OVERLAP): the live map draws no text over another text: a branch chip sits on its own row above its steps (a second branch under one station no longer lands on the first one's step labels) and is cut to its branch; a branch keeps as many steps as leave every name clear of its neighbours; the parent rail's label, questions, answers and the user question take the first free text row above the rail (two asks at one station stacked); whatever is cut keeps its full text as a tooltip (`title` on the scene element, a `<title>` in the SVG). `src/map-layout.test.ts` measures every text of a band and asserts no two rectangles intersect at four widths, labels on and off.
- engine (ENG-DASH-MAP-V1): the live workflow map, version 1 (`/map`, `/map/<session id>` highlights a lane): one band per workflow kind on its stations, lanes from a start dot to their plaque (landed on top, folded after an hour into `landed earlier: … +N`; running below with a faint tail over the stations ahead; `next: … +N` from the queue), child sessions as branches under the station they ran at with `model ▮▮▮▯ effort · role` chips and done / live / ahead steps, review loops as a dashed line back to a second pass, red stops for a replaced or stopped agent and a failing exec, the driver chip (the workflow's `driver:` model, now in `/api/workflows`), the parent rail with `ask: parent` question / answer links and the dispatcher's own question as a glowing mark, the fit rules. The picture is `layout(fold(events))`: `fold` moved to the pure `src/event-fold.ts` (re-exported by `event-log.ts`; a move seeded and logged counts once, agents keep their link time, step and where they ended, exec ends their duration) beside `seedEvents` (a session file's events, so the map survives a server restart), and `src/map-layout.ts` (layout + keyed scene); both are served to the page, `dashboard/map.js` reconciles the scene into one SVG with ~300 ms keyed transitions, follows SSE (2 s poll fallback), keeps follow mode with a `↓ N new events` pill. `session_start` names a slice's `slice` as its task. Plan pane: `map ↗` in the header, `lane ↗` on a pipeline agent's row, `/wf-map` prints the URL. Tests: `src/map-layout.test.ts` (a fixture event log: loop, stop, ask/answer, landed fold, fit rules, seeding — positions), `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (the served modules import and run, map.js on the tokens), `src/dashboard-routes.test.ts`, `hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx`.
- engine (ENG-DASH-STYLE): one style for the whole dashboard, on the live map's look: `dashboard/tokens.css` (surfaces, text, state / model colours, the difficulty scale, Inter-else-system fonts, no web-font download) is the only file with a colour literal; `dashboard/components.css` holds each component once (plaque, agent chip, step dot, script square, section label, band panel, `next:` / `landed earlier:` rows) and the page builds every view (sessions, session detail, workflow graphs, plan) from the `ui` helpers. States read the same everywhere; dagre graphs take the palette (plaque nodes, square exec nodes, glowing current state, dashed backward edges). Behaviour and routes unchanged. `buildApp` takes an optional route-table path so `GET /routes.js` is testable. Tests: `src/dashboard-style.test.ts` (every view rendered by the page's own script against a stub document: no colour literal outside the tokens, every class defined, tokens match the spec, offline; `GET /routes.js` pinned, 200 as a module and 404 when missing).
- engine (ENG-DASH-ROUTES): one router for the whole dashboard on real paths with the History API (`src/dashboard-routes.ts`, served as `/routes.js`): `/sessions`, `/session/<id>`, `/workflows`, `/workflow/<name>`, `/plan` (`/` is the sessions view); links are anchors, pushState/popstate drive the tabs, back/forward work. The server answers every non-`/api` GET that is not a static file with `index.html`; an unknown `/api/...` stays a JSON 404. The `#session=<id>` hash code is gone (no compatibility); the plan pane and README link to `/session/<id>`. Tests: `src/dashboard-routes.test.ts`, `src/dashboard.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-MAP-STATIONS): a workflow's stations for the live map (`src/stations.ts`): the shortest happy-path route from the initial state to the success terminal (the farthest terminal that is not `outcome: fail`, or one marked `station: true`), exec states marked as scripts; a state's `station: false|true` overrides. `/api/workflows` carries them as `stations: [{name, script, terminal}]`. Pinned on fixture copies of the slice-pipeline, engine-task and slice-worker shapes.
- engine (ENG-READONLY-NO-REWRITE): the read-only gate no longer rewrites an allowed call it need not: auto mode refuses a call a hook rewrote (`updatedInput`) without `permissionDecision: "allow"`. An allowed call passes UNCHANGED (no hook output) unless a segment starts with a project `read:` CLI (the only readers that read the exported env; wrappers looked through); for those the `export <env>;` rewrite now carries `permissionDecision: "allow"` like pool-gate (every segment was already proved a declared reader), and so does ANY pool-gate `wf-run` rewrite of an allowed read-only call, with or without a project reader. Tests: `src/read-only-gate.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-PRIORITY): a task takes `priority: urgent|high|normal|low` (default normal; `-` = normal). The pick admits ready lines by priority, then file order; the reasons stay in file order. `queue_add` / `queue_edit` accept the key and refuse other values; `queue_next` shows `priority` on an admitted line when it is not normal. Pins in `src/queue.test.ts`: a high line far down is admitted before a normal one on top, equal priorities keep file order, add/edit validation.
- engine (ENG-EVENTS-LOG): an append-only event log with a global sequence per server run (`src/event-log.ts`), derived at the one place every session write passes (`Storage` / test storage `observe(prev, next)`): `session_start` (workflow, parent, task, difficulty), `transition` (from, to, name, ts), `exec_start` / `exec_end` (+ shard results), `agent_link` (agent id, role = the session's root workflow, model + effort in force), `agent_respawn` (a different agent id for a session that had one), `agent_stop` (an abandoned session that had an agent: ended without a hand-back), `rescore`, `session_end`, and `ask` / `answer` (state name only) on entering / leaving a state tagged with the new optional YAML key `ask: parent`. Served as `GET /api/events?since=<seq>` (`{run, events}`) and SSE `GET /api/events/stream` (`?since=` or `Last-Event-ID`, both `<run>:<seq>`, another run's position = 0; replay then live; covers the dashboard-holding process only). Retention is run-scoped: a file `events/run-<stamp>-<pid>.jsonl` under the state dir (the 20 newest kept), 5000 events in memory. `fold(events)` rebuilds a per-session picture. Tests: `src/event-log.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-QUEUE-YAML): the task queue is YAML only — default `.claude/batch/queue.yaml`; header keys (`batch_size`, `reason`, `overlap`, `base`, any other) and `tasks:` of `{id, status: pending|running|done|failed|backlog, difficulty, model, effort, repo, workflow, after, touches, owner, exclusive, parked, text, notes: [{from, date, text}]}`. Every queue tool, `wf-queue` and the picker read and write it under the same lock, editing the parsed document in place (key order, comments kept). `queue_add` takes a `task` object; `queue_mark` gains `promoted`/`shelved` (backlog ↔ pending) and writes notes to `notes:`; `queue_header` writes `batch_size` + `reason`. `wf-queue --convert <old.md> [--into q.yaml] [--status S]` converts the Markdown queue once (the old line parser lives only in `queue-convert.ts`) or appends a second file's open lines; `--convert <x.json>` does the configs, which are now YAML: pool `config.yaml`, `pool-gate.yaml`, `read-only-commands.yaml`. MIGRATION before deploy: convert every live JSON config and queue (`wf-queue --convert <x.json> --into <x.yaml>`, `--convert <q.md> --into <q.yaml>`; README § Task queue) — a leftover JSON config is ignored with one stderr line naming that command. The converter refuses an unreadable open task row (line numbers), names a skipped done row, accepts `- [m] ⏸ ID | …` and `- [m] ID prose` rows, appends under the queue's lock and reports the count (none = error). The plan pane reads `/api/queue` instead of parsing the file and has its first tests (`hooks/plan-pane.test.tsx`, `npm run test:pane`, part of `npm run check`); the engine replies it reads are a recorded fixture `src/tools.test.ts` checks. Tests: `src/queue.test.ts`, `src/queue-convert.test.ts` (a real queue excerpt), `src/tools.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-READONLY-ALLOWLIST): the read-only gate is an ALLOW list: the first word of every pipeline segment of a `wf-recon`/`wf-reviewer` Bash call must be a declared reader (`DEFAULT_READERS`: ls, cat, head, tail, wc, rg, grep, jq, find without actions, git log/show/diff/status/grep/blame/… and the like, plus the project config `read` list), `$( )`/backticks/process substitution are refused, and the write list stays as the more specific message. Closes `perl -i`, `python -c`, `node -e`, `tee`, `dd`, `cp`/`mv`, `find -delete`. Projects must list their read-only CLIs in `read`. Tests: `src/read-only-gate.test.ts`.
- engine (ENG-AGENT-TYPES): the plugin ships subagent types in `agents/`: `wf-driver` (haiku/low; the task-driver rules, workflow tools listed by name, no Edit/Write/Bash), `wf-recon` (sonnet/medium) and `wf-reviewer` (opus/high), the last two read-only by tool list AND by a new PreToolUse(Bash) hook `hooks/read-only-gate.sh` (`read-only-gate.ts`): for those agent types a Bash write is refused (git write subcommands, file writers, `sed -i`, redirects outside scratch dirs, `sh -c` bodies, wrappers `rtk`/`env`/`xargs`/`nice`/… judged by the command they run) and the project's `.claude/read-only-commands.yaml` (`write`: leading words or `/regex/`; `env`: exported before the command, e.g. `APQ_READ_ONLY=1`; `agents`: the type names) extends it. Measured: a subagent PreToolUse payload carries `agent_id` + `agent_type`. The read-only hook owns the one rewrite of those agents' calls (it runs the pool gate itself via `pool-gate-run.ts`; `pool-gate-hook` skips them). Takes effect after a Claude restart. Tests: `src/read-only-gate.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-DASH-PLAN): web dashboard Plan tab. `GET /api/queues` lists the queue files sessions name in `context.queue_file`; `GET /api/queue?file=` (404 for an unnamed file, 422 for a bad queue) answers every line with, for pending ones, the picker's real reason. `planPick(queue, {ignoreWindow})` skips "window full"; `describeQueue` builds the answer. The tab: running (pipeline state, time since entering it, link to `#session=<id>`), ready, waiting with reasons, failed, done today. Tests: `src/queue.test.ts`, `src/dashboard.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-LEDGER-STAGES-AS-RUN): the ledger's `stages` are the rows each stage ran on — recorded (first value wins) when a state reading `context.model_<x>` / `effort_<x>` is entered, so a later rescore does not rewrite them; the line gains `claim_difficulty` (the first difficulty the table saw) beside the final `difficulty`, and `wf-calibration` bands by the claim. Tests: `src/model-table.test.ts`, `src/calibration.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-STAGE-NAMES-OPEN): model_table stage names open — any `^[a-z][a-z_]*$` except model/effort/step/reached (e.g. `recon`); the ledger's `stages` records every `model_<x>`/`effort_<x>` pair and wf-calibration follows the recorded names. Tests: `src/model-table.test.ts`.
