- engine (ENG-CALIBRATION-REPORT): `wf-calibration [--since D] [--project P] [--findings-key K] [--json]` folds `ledger.jsonl` per project x workflow x difficulty band x stage (tasks, review rounds, escalations, tokens, outcomes, verdicts; a line without stage data counts under `-`). Ledger lines gain `project`, `visits` (entries per state), `verdict`, `plan_handoff`, `agent_ids` (`agent_id_<stage>`) and `counts` (length of each context list). Tests: `src/calibration.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-DIFFICULTY-INTERACTIVE): bundled `coding` and `bug-fix` take a `difficulty` (at start, or set at the plan state `think` / `diagnose` from a rubric of anchors 2..10 by the work, +1 for a stale measurement or a hypothesis) and declare an `execute` + `review` stage table; their implementer and reviewer spawns read `model_<stage>` / `effort_<stage>`. Without a difficulty the keys are empty and the prose rule applies (the prompt gains the inert rubric and "with a difficulty set" lines; the template language has no conditional). The ledger line of such a session carries `difficulty` and `stages`. Bundled `batch-task`: a fix round compares the worker's `model/effort` (not the model alone) to choose message-the-old-worker vs spawn-fresh. Rubric in README and the workflow-authoring skill. Tests: `src/model-table.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-STAGE-MODEL-TABLE): `model_table:` gains `stages:` — named stages (`plan`, `execute`, `review`, `review_verify`, `fix`), each its own `rows` by difficulty. `difficulty` writes `model_<stage>` / `effort_<stage>` for every declared stage and keeps `model` / `effort` = the execute row, so existing workflows run unchanged; `rows` is the same as `stages.execute` (given once); `escalate` moves execute and fix only; a `review_class` entry still overrides the review stage. The loader refuses a stage row set that does not end at 10, an unknown stage name, and a state reading `context.model_<x>` / `effort_<x>` for a stage the table lacks. The ledger line carries `stages` (model/effort per stage). Bundled `batch-task` starts from a stage-aware table (calibrated on one project: 154 round-1 findings, 39 tasks); its fix round, round-2 review use the fix / review_verify rows. Tests: `src/model-table.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-REVISIT-PROMPT): optional state field `revisit_prompt` — a template rendered like `prompt` and shown on a revisit instead of the generic "Revisit #N" line; absent = unchanged; a forced full prompt (`status()`) and `full_on_revisit: true` keep precedence. Tests: `src/tools.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-DRIVER-RESUME): a driver messaged after its last line is not finished. `task-driver.md` gains "Resumed after your last line": `status` on P once and continue (a relayed worker/reviewer line is that agent's answer; never "my run already ended"). Bundled `batch` `collect` relays an orphaned worker/reviewer line after a restart to the driver linked to P, re-spawning from `driver_call` only if that fails. Test: the shipped texts.

- engine (ENG-SKILL-SYNC-2): (1) the engine-task land keeps the pre-merge sha in `<git-common-dir>/land-before-<task>` (removed on a taken-back merge and after the sync), so a retry after `land_failed` (merge already on master) syncs the live skills from it instead of from a no-op `before == merged`. (2) `sync-live-skills.sh`'s "left alone" WARNING now names the next step: the `rsync` command that takes the bundled copy, or adding the name to `.skillsyncignore`. `engine-task.yaml` is git-ignored: installed by the parent.

- engine (ENG-DECIDER): design choices no longer go to the parent. `templates/drivers/task-driver.md` gains "Design choices — ask the DECIDER": on a FIXES with `design=yes` (or a further allowed FIXES) the driver spawns one fresh opus/high decider that reads review/handoff/report, the task line and the project memory index (context key `memory_index`, optional, never hardcoded), appends `decisions.md` in `<wt>.log`, and answers `DECIDED file=…` or `ESCALATE reason=…` (scope change, project-wide rule, user-visible behaviour, contradicts a user decision -> NEEDS-PARENT). The driver's last line gains `decided=<file>`; bundled `batch` `collect` tells the parent to audit it; `batch-task`'s review route mentions the decisions file. Test: the shipped texts.

- engine (ENG-SKILL-SYNC-FLOW): a task that edits a bundled skill no longer needs the parent to sync it. `scripts/check-skill-sync.sh` (the pre-commit drift check), in a linked worktree on an `eng/*` branch, accepts a bundled skill AHEAD of live (bundled differs, live equals master's bundled; prints `skill-sync: <name> is ahead of live`); any other drift still fails. New `scripts/sync-live-skills.sh <before> <merged>` (the land runs it after a green build) rsyncs each bundled skill the merge changed to the live copy and prints `synced live skill: <name>`; it skips `.skillsyncignore` names, skills without a live dir and skills deleted by the merge, leaves a live copy edited since `<before>` alone with a WARNING, and never fails the merge. Tests: `src/skill-sync.test.ts`.

- engine (ENG-DRIVER-4): ENG-DRIVER-3 review notes. (a) `status`'s LINKED AGENTS scan runs only for a session of a `driver:` workflow and fails soft (no `readAll` otherwise). (b) After a restart, under a parent of a `driver:` workflow, an agent linked at another STEP of the parent than its current one (e.g. review 1's still-running session while the parent is in `fix`) is named `… an earlier step's agent — not woken; abort(…) once it is not needed` with no wake `SendMessage` (a parent without `driver:` records no `agent_step`, so its agents are all woken as before; tested). (c) The generic DRIVER block's re-spawn rule names no batch-task specifics (gone agent: check the tree for the step's leftovers, follow the step's own verify instructions, else a fresh agent and `link_agent`).

- engine (ENG-QUEUE-NAMES): every queue tool (`queue_next` / `queue_mark` / `queue_add` / `queue_edit` / `queue_header`) and `wf-queue` takes an optional queue NAME beside `file`: neither = the project's `.claude/batch/queue.md`, `queue: "<name>"` = `.claude/batch/<name>.md` (project = git root of the cwd; a worktree resolves to its main checkout), a full `file` still works; both = error. `wf-queue [<file>|--queue NAME]`, new `--path` prints the resolved file. `queue_add` / `--add` CREATES a missing queue (`batch_size: 1`, `## Tasks`) and says so; the first line is checked against the bare header before the file is created, so a refused one creates nothing. Names are resolved once, at the dispatcher: `context.queue_file` stays the absolute path task sessions get, and they never re-resolve a name from their own repo. `resolveQueueFile` in `queue.ts`. Tests: default, name, path, worktree, create, rollback race, a task in another repo marking the dispatcher's queue.

- engine (ENG-DRIVER-LESSONS): follow-ups found by workers and reviewers reach the parent. The `task-driver` last line gains `lessons=<phrase (file)>; <phrase (file)>|none` (also on `CLOSED`): up to two items COPIED, not judged, from `report.md` / `handoff.md` (`## Follow-up not done`, `## Tool defect found`, `## Open`) and `review.md` (`## Notes (non-blocking)`). Bundled `batch-task` names those sections in the worker and reviewer prompts and has a fix round / later review carry the previous file's items forward; bundled `batch` `collect` tells the parent to turn each lesson into a `queue_add` line, a memory note or nothing. Test: the shipped prompt texts.

- engine (ENG-DRIVER-3): the ENGINE records which visit a linked agent belongs to (replaces ENG-DRIVER-2's template visit markers: a revisit transition renders no prompt — "Revisit #N" — so `{{visits.work}}` never reached a fix round). (1) Under a parent of a `driver:` workflow (opt-in by workflow; a looping dispatcher without `driver:` keeps today's behaviour — its earlier visits' agents are still woken, never marked) `link_agent` stores `agent_step` = the parent's active-frame workflow, state and that state's visit beside `agent_id` (absent otherwise; linking again re-records it; an exec's routed write keeps it); its answer names the step. `status` of the parent lists `LINKED AGENTS at <state> (visit N)`: every child agent linked at the current state, `linked at visit 2 → current` or `linked at visit 1, now visit 2 → stale (its session ended)` / `an earlier visit's (its session still runs)`; after a restart `resume`'s and the SessionStart pickup's sub-agent lines name an agent linked at an earlier visit of its parent's current state as such (`not woken; abort(…) once it is not needed`) and print no wake `SendMessage` for it (`earlierVisitNote` in `takeover.ts`; `ResumeResult.session`). New `src/agent-link.ts` (pure). (2) The DRIVER block's re-spawn rule and `templates/drivers/task-driver.md` take only a `current` agent as this visit's; a "Revisit #N" reply's rendered values are the first visit's — read them from P's context. Bundled `batch-task`: no visit keys; a fix round that SendMessages the same worker calls `link_agent` again; the later-visit branch reads `model` / `worker_model` / `worker_agent` / `worker_session` from the context. (3) `driverRefError` names what `resolveDriverRef` takes as a path: `"/"`, `"~"` or `".md"`. (4) Tests: a fix round after a linked worker shows it stale, re-linked current; a restart mid-review (review 1's session suspended, not ended) wakes the current reviewer only, through `resume` and through the pickup; `start` with a `context` on a `driver:` workflow.

- engine (ENG-DRIVER): items 3–6 of the agent-driven-queues proposal. (1) A workflow's `driver: { model, effort, prompt_ref }` (strict; a bare `prompt_ref` = a shipped driver `templates/drivers/<name>.md`, unknown name fails the load; else a path, absolute / `~/` / relative to the session's cwd; a heir inherits it). Shipped: `templates/drivers/task-driver.md`, the generic driver instructions (from anyparse's slice-driver). (2) `start` of such a workflow appends a DRIVER block — the exact Agent call (model, effort, background, the prompt filled with session id, `context.task`, `context.queue_file` or the parent's, the instructions path; a missing file → WARNING) and its `link_agent`. New `start` argument `context` (string map; `cwd` / `claude_code_pid` refused; `difficulty` goes through the `model_table`). (3) Bundled `batch-task`: `driver: haiku/low task-driver`; `mark_done` is the bookkeeping exec — `[x]` + one record line from the context into the queue header's `record_file:` when set. (4) The worker writes `report.md` / `handoff.md`, the reviewer `review.md` in `<worktree>.log`, each answering with one line (`DONE …`, `BLOCKED …`, `VERDICT X … review=… design=yes|no`); fix rounds pass the line + the file, never the text; the spawn prompts and the claim say a transition reply IS the next prompt. Bundled `batch`: `dispatch` passes `context` to `start` and spawns the DRIVER block as given; `collect` routes on the drivers' `LANDED` / `CLOSED` / `NEEDS-PARENT` lines. New `src/driver.ts`. Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.

- engine (ENG-STALE): stall detection, item 2 of the agent-driven-queues proposal. A state's `stale_after: <seconds>` (0 = never; a load error on a terminal or `sub_workflow` state); unset, by type — prompt 1800 s, exec/fetch 3600 s, a `resource:` / `fanout:` exec 14400 s, an exec never below its own `timeout` + 300 s. A session whose state saw no engine call that long is STALE — never when ended, suspended, waiting for an active child, or a `resource:` / `fanout:` exec waits for its pool lease (no run yet, or `pool_starved`) while its owner process lives. The clock = the later of the last write and the run's start (`running_exec`, else the new `pending_action.run_at`, written on the step's first granted lease — a fanout's clock), so a takeover, resume or `context_set` during a run resets it. `sessions` marks `[STALE: no engine call for 47m (since …), past its state's stale_after 30m]`; `status` lists the STALE sessions below it on top with what to do (SendMessage the agent; no answer → abort, or a new agent continues from `status` — the session holds the step), `transition` appends them (never twice: a prompt that renders them replaces the notice, a revisit digest brings it back); a step run again drops its old `run_at`; new template var `{{stale_sessions}}` (stale sessions below this one, or `none`; computed only when named; also in an action's `success_prompt` / `error_prompt` / `context_set`). The bundled workflow-authoring skill documents `stale_after`, the STALE marks and `{{stale_sessions}}`. Bundled `batch`: `collect` lists the run's stale sessions and says how to message or restart their agents; `paused` is `stale_after: 0`. New `src/stale.ts` (pure).

- engine (ENG-DIFFICULTY): a task's difficulty picks its agents. (1) Queue key `difficulty: 1..10` (`-` = none): `wf-queue` prints it as a sixth column (`ID\tmodel\teffort\trepo\tworkflow\tdifficulty`), `queue_next` as `| difficulty: N`; a value outside 1..10 keeps the line out with that reason, and `queue_add` / `queue_edit` refuse it. (2) A workflow's `model_table: { rows: [{ upto, model, effort }], escalate?: { <state>: N }, review?: { <class>: { model, effort } } }` (rows ascending, the last `upto` 10, effort one of the Agent tool's levels; anything else fails the load, a typo names the nearest key; a heir inherits it unless it sets its own). Under it, `context_set` of `difficulty` writes `model`, `effort`, `model_step`, `escalations` into the context (the answer and a `model` history line name them; outside 1..10 is refused), and every move into a state recomputes the row — the difficulty's own plus one per `escalate` entry whose state's visits reached N, capped — rewriting model/effort only when it moved. Escalation is per session — fix rounds, and a blocked re-entry within one session (a workflow whose `blocked` routes back declares `escalate: { blocked: 1 }`); no hidden state crosses sessions: `queue_mark` `retry: true` (`wf-queue --running <ID> --retry`) on a `[!]` line with `difficulty: N` rewrites it to `difficulty: min(N+1, 10)` in the line, under the same lock, and the answer says so (`A: [!] → [>] (difficulty 4 → 5)`), so the re-run session starts a row up; a line without `difficulty:` is retried unchanged. (3) `review_class` set by an exec's `context_set` writes `review_model` / `review_effort` (the class's `review` entry, else the task's own); new `scripts/review-class.sh <base> <tip>` prints `light` when every path of `git diff base...tip` is a test or a doc — `docs/` / `specs/` at the repo root, `tests/` / `__tests__/` anywhere, `*.md`/`.rst`/`.adoc`, `*.test.*` / `*Test.*`, CHANGELOG/LICENSE/README; a nested `docs/` package or a `.txt` build file is code — else `code` (doubt is `code`; `REVIEW_LIGHT_PATHS` overrides the regex). (4) The ledger line carries `difficulty`, `model`, `effort`, `escalations`, `review_model`, `review_effort` and `tokens` (`tokens` → `total`, `tokens_<role>` → `<role>`, whole numbers) from the ended session's context. templates: `batch-task` declares a table (1–2 haiku/low, 3–4 sonnet/medium, 5–6 sonnet/high, 7–8 opus/high, 9–10 opus/xhigh; `escalate: { work: 3 }` = the second fix round, where the driver spawns a fresh worker on the new model; review `light` sonnet/medium, `code` opus/high), its claim sets `difficulty` (model/effort columns only for a line without one), and a task now passes `review_scope` (exec) → `review` (one reviewer on `review_model`, FIXES → `work`, at most 3) before `project_land`; the driver sums `tokens_worker` / `tokens_review`. `batch` documents `difficulty:` and its `cross_check` reviews the run as a whole. The bundled `workflow-authoring` skill (and its live copy) names `difficulty:`, `model_table` and the new batch-task states. Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.

- engine (ENG-POOL-SCHED, the ENG-2c the ledger was recording for): the pool backfills. The first waiter blocked only by threads is the head; a later waiter starts ahead of it only when that cannot delay it — its estimate ends before the threads the head waits for (its min; a flexible head inside `wait_full`: its max) are expected free (the running leases' `started + est_ms` in order; a lease with no estimate, past it or over-running ends at an unknown time). Every head is a hard reservation, starved or not: the brief's second rule "fits in room − head.min" is vacuous (a head blocked by threads has room < its need), so it is not implemented. Estimates: new `src/estimate.ts` — the p90 of a step's last 30 successful runs on the same threads granted from `ledger.jsonl` (key `step_key`, at least 3 runs, within 30 days; a flexible request or a `fanout:` shard has none), re-read by its appended tail, looked up by the requester outside the pool lock; `wf-run --est SEC` gives one by hand. No estimate → today's strict order. Damage bound: a lease backfilled past a head that runs past 2× its estimate sets `backfill_off` (pool.json) for that head — nothing passes it any more; `pool.log` gets `backfill:` and `backfill off:` lines. No new status lines or notices (a blocking transition stays intended). Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.

- engine (ENG-12): queue admission stops serialising unrelated work. (1) A queue header `overlap: paths | files` (default `paths` = the behaviour so far; any other value is an error). A bad value refuses the pick (exit 2) but not the marks, edits or `queue_header`, so a typo cannot strand running lines. Under `files` only a `touches:` entry naming a FILE — no glob, and a regular file under the line's repo (`Makefile`, `bin/hxq`) or, when the path does not exist yet, a last segment holding a `.` (`src/x/Foo.hx`, `.gitattributes`) — conflicts, with the same file (or a file under it) in another line; a directory or glob entry (`src/anyparse/query`, `src/format/Doc*`) is a hint that conflicts with nothing. `owner:` (equal or `.`-prefix) still conflicts and a line without `touches:` still overlaps every line of its repo. (2) A line key `exclusive:` (any value but empty or `—`): the line overlaps every other line of every repo in either mode — `overlaps R (exclusive R)` in `--explain`; `queue_add` / `queue_edit` accept it. (3) Fix, both modes: `touches:` — and its absence — is compared only between lines whose `repo:` resolves to the same repository (through symlinks; an engine line's `src/queue.ts` no longer "overlaps" another repo's `src`, and a line without `touches:` runs alone only in its own repo); `owner:` and `exclusive:` still compare across repos. Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.

- engine (ENG-11): the pool no longer starves silently. (1) A lease's process tree is its own load: each pass reads the process table (`ps`, at most once a second, outside the pool lock; a failed read backs off 30 s) and sums the busy cpus of a lease's recorded group and its descendants (another lease's group excluded); `external = max(0, load − Σ max(threads, tree) − load_threshold)` over the leases with threads (a lock-only lease's tree stays external) — a lease of 4 whose command ran ~12 busy `haxe --wait` read as 7.8 cores of foreign load, and 7 waiters waited 2+ hours. An over-running lease is therefore no longer bounded by admission (only `held ≤ budget` is): it is flagged, not throttled — a tree at least `max(2, threads/2)` cpus over its threads is flagged: `holds 4 threads, runs ~12 (OVERRUN)` in `--status` / `pool_status`, `label (4, runs ~12)` in a waiting `wf-run`'s progress line, one line in the new `<pool dir>/pool.log`. (2) Starvation: `config.json` `starve_after` (seconds, default 900); a waiter queued that long is marked `STARVED` (status row, `wf-run`'s progress line, one `pool.log` line) and a pool with waiters that granted nothing that long prints `STARVED: nothing granted for …, N waiting (held, external, over-running leases)`; `pool.json` keeps `last_grant` while waiters exist. An exec waiting on a lease checks every 10 s and, once starved, records `pool_starved` on its session until no wait of the step is starved (a fanout: no shard), cleared again when a step starts and ends so a crashed server's mark does not linger: the progress heartbeat says `— STARVED: <why>`, `sessions` marks it, and `status` / `transition` of the session or any ancestor carry a `POOL STARVED: session …` line. (3) Orphaned leases: a `wf-run` lease owns its group (`owns_group`); a pass that finds the `wf-run` dead while its detached child group runs SIGTERMs the group, SIGKILLs it after 3 s, and frees the lease once it is gone — a SIGKILLed `wf-run` leaves no live child and no lease (before: the child ran on, holding the lease). The lease records the boot (`boot_ms`) and the group leader's start time (`pgid_start`) at attach; a lease from another boot or whose pgid leads another process now is dropped without a signal, and a group from another boot no longer keeps any lease alive. An exec's group still outlives its server for `adopt`. Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.

- engine (ENG-9): a live queue is changed through the engine, under the lock `queue_next` / `queue_mark` take (a script or sed edit outside it could drop a mark an exec wrote at the same moment, and nothing checked the line). New MCP tools: `queue_add({ file, line, after_id? | before_id? })` — a new `[ ]` line (the `- [ ] ` may be left out) after / before a line, else after the last task line, refused unless the parser a pick uses reads it as meant: a new id, one row, each `key:` field a known key at most once (an unknown `toches:` would be read as text), every id-like token in `after:` a line of the queue and no `after:` cycle (prose beside the ids stays a manual condition), a one-word `workflow:`, a `repo:` that resolves; `queue_edit({ file, id, set?: { field: value }, append_note? })` — metadata fields of one line (`""` removes a field; a set field keeps its place, a new one follows the line's metadata, a duplicate of the key goes), a note appended as the last field, the set fields checked as `queue_add` checks them, the mark untouched; `queue_header({ file, batch_size, reason })` — `batch_size: N  # <reason>`, the reason required. `queue_mark({ …, to: "running", retry: true })` restarts a failed line, `[!]`→`[>]` (without `retry` a `[!]` line is refused with that hint). `wf-queue` subcommands: `--add <LINE> [--after ID|--before ID]`, `--edit <ID> [--set key=value]… [--append-note T]`, `--batch-size <N> --reason T`, `--running <ID> --retry`. templates: `batch` names the tools for a live queue and lifts a pause through `queue_header`. Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.

- engine (ENG-10): pool gate. (1) A rule's new `env: { NAME: true | false | "/regex/" }` conditions it on the segment's leading assignments (also after `env` / `time`): set / not set / set with a matching value — `APQ_TEST=x node bin/test.js` and a bare `node bin/test.js` were indistinguishable, since `match` sees the text from the command word on. A broken `env` (not an object, a key that is no variable name, a value not boolean or a valid `/regex/`) is a config error naming the rule. (2) A segment running `wf-run` or a `wrappers` script is no longer matched against the rules at all — before, a `deny` (a `/regex/` without `^` above all) refused a command already under a lease, the gate's own wrapped form included; other segments of the same command are still matched and a `deny` there still refuses. Deploy: `npm run build`.

- engine (ENG-8): (1) `npm run check:ci` (`scripts/check-ci.sh`, part of `npm run check`) runs the suite the way CI does, on this machine: `env -i` with PATH = node's dir + the system dirs, a fresh empty `HOME` (no global git config or ignore file) that is also `TMPDIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, git `user.useConfigOnly=true` (a commit without an identity the test set itself fails instead of borrowing one guessed from the host), `TZ=America/St_Johns` (neither the local zone nor CI's UTC; `CHECK_CI_TZ` overrides), `LANG=C`, `CI=true`; first it runs CI's package.json / plugin.json version check. A test that is green only thanks to the local environment (ENG-4's missing git identity in the batch-task tests) now fails locally. `.gitignore` names `node_modules` without the slash, so a worktree whose `node_modules` is a symlink stays clean. (2) A session started with `start({ cwd })` in another project that has its own `.claude/workflows/` (a worktree counts as its main checkout) sees the workflows that project defines itself laid by name over the server's (a name only the server's project or the global tier has is still found; a cwd whose `.claude/workflows` is the global dir — the home dir — is no project) — at start, on resume, on a taken-over exec and in a server that holds no snapshot of it; before, only the server's cwd counted, so a dispatcher in one repo could not run another repo's task workflow. (3) A queue line's `workflow:` names its own task workflow: `wf-queue` prints it as a fifth column (`ID\tmodel\teffort\trepo\tworkflow`, `-` = none), `queue_next` as `| workflow: X`; a value that is not one word keeps the line out with that reason; the bundled `batch` dispatch starts the line's workflow, else `task_workflow`. Deploy: `npm run build`, then restart Claude.

- engine (ENG-6): pool gate — a PreToolUse(Bash) plugin hook (`hooks/pool-gate.sh` → `build/pool-gate-hook.js`, logic in `src/pool-gate.ts`) runs a command the project marks heavy under `<plugin>/scripts/wf-run --fail-open --threads <N|MIN-MAX> --label '<cmd>' -- <shell> -c '<cmd>'` (`hookSpecificOutput.updatedInput`), so agents' own test runs and builds take a lease from the machine-wide pool without calling `wf-run`. Heavy = only what `.claude/workflows/pool-gate.json` (nearest from the payload cwd up to the git root) lists: `rules: [{ match: <leading words | /regex/>, threads: N | "MIN-MAX", pass?: <flag with $WF_THREADS | env:NAME>, deny?: <reason> }]`, `wrappers: [<scripts that take their own lease>]`; no config or no hit → untouched, silent — the no-config case exits in the shell script without starting node. Quote/escape/`$( )`-aware split at `&&` `||` `;` `|` `( )` newlines; a hit wraps the WHOLE command under one `wf-run` with the largest share any hit asks; a `pass` flag goes before a `--` and is not added when given (`-j 2`, `-j8`, `--x=2`). `deny` refuses (watch modes). Left alone: `WF_THREADS` in the hook's environment, `wf-run` or a wrapper anywhere in the command, a background `&`, a heredoc or an unterminated quote; `WF_POOL_BYPASS=1 [WF_POOL_BYPASS_REASON=…]` up front → untouched with one stderr line. Any gate failure (no node, no build, broken config) → the command passes as is plus one stderr line. RTK: Claude Code keeps the LAST `updatedInput` of parallel PreToolUse hooks (almost always the gate's), so with `rtk` on PATH the gate puts the body through `rtk rewrite` — 0 → its rewrite with `permissionDecision: "allow"` (RTK vouched the user's allow rules cover every command), 3 → its rewrite, 2 → the command left alone (Claude Code's deny sees the original), else the body as is. Docs warn against an allow rule on `…/scripts/wf-run`. New `wf-run --fail-open`: a pool that cannot be used (unwritable / uncreatable `$WF_POOL_DIR`, broken `config.json`, lock not taken) runs the command without a lease (`WF_THREADS` = the smallest share asked) plus one stderr line instead of exit 1; busy still waits; a failed release keeps the command's exit code. Deploy note: `npm run build` (the hook needs `build/pool-gate-hook.js`), then restart the Claude sessions; nothing changes in a project until it adds a `pool-gate.json`

- engine (ENG-4b): `wf-queue --explain` / `queue_next({ explain: true })` fold the `window full` lines (and the paused ones) into one summary line (`58 lines: window full — first: A, B, C, D, E …`) after the substantive reasons (`after:`, `overlaps`, `⏸`, parent-run, repo) listed by name; `--explain-all` / `explain: "all"` keep one line per pending line.

- engine (ENG-4): the task queue is an engine capability for every project (was a project script, anyparse's `queue-pick.mjs`, whose semantics it keeps — checked line by line on a live queue). New `src/queue.ts`: a queue file (`batch_size:` header, `- [ ] ID | key: value | … | text |` lines) is parsed and admitted by a pure pass — window = `batch_size` − `[>]`; `after:` ids must be `[x]` (an unknown id-like token or prose beside the ids is manual); `touches:` prefixes on a `/` boundary and `owner:` names (equal or `.`-prefix) must not overlap a running or just-admitted line, a line without `touches:` overlaps everything; `⏸` in metadata or `after:` skips; `model: —` = parent-run — and the only writes are `[.]` marks under `<file>.lock` (proper-lockfile; the same directory the script's mkdir lock used, so the two exclude each other during a migration). New: `repo:` per line (absolute, or a name → the `base:` repo of that basename, else the directory beside the queue's project; default the queue's project; an unresolvable name is not admitted), returned with each admitted line; `batch_size: 0  # <why>, was N` is the pause — nothing is admitted, the pick says `window 0 — paused: <comment>` (the script did not read a comment on that line). MCP tools `queue_next({ file, dry_run?, explain? })` (id / model / effort / repo / text per admitted line, marked `[>]`; two parallel calls never admit one line) and `queue_mark({ file, id, to: running|done|failed|released, note? })` (only from the expected mark; `note` lands after the id and may not hold `|`, `⏸` or a leading `key:`; a write is re-planned when the file changed since its read, so a hand edit is never overwritten). Exec form `wf-queue` (`bin`, shim `scripts/wf-queue`): stdout `ID\tmodel\teffort\trepo`, exit 0 / 10 / 11 as the script, plus 12 = paused with nothing running, 2 = error; `--done|--failed|--release|--running <ID> [--note T]`, `--header <key>`. A `resource.locks` name now renders template vars (`"{{context.cwd}}-land"`; rendered whitespace → `_`). templates: `batch` admits through a `pick` exec (`wf-queue`, routed by exit code: `dispatch` / `collect` / `cross_check` / new `paused`) instead of a prompt that reads the queue, and runs every admitted task as a child session of `context.task_workflow`; new bundled `batch-task` (`claim` → `project_prepare` [exec: worktree `<repo>/../.wt/<repo-name>/<ID>`, branch `batch/<ID>`] → `work` (one worker, its session started and linked by the driver) → `project_land` [exec under the lock `{{context.cwd}}-land`: refuses a dirty repo (3), `git merge --no-ff`, the queue header's `land_test:`; a conflict or a red test takes the merge back with `git reset --keep` — never `--hard`, so an edit made meanwhile survives — and only while HEAD is still the merge, else exit 4 with nothing touched] → `mark_done` → `cleanup`), with `project_prepare` / `project_land` as extension points for heirs. Deploy note: `npm run build` (the `wf-queue` shim needs `build/queue-cli.js`), then restart the Claude sessions; a running `batch` session keeps its old snapshot

- engine (ENG-5c): a tool call that waits for an exec (pool-lease queue, fanout, run) sends MCP `notifications/progress` every 60 s (`waiting for pool lease (Ns)` / `running <state> (Ns)`) when the request carries a `progressToken`, and stops on any outcome; Claude Code aborted a `transition` into a long `land` step with "sent no response or progress for 1800s" although the exec lived on.

- engine (ENG-3c): two defects of the `claude --resume` pick-up. (1) The hook told the main agent to `SendMessage` a non-existent agent for a session it runs itself (a pipeline with no `agent_id` under the dispatcher): a session with no `agent_id` on itself or any session up to the picked-up root is now one line `… no agent linked — yours, unless a sub-agent you spawned runs it (then link_agent + SendMessage) → status({ session_id })` (in `resume`'s list: `resume`); an unlinked session under a linked agent's keeps the old hint. The action step is named apart — `exec in flight` / `exec finished while no server ran` / `exec killed by the restart` / `step interrupted before its exec started` — in the pick-up, `/compact` and `resume` lists. (2) A session whose exec waited for its pool lease when the process exited (no `running_exec` yet) was stuck after the pick-up: `status` showed the state with `[sub_workflow: unknown]` and no transitions, only an explicit `resume` (which the hook forbids) got it moving. The engine now writes `pending_action` (`{ frame, state, at }`) before an action state's exec waits for a lease or spawns, and clears it with the routed write, on an unroutable result, or when the step throws in a live server; the first `status` / `transition` of a picked-up session stopped there runs the step again (lease wait included) — `INTERRUPTED STEP RUN AGAIN`, as for an exec gone without a result. This server's own running step (`_inFlight`) and one another live process owns are never taken; a surviving old server (Claude crashed, the server lived on) granted its lease after the session left it gives the lease back unstarted (`ExecContext.mayStart`) and throws instead of routing. Not covered: a `fanout:` with shards already running in such a server (the rerun runs beside them), and an MCP-server restart inside the same Claude process with the old server alive (same pid). `status` of a session sitting in an action state names what it is doing (running here / in PID … / begun by a server that is gone / not running — `resume` runs it again) instead of `[sub_workflow: unknown]`. New `Engine.orphanedStep`, `Executor.fate`.

- engine (ENG-3b): `claude --resume` / `--continue` picks its workflow sessions up by itself. The SessionStart hook (`source: "resume"`) runs the new `build/session-cli.js pickup`: every session stamped with the resumed conversation id that is suspended — or owned by a dead process (a crash past SessionEnd) — is handed to the new process with its whole active sub-tree (each file under `Storage.update`'s lock, via `takeOver` in the new `src/takeover.ts`, which `Engine.resume` now uses too) and every suspension in it is cleared; a session another live process runs is left alone — so is a descendant it runs unsuspended, and a session whose parent is active (it is the parent's to hand over, e.g. the child an explicit `resume` left suspended in another process). The hook prints `picked up: <session> @ <workflow @ state>` per root, its exec in flight, and the sub-agent sessions with the `SendMessage` that wakes each agent — the agent calls `status` on its own session (no `resume`). The first `status` / `transition` of a session whose foreground exec a previous server left running (`running_exec`, not this server's own: `_inFlight`) takes it over like `resume` (waits, takes the recorded result, or re-runs); a `transition` then answers with the routed state as an error (`isError`) and is NOT taken. Without a build or node the hook falls back to the ENG-3 listing for an explicit `resume`; `startup`, another account and `--fork-session` stay explicit (`suspended()` + `resume`). `source: "compact"` / `"clear"`: the hook appends this process's active sessions as a tree (id, workflow @ state, nested stack, agent, exec in flight) to its output, which lands in the new context. The startup reaper re-checks the dead owner under the lock, and re-reads a session before expiring it, so a hook handover between its read and its write is neither re-suspended nor abandoned. Deploy note: `npm run build` (the hook needs `build/session-cli.js`), then restart the Claude sessions

- engine (ENG-5b): an exception inside an exec's `close` / `error` handler (e.g. `_runResult` failing to read the result files) now ends the step with exit 1 and the error text instead of being lost or killing the server; `index.ts` also logs `unhandledRejection` / `uncaughtException` to stderr and stays alive (Node ≥15 exits on them by default)

- engine (ENG-5): an exec killed by its `timeout` whose process-group reap fails (`_reapGroup` rejects) now ends with exit 124 and the reap error in `error`; it was an unhandled rejection — under Node ≥15 that exits the MCP server — and the exec never settled. The other `ast-grep` hits were checked by tests and are safe: the per-session queue in `tools.ts` hands a failed task's rejection to its caller and runs the next task (its tail now settles on both paths via one `then(release, release)`); a failed detached-run record in `_exec` is caught where it is made; `wf-run`'s `run()` executor is synchronous, so a throwing `spawn` rejects and `wf-run` exits 1 with the reason. Those sites carry `// ast-grep-ignore: <rule> -- <why>`. `npm run check` now runs `npm run scan` (`ast-grep scan --error`, rules/ as errors, 0 findings; needs `ast-grep` on PATH)

- engine (ENG-3): a restart no longer loses the workflow. SessionEnd SUSPENDS this process's active sessions and their active descendants (`suspended_at`, stack/context/children kept, no ledger line — nothing ended) instead of abandoning them; the hook no longer ends any session, so `build/ledger-cli.js` is gone. The SessionStart hook writes `$STATE_DIR/claude-session-<pid>` = the conversation id; `start` stamps it on the session (`claude_session_id`), SessionEnd stamps the ending one; on `source: "resume"` (`claude --resume` / `--continue`, same id) the hook lists that conversation's suspended root sessions with their `resume` call, on `startup` it lists nothing. New tools: `resume({ session_id })` — re-owns the session and its active sub-tree for this process, clears the session's own suspension (each child waits for its agent's `resume`), returns its state and lists the suspended sub-agent sessions as agent → session → state with the `SendMessage` that wakes each; `suspended({ all? })` — suspended sessions of this project (git root), for an account switch; `link_agent({ session_id, agent_id })` — which agent drives a child session. `transition` / `context_set` / `modify` refuse a suspended session and name `resume`. Foreground execs now run detached through a wrapper that writes output to files and the exit code to a file (`$STATE_DIR/exec/`), so an exec running at exit is not killed; the session records it while it runs (`running_exec`), and `resume` of a session stopped in that state waits for it (abort kills it, its `timeout` counts from its start), takes its recorded result, or — neither survived, e.g. a reboot (boot time checked) — runs the step again; its pool lease already lives while its process group does. A `fanout:` state is re-run; a result that cannot be routed (no target, max transitions) drops the run's files and record; the run dir (`$STATE_DIR/exec/`) is 0700 and server start removes run files older than a day that no session references. Behaviour changes of the file-based exec: a foreground exec resolves when its shell exits (a background child it leaves no longer holds the step open); a command killed by a signal exits 128+signal (was 1); a timed-out run resolves only after its whole group is gone (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 5 s); `max_output` counts bytes (was characters); the output files are not capped while the command runs (`yes` fills the disk until its `timeout`). `--fork-session` gets a new conversation id, so its SessionStart hint lists nothing — `suspended()` finds the sessions. Server start suspends the sessions of dead processes (a crash past SessionEnd; was: abandon) and abandons, with a ledger line, sessions suspended longer than `WF_SUSPENDED_TTL_DAYS` (default 7; history event `expire`). Plan resume from a new process now says `resume` first. Deploy note: `npm run build`, then restart every Claude session — sessions of a process still running the OLD hooks are abandoned at its exit as before; only processes started after the build suspend

- engine (ENG-2d): `extends: <workflow>` — a workflow file inherits another: the base's states plus its own laid over by name (a same-named state replaced whole, new ones added), `initial` / `max_transitions` / `description` from the heir when set; `extends` of its own name resolves from the tiers below the file (a project `coding` with `extends: coding` inherits the global/bundled one), another name from the top tier; chains, cycle / missing-base detection, validation on the merged result (a file that fails it falls back to the same name one tier down, as a schema failure always did); without `extends`, `initial` + `states` stay required (strict). Extension points with contract names in the bundled templates: `testing` gets `project_test` (the run half of the old `unit_tests`: `unit_tests` now writes and hands over via `run`; `project_test` routes `pass` → check_integration / `fail` → fix_unit as before), `project_build` (build before integration; a fix round rebuilds; `fix_integration` gets `max_visits: 5` → `failed`, since a failing build loops there without passing integration's budget) and `project_gate` (bundled: `exec true` → done, so no gate; every path to `done` passes it, `assess.skip_all` included); `bug-fix` gets `project_build` before `verify_visual`. Bundled defaults keep the old behaviour (agent-run prompt or no-op); a project heir overrides them with execs. `fanout: { count | items_command, threads_each, max_parallel? }` on an exec: N instances (`WF_SHARD=i/N` 1-based, or `WF_ITEM=<line>`), each with its own pool lease of `threads_each` threads (`WF_THREADS`), at most `max_parallel` in flight (default: what the budget runs side by side, so the pool queue does not fill with the whole fan-out); `items_command` lines lose a trailing `\r`; `timeout` per shard; exit 0 only if all are 0, else their common code (all timed out → 124) or 1; `{{stdout}}` = summary + failed tails; abort kills / withdraws / starts no more; history and ledger carry `shards` (per-shard exit, run, wait, threads); load error beside `background` / `resource` (so no `wait_timeout` / `locks` / step `priority` for shards — a known limitation). `on_timeout: <state>` on a foreground exec routes a run killed by its `timeout` (124) apart from `on_error` / `cases` (load error beside `cases: {"124": …}`). Deploy note: a server built before this rejects `extends` / `fanout` / `on_timeout` workflows (unknown key) and still has the old `testing` / `bug-fix` routes — `npm run build`, then restart every Claude session (templates are read at boot; running sessions keep their snapshot)

- engine (ENG-2b): session priority levels — `low` −100 / `normal` 0 / `high` 100 / `urgent` 200: `start({ priority })`, inherited by child sessions, default `normal`; a lease's priority = the level + the step's `resource.priority` (fine-tuning within the level). New tool `set_priority({ session_id, level })`: changes a session and its active descendants on the fly and re-ranks their WAITING pool entries at once (running leases untouched, no preemption); it runs outside the session's queue, changes each session through the new `Storage.update` (read-modify-write under the session file's lock, so a session that ended meanwhile stays ended), and the action chain keeps the level written during its exec; contended session-file locks now retry instead of failing. Every exec gets `WF_PRIORITY` (the sum) + `WF_PRIORITY_LEVEL`; `wf-run` without `--priority` queues at them (and passes `WF_PRIORITY` to its child). Flexible leases: `resource: { threads: { min, max: N|budget }, wait_full: SEC }` and `wf-run --threads A-B [--wait-full SEC]` — the max when it fits, else after `wait_full` (default 0) every thread that fits if ≥ min; `WF_THREADS` = granted; a waiting flexible head closes the threads behind it like any waiter (no overtaking); `threads: N` = `{ min: N, max: N }`. `wf-run --status` and the new `pool_status` tool print one line per lease/waiter: pid, held/wanted threads and locks, priority + level, since when, project (git root; a linked worktree → its main repo), session, step, label. History and `ledger.jsonl` of a leased exec record `threads_granted` and `step_key` = `<project>|<workflow>|<state>` beside `wait_ms`/`duration_ms` (raw data for a time model; nothing is modelled). Deploy note: a server or `wf-run` built before this knows neither levels nor the flexible form (a `{ min, max }` workflow fails its load, an old pass treats a flexible waiter as a fixed request for its max) — restart every Claude session after the build

- engine (ENG-2a): the leased command learns its share — an exec under `resource:` with threads and the child of `wf-run` get `WF_THREADS` = the threads granted (after `budget` and the clamp to the budget); a light exec, a lock-only lease and `--efficiency` get none (an inherited value is dropped). Documented recipe: pass it to `-j` / `--maxWorkers` / `HXCPP_COMPILE_THREADS` / `APQ_ORACLE_PARALLEL` — compilers and test runners otherwise take every core whatever the lease says. Named locks: `resource: { locks: [NAME, …] }` (`threads` now optional beside `locks`, at least one of the two — a schema error otherwise) and `wf-run --lock NAME` (repeatable; without `--threads` a lock-only lease) — machine-wide mutual exclusion by name in the same `pool.json`, granted atomically with the threads (all or nothing, nothing held while waiting → no deadlock); nothing overtakes a waiter on what it waits for (threads as before, a lock in queue order), and a waiter blocked by a held lock claims no threads, so a long-held dev-server lock does not stall the queue; a lock-only lease is never blocked by load; dead holders reaped, abort withdraws, `wait_timeout` → 75, `--status` lists held locks, `--efficiency` keeps its locks. templates: file-review `mechanical_pass` and lint-review `run` lease `{ threads: 4, priority: 1 }` behind a lease-free probe (`mechanical_probe` / `probe`, `hxq-lint-file.sh` with `LINT_PROBE=1`: exit 0 + SKIP lines when there is nothing to lint, 10 when there is) — a review of a non-Haxe file never queues; under a lease the script sets `APQ_ORACLE_PARALLEL` from `WF_THREADS` unless the caller set it. Deploy note: a server built before locks ignores them — restart every Claude session before relying on a lock; an exec lease also names its child's process group, so a server that dies mid-exec keeps its lease (and locks) until the group exits
- engine (ENG-1d): `status({ session_id, key })` returns one context value WHOLE (a string as is, anything else as pretty JSON; an unset key errors with the key list) — the plain status still cuts values to 200 chars and now names the call on a cut value; works on finished sessions, no ownership check (as status). A state's `on_exhausted: <state>` turns a move past its `max_visits` into a move into that state instead of an error — for agent transitions, action-state routes and skill-gate auto-transitions alike (chained routes followed, a cycle is an error, the history `detail` names the exhausted states, a rerouted action move shows the routed-to state's own prompt instead of the action's `success_prompt`/`error_prompt`; load error without `max_visits` or to an unknown state); without it nothing changes. `resource: { threads: budget }` and `wf-run --threads budget` lease the whole configured budget (any other string is a load / usage error). The contract that `{{stdout}}` / `{{stderr}}` / `{{exit_code}}` (the session's `last_action_result`) reach the routed-to state's own prompt, later prompt states and the next action's command/env is documented and pinned by a test
- engine (ENG-1b): machine-wide thread pool for heavy work. `wf-run [--threads N] [--priority P] [--wait-timeout SEC] [--label TEXT] [--efficiency] -- <cmd>` (`bin`, shim `scripts/wf-run`) and the exec field `resource: { threads, priority?, wait_timeout? }` take a lease from one pool dir per machine (`$WF_POOL_DIR`, default `~/.claude/workflow-pool`, independent of `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`); admission under a file lock: `held + N <= budget` and, beside a live lease, `held + N + external <= budget` — external load never blocks an empty pool (`budget` default performance cores − 2, `external = max(0, load − held − load_threshold)`, `load_threshold` default 2; `load` = busy cpus over the cpu-tick window since the pool's previous pass, loadavg1 only as the fallback before a window exists), strict priority/arrival order without overtaking, `N > budget` waits for an empty pool, dead entries reaped every pass (a `wf-run` lease also lives while its child's process group does, so a SIGKILLed `wf-run` cannot push its child outside the budget); busy past the wait timeout = exit 75. `wf-run` runs the child in its own group, forwards SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP, exits with its code, `--status` lists leases and the queue, `--efficiency` (macOS) runs under `taskpolicy -b` outside the budget. Exec history and ledger record `wait_ms` (ledger `wait_ms_total`), `{{wait_ms}}` is a template var; an abort that lands as the lease is granted gives it back without running; `resource:` is strict and a load error on a background exec or a fetch. `killProcessGroup` moved to `src/process.ts`
- engine (ENG-1a): `start` takes an optional `cwd` (absolute, existing dir → `context.cwd`; execs without their own `cwd:` run there, a relative one resolves against it) — a sub-agent's execs run in its worktree, not the server's tree. Execs get `WF_SESSION_ID` / `WF_PARENT_SESSION_ID` (empty at the root) / `WF_ROOT_SESSION_ID`, prompts `{{session_id}}` / `{{parent_session_id}}`. A root ending on a `fail` terminal is now `outcome: "failed"` (was recorded as completed); `status` / `sessions` / dashboard show it. `status` lists the session context (values cut to 200 chars, keys past 40 folded) so a parent can read a child's results. `abort` and a parent's abort cascade kill each background exec's process group (was: the bare pid, leaving `sh -c`'s children) and in-flight foreground execs. Unknown YAML keys fail the load, naming file, state, key and nearest known key (all bundled + user + anyparse workflows load clean). Each exec's history entry records `exit_code` + `duration_ms`; every session end (complete / fail / abort / cascade / hook abandon via `build/ledger-cli.js`) appends one line to `$STATE_DIR/ledger.jsonl`. `workflow-start.sh` deletes `context-epoch-<pid>` markers of dead pids
- scripts: `hxq-lint-file.sh` passes `--full` when hxq offers it — a newer hxq summarises a text report over `reportSummaryThreshold` (200) per rule, which would have dropped the per-finding lines a review reads on a large file or batch
- scripts: `check-skill-sync.sh` ignores Finder's `.DS_Store` — a metadata file macOS drops into a live skill dir no longer reads as drift and blocks the pre-commit hook; the suggested rsync excludes it too
- templates: file-review subtracts its manual checklist only by the lint rules hxq reports ON for the reviewed files (`hxq lint --list-rules <files>`: effective on/off per `apqlint.json` chain, default-off and `languageVersion` resolved) — decided once in `build_checklist` as `lint_rules_ran`, which `check_correctness` / `check_loops` / `check_style` now read instead of restating the registry criteria. `hxq-lint-file.sh` drops its hand-rolled nearest-only config walk; with an hxq too old for the scoped form it prints the bare registry marked as an upper bound and the review subtracts nothing. A rule its own options skip for a file (`needs-config` / `config-excluded` / `config-noop`) and a file with a `// CHECKSTYLE:OFF` region are never subtracted. lint-review output is unchanged
- engine: `docs` and `batch` join `GLOBAL_WORKFLOWS` — `batch` shipped in 0.1.10 without it, so the route prompt listed it as a PROJECT workflow and Step 1 could route it via `project`. A new test loads the real `templates/` and fails on the next bundled workflow left out
- tools: `list` / `start` / `status` / `transition` / `context_set` / `sessions` carry `readOnlyHint`, so plan mode and subagents stop prompting on every call (note: a user/project workflow's exec/fetch states now also run unprompted under plan mode). That also makes them concurrency-safe, so same-session mutations (incl. `modify` / `abort`) run through a PER-SESSION queue — a parallel `context_set` + `transition` no longer drops one change, and a sibling agent is never held behind another session's exec
- engine: a session whose parent was aborted while its exec/fetch state ran is no longer resurrected — the action chain re-reads the session after the command returns instead of writing its pre-exec copy back over the cascade
- templates: new text-only `docs` route (write → verify, no lint/tests/reviewer agent) — reachable from master's router, from `route_execution` after a plan, and from `subagent`; falls back to coding when a code file turns out to be involved
- templates: review lives with the SPAWNER — a sub-agent's coding/bug-fix review stops at lint + intent re-read, and `batch` `cross_check` is now the run's one fresh-eyes review of the combined diff (it previously assumed workers had reviewed themselves). **Project workflows that spawn code-writing `subagent` workers must now review the workers' diff themselves** (task-delegation says so at spawn time). Delta reviews get a real baseline: `write_fix` and code-review's hand-over record a `git stash create` snapshot in `review_base`, and batch records `base:` / `review_base:` per repo in the queue header. coding and bug-fix share the review sizing rules: mass gate for mechanical diffs (reviewed inline at `cross_file`), fix-delta-only re-entry, inline nit rounds
- templates: code-review — an approved change plan now executes (`plan_changes` → `code_changes`); it used to end the review without applying anything
- templates: file-review builds its checklist from the linter's rule registry and config printed by `hxq-lint-file.sh` (`LINT_LIST_RULES=1`)
- hooks: plan-resume detection and cleanup honour `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (`${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/plans`)
- skills: bundled snapshots resynced to live — architecture (lessons moved to `references/lessons.md`), debugging (patterns moved to `references/patterns.md`), lang-haxe (split into `references/`), hxq (+ `references/confusions.md`), browser-verify, claude-code-config, domain-gamedev, domain-pixi, domain-ui, domain-yolo, lang-as3, preferences, skill-manager, target-openfl-native, task-delegation, workflow-authoring
- skills: `math` removed — float parity moved to `lang-python`, modulo-wrap to `domain-gamedev`, the generic lesson to `debugging`; `domain-pony` bundled; bundled `coding-skill-selector` restored to the generic template; `check-skill-sync.sh` now flags an ignored skill that became identical to its live copy

## 0.1.11

- templates: testing — the fix-loop states (`fix_unit` / `fix_integration` / `fix_user_issue`) gain delegation discipline: a small, already-diagnosed fix is edited INLINE, not handed to a background `SendMessage`-resume and idle-waited on. A completed agent's `SendMessage`-resume is background-only — it has no `run_in_background: false`, so re-using it to gate a small follow-up becomes spawn-one-and-wait, the exact anti-pattern the sync-spawn rule forbids. This is the release's user-facing fix — it reaches every user through the live-loaded workflow template (unlike bundled skills, which only provision on first run)
- skills: task-delegation — the same `SendMessage`-resume-is-background-only rule in the Lifecycle section: decide a follow-up by SIZE, not by "an agent already has the context"; a small precisely-diagnosed fix goes inline, `SendMessage`-resume is reserved for a large/coupled follow-up where the background wait is worth it
- skills: debugging / domain-gamedev — the simulation-specific "A Rate Step in a Smooth-Input System = clamp boundary" entry moved out of the universal `debugging` skill into `domain-gamedev` (its `value − bound(t)` per-frame clamp probe, masked-clamp trap, resampled-path metric caveat); `debugging` keeps only the transferable disciplines (predicted-vs-measured mismatch = falsified hypothesis; verify a fix against the ORIGINAL failing metric)
- skills: bundled-snapshot resyncs (maintenance) — `debugging`, `hxq`, `claude-code-config` brought back in line with their live copies so `check-skill-sync` is green again

## 0.1.10

- templates: batch workflow — long-running batch orchestration over a file-backed task queue: rolling waves of background workers with one-by-one collection, cross_check gate; sync-vs-background decided per wave (a wave holding a single worker spawns sync — its report gates collect); per-worker git worktrees when logically-independent tasks collide only on infrastructure (registration files, build artifacts) with mechanical branch merge at collect
- templates: testing — `reassess` re-entry state after `fix_user_issue`: a user-reported fix re-runs unit tests when it touched logic (Red-Green on the user's exact scenario) before integration → user re-testing; dedicated state instead of a jump into `assess` so no `skip_all` escape hatch can end the loop without the user's verdict
- templates: explicit sync-spawn + bash fg/bg discipline in spawn states — gating agents spawn with `run_in_background: false`, bash runs foreground with an explicit timeout, background only for never-exiting processes
- skills: task-delegation — sync-vs-bg spawn rule, background-agent premature-report gotcha, artifacts-outrank-report symmetric rule, worktree-commit-is-interim merge rule
- skills: workflow-authoring — loop re-entry needs a dedicated state: audit a decision state's outbound transitions for early-exits before routing a retry into it (dual of the inbound gate-bypass audit)
- skills: enrichments + bundled resyncs — hxq (HXQ_QUIET=1 stale-binary probe, clusters dir-scope warning, `#if`-blindness of unused-private/refs, `--fix` fixpoint discipline gated on the project typecheck), lang-haxe (`Exception` is a full catch-all since 4.1), claude-code-config (hook payload capture recipe, Stop-hook `background_tasks` shape), browser-verify/domain-gamedev/domain-yolo/debugging live-side syncs
- skills: preferences JIT-placement rule (rules live in the process moment's carrier, not global CLAUDE.md); skill-manager machine-specifics gate; deployment notes generalized

- engine: every exec state gets `WF_PLUGIN_ROOT` in its env (plugin root, resolved from the executor module's own location) — templates reference bundled scripts portably (`sh "$WF_PLUGIN_ROOT/scripts/foo.sh"`), no per-machine script installation; a state's own `env:` can override it
- templates: master.yaml starts with a `scan_project` exec state — engine-run project language scan (bundled `scripts/scan-project.sh`) whose per-extension menu (+ tool probes, e.g. `hx … (hxq OK)`) renders in the route prompt via `context.project_langs`; skill selectors pick from the menu instead of re-scanning (empty menu → manual fallback)
- templates: file-review.yaml gains engine-run mechanics — `probe_hxq` exec (tool availability → `context.hxq_available`, consumed by a read_context top-up instruction) and `mechanical_pass` exec (bundled `scripts/hxq-lint-file.sh` on the reviewed file → `context.lint_report`); build_checklist carries lint findings as facts, drops linter-covered rules from the manual checklist (stale-report guard via `===` file headers), keeping only judgment rules
- templates: exec states pass agent/context-derived values via `env:` (not template interpolation into `sh -c`) — a `$(cmd)`-named directory or reviewed file cannot execute
- skills: coding-skill-selector reworked to menu-driven selection — pick languages the task touches from the engine scan, tool-bound skills (hxq) load only when the menu marks the tool available (or a probe succeeds), top-up rule for mid-task language widening
- skills: anti-drift for bundled snapshots — `scripts/check-skill-sync.sh` diffs `templates/skills/*` against the live `~/.claude/skills/*` (same-named pairs; `.skillsyncignore` lists intentional divergence like the generic coding-skill-selector), wired as a local git pre-commit hook so a stale snapshot can't be committed; every silently-drifted bundled skill re-synced from live; skill-manager checklist now requires copying every live-skill edit onto the bundled twin
- skills+templates: top-tier orchestrator unloading — a top-tier main session writes plans/briefs/judgment inline but delegates code >~30 changed lines to implementer agents (coupled set → ONE agent with the whole plan, never per-file fan-out; implementer tier = plan detail × mechanical net: sonnet for detailed-plan+linter/tests, opus otherwise); the plan itself is ALWAYS built inline (dialog condensate, planner=dispatcher); wired through task-delegation + coding/bug-fix/planning states (incl. fix_coupled delegation carve-out and single-file >30-line routing)
- skills: architecture skill cleaned and split — project-specific example stories abstracted (no leaked nouns), 4 debugging meta-patterns → new `debugging` skill (loaded via debugging/bug-fix gates + selector trigger), 3 UI-component entries → new `domain-ui` skill; body 26.3KB → 15.4KB always-loaded; bug-fix logic branch gained its missing skill gate
- engine: `digest_on_repeat` state flag — a flagged prompt state delivers its full text ONCE per server process, later deliveries send a 2-line digest (status() always returns the full text; template reload clears the delivered-set; same-session Revisit abbreviation takes precedence; start() no longer forces the full prompt so master `route` digests on repeat start() calls). Flagged: route, doc_sync, suggest_commit, reflection.evaluate, testing.assess — ~4-5k tokens saved per task after the first in a session
- templates: lint-review workflow — the trivial-change path (router-gated ≤2 files/≤50 lines) drops the 10-state file-review: the linter IS the review (collect paths → engine-run lint → act on findings + one intent re-read); wired as review_single in coding/bug-fix/code-review; registered in GLOBAL_WORKFLOWS
- skills: lang-haxe progressive-disclosure diet — body 49.8KB → 20.9KB by loudness criterion (silent-bug gotchas stay; loud compile traps → references/compile-traps.md, Strict null-safety cluster → references/null-safety.md, abstract-types cluster → references/abstracts.md; verbatim script-asserted lossless split, three imperative index blocks in the body)
- skills: hxq skill progressive-disclosure diet — body 81.5KB → 27.5KB (mutation-op contracts+caveats → references/ops.md, full lint semantics → references/lint.md, query recipes+nudges → references/queries.md; verbatim line moves, heading/line-reconstruction verified); body keeps gate rules, addressing, op index with imperative read-triggers and the three silent hazards (--write print-only, Edit-gate CRUD rule, --at --kind co-starting grab) — ~13k tokens saved per .hx-touching agent
- templates: cross_file states focus on seams BETWEEN batches + set-wide patterns (within-batch seams are each batch agent's job; set-wide checks apply even with one batch)
- skills+templates: global Agent-model policy — task-delegation gains a mandatory model table (pick by cost-of-error × mechanical safety net; sonnet for lint-covered/routine/locate/runner spawns, opus for macro-heavy/engine-critical/no-linter/judgement, opus wins on overlap, never haiku, never silent session-model inheritance); every spawn site across coding/bug-fix/testing/explore/web-research/planning names its default explicitly
- templates: review routing is size-based, not count-based — trivial (≤2 files AND ≤~50 lines to review; full scope measures the FILE, diff scope the diff) → inline self-review, everything else → batched agents even for a single batch (fresh eyes on the author's code + main-context offload); dispatchers must pass an explicit Agent `model` (sonnet for linter-covered/routine batches, opus for macro-heavy/no-linter; never haiku, never silent session-model inheritance)
- templates: review dispatchers batch files logically instead of one-agent-per-file — source+test together, change-coupling (hxq importers/callees for .hx), directory grouping, cap 5 files/~500 diff lines, oversized solo — amortizing each agent's fixed skill-load cost (~35-45k tokens) and putting coupled files in front of ONE reviewer; file-review consumes one file or a batch (space-separated `file_path`, per-file report sections + cross-file section), `hxq-lint-file.sh` lints a whole batch in one call (glob-safe: `set -f` held through the unquoted list expansion); applied to all three dispatchers (code-review, coding, bug-fix)
- templates: file-review checklist dedup vs the linter — build_checklist decides report usability ONCE (stale/failed-lint guards) and records `context.lint_subtraction`; check_style/check_loops guard their hardcoded lists on that flag, subtracting at rule-description granularity by `hxq lint --list-rules` (residues named: local-var annotations, beyond-threshold complexity judgments)

## 0.1.9

- engine: deliver hard-terminal state prompts — rendered and prepended (with `---` divider) to sub-workflow pop and root-completion output, so report contracts and push instructions actually fire
- engine: resolveSessionId throws on ambiguous ppid (multiple active sessions) listing candidates — parallel subagents must pass explicit session_id
- engine: add github-init to GLOBAL_WORKFLOWS; remove dead `needs_action` outcome from schema
- templates: full review pass over all 17 workflows — fail terminals everywhere (no dead on_fail edges), soft-terminal pattern for report/push states, skill gates (testing, debugging ?debug-bridge, web-research, reflection), session_id in all spawn preambles, background-by-default Agent tool guidance, subagent-context guards on clarify states, max_visits budget hints, secrets scan before commit/push, hunk-only revert guidance
- skills: sync all 20 bundled skills with live copies (architecture +10 sections, lang-haxe +20, domain-pixi +18 gotchas) and fix factual errors across build-cmake, ci-github-actions, aws-lambda, claude-code-config, domain-{reid,yolo,gamedev}, math, mcp-setup, workflow-authoring, web-reading, lang-{as3,python}, target-openfl-native; rewrite task-delegation for typed agents + background lifecycle
- skills: bundle 4 new — skill-manager, cleanup, ide-zed, debug-bridge-scaffold
- hooks: SessionStart branches on stdin `.source`; SessionEnd matcher no longer abandons sessions on /clear; safer temp-file writes; jq guard
- docs: README install channels, recommended CLAUDE.md snippet and permissions block (abort deliberately excluded)

## 0.1.8

- Cap retained terminal sessions via shared prunePolicy() — keep N most recently updated, delete older (Storage, InMemoryStorage, SessionEnd hook)
- engine: _pruneTerminal runs once after cascade (not per child), avoids quadratic scans
- prunePolicy: stable tiebreak by session_id, throws on negative keep, skips transient placeholders

## 0.1.7

- Expand bundled skills from 9 to 20 (lang-as3, target-openfl-native, domain-{yolo,pixi,reid,gamedev}, build-cmake, ci-github-actions, aws-lambda, mcp-setup, claude-code-config)
- Sync architecture (+7 sections) and lang-haxe (+650 lines on macros, null safety, hxcpp gotchas) with project-specific examples abstracted
- Update coding-skill-selector to map all bundled skills by extension/domain/tooling category
- Add Loader.validateSkillReferences() — checks required skills resolve in plugin/user/project skill dirs; logs missing on startup
- Mark debug-bridge as optional in bug-fix.yaml (not in bundle, prompt handles missing tooling)

## 0.1.6

- Add version consistency check in CI (package.json vs plugin.json)
- Automate release process: version bump triggers tag, GitHub Release, and npm publish
- Show available transitions in state output, simplify route prompt
- Add `include_workflows` state field and `DASHBOARD_HOST` config
- Add optional skills, exec improvements, `context_set`, and workflow fixes
- Restrict requirements state to user questions only

## 0.1.5

- Add action states (exec/fetch) for running shell commands and HTTP requests from workflows
- Add skill gate states with epoch-based freshness tracking
- Add session ownership guards and idempotent start for sub-agent safety
- Add force flag for cross-process session operations and auto-reap orphaned sessions
- Add test infrastructure with Vitest and dispatch chain tests
- Fix EPERM handling in PID-alive checks
- Remove vendored dagre.min.js, serve from node_modules
- Replace Russian text with English in dashboard

## 0.1.0

- Initial release
- FSM engine with stack-based sub-workflows
- YAML workflow definitions with Zod validation
- Session persistence with atomic writes and file locking
- Web dashboard with session visualization
- MCP server with stdio transport
- Claude Code plugin packaging
